## **Reading Notes**

**This is an out-of-character analytical document.** It uses mechanics, GM-side lore and player knowledge freely. Where a judgement depends on information no PC possesses, it is marked **⟨OOC⟩**. Where a judgement depends on information only *some* characters hold, the holder is named.

**How to read an entry.** Each of the 505 placements has one of two shapes. A returning member's entry looks like this:

1\. **Name** \`score\`

- **All-time.** The standing, campaign-wide case for the score.
- **Session 16: 7.0 → 7.5 (+0.5).** The shift since the last edition, and why.
  * **All-time** is the character's standing file on that axis across Sessions 1–15: the sheet, the traits and backstory, the repeated behaviour, and the incidents that set the level. **Session 15's detail has been folded into it**, and earlier sessions compressed, so that the file reads as one case rather than a stack of revisions.
  * **Session 16** states the shift as numbers, measured from the Session 15 edition's score: `S15 → S16 (change)`, or *holds at* when the score did not move. It then gives the Session 16 evidence and the reasoning for the size and direction of the move, or for the hold. Position changes among the returning four are measured like-for-like, with Edge removed (§10.0).
* **On the §2 risk axes** a more negative number means more exposure, so the label adds *exposure up* or *exposure down*.
* **Edge's entries carry a single item, *Debut, Session 16*,** because he has no file before this revision. It gives the case for the debut score from three sources only: his sheet, his backstory and one fight. Read every debut score as provisional.

**Ranking convention:** Position 1 \= most of the named quality. Where a low score is desirable (burnout risk, enemy targeting, legal exposure), position 1 \= highest risk, and the axis header says so explicitly. **This revision ranks five characters, so positions run 1–5.** Where two characters share a score, the printed order is the tie-break and the axis carries a note: on an otherwise unbroken tie the newcomer is listed second, and on the Logic-driven non-combat axes, ties between Intrepid and Edge are broken on Logic.

**Confidence markers:**

* Orderings drawn from repeated logged behaviour are stated plainly.
* *Closest call* explains the split where the logs support two defensible orderings.
* *Volatile* marks a ranking that would move on a single die roll, GM ruling or piece of unconfirmed lore.
* **⟨Flag⟩** marks a score that depends on something the document cannot see, chiefly Oracle's undeclared advances and Edge's mentor tag.
* **⟨Ruling⟩** cites a house rule or roster ruling.
* **⟨Mechanical flag⟩** marks a sheet-level caveat.

**Damage notation.** Damage multipliers are written *multiplier \+ add* per the roster convention: `7+7` is a ×7 multiplier with \+7 applied after the multiplication, not a flat bonus of 14\. Expected values use a Marvel-die mean of 4.33 (a roll of 1 counts as 6\) and exclude Fantastic doubling, edges, assists and Focus investment. ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ Edge's roster line is used as printed (×7+7), although it does not appear to include his Iconic weapon's \+1 ranged multiplier (§4.10).

**The roster changed again.** **Edge joins the team in Session 16**, filling the vacancy Exemplar II left.

* **Who he is.** Murphy King, fifteen, played by Aiden Snowveil. Monstrous origin (the Monster trait), Rank 3.5, all advances taken. His roster title is *The Reborn Calamity* and his role *Precision Marksman & Spatial Infiltrator*. He lives in bedroom 3, which was Exemplar II's.
* **How he arrived.** The roster records that he became aware of the team via Oracle, and was approached and recruited by Nightwing, operating as Batman. In play, Nightwing briefed him on the five villains before the fight, and the team did not know.
* **What he brings, in one line.** The best ranged attack on the team, a second Suppressive Fire, Heightened Senses 2 (the senses that left with Exemplar II), a healing factor and a dodge — and a backstory about an ancient monster that *"cut down armies,"* which this document treats as ⟨Volatile⟩.
* **Comparisons.** Score-based aggregates are per-character means, so his arrival changes no returning member's figure. Position-based aggregates are reported on the five-person board and, for the returning four, like-for-like against Session 15 (§10.0).
* **The archive.** Exemplar II stays frozen in **§11**, which now also measures the replacement against the vacancy.

**Edge is the story of this revision.** **He enters third on the composite (+0.57), ahead of Lock-Step and Intrepid**, on the flattest profile on the board, and second on both combat aggregates. In his first fight he named the first target — *"can turn 5 vs 5 into 6 vs 4"* — and the plan was built around it. He put 101 into one villain in one action, knocked out the member of the brood who had stolen a Titan's power, shot the device the brood came for out of a phaser's hand and destroyed it, and held off Blitzkrieg alone so the extraction could start. He is **first on Ranged Combat** and the only member with Heightened Senses. Against it: Logic −1, sixteen last places (seven of them tie-breaks and four of them fame axes, where last is good), no record outside one afternoon, and a legend nobody has examined.

**The fight is the story of the session.** Seven rounds and a hand-waved ending against five teenage villains carrying the Breeder's brood, and **every member of the team went to zero Health or below**. The brood explained, mid-fight, that it had ganged up on Quasar-Force first because of his fame, his known fragility and his preference for grappling. Then its orders changed to *"collecting you since you proved yourselves worthy."* The team destroyed the device the brood came for and went home one teleport at a time; three of the five were left unconscious in a Marrow Syndicate warehouse, one of them in Lock-Step's handcuffs, and two escaped.

**The standings barely moved, and they moved toward Oracle.** Fifty-nine axes changed among the returning four, a third of Session 15's 183\. **Oracle's composite lead widened from 0.36 to 0.39**, the first time it has widened since 0.77 → 0.81, because the session ran on healing, debuffs and a plan, and none of it needed an engineer. On the 86 favourable axes Oracle is back in front by three hundredths, and 93% of his lead is still the risk section. The fame-inclusive flip held for a second revision: Quasar-Force leads that treatment by 0.21.

* **Lock-Step** moved on 24 axes, sixteen for the better. He set the chair down in front of the newcomer (*"Oracle is our plan guy"*), asked a teammate's consent before teleporting him for the first time, extracted all four teammates on the last of his Focus, and gave the first explanation of himself on record (*"Once I get that back, I think I'll be a bright-eyed kid just like you… But until then, this is me"*). He also cuffed one of the five teenagers he had publicly mocked and left her in a mob warehouse.
* **Quasar-Force** moved on 9\. The circlet passed its first hostile test, and he was knocked down twice anyway, the second time to −30.
* **Intrepid** moved on 13\. He became the first Titan to have a power stolen, took 290 damage, and refused an extraction under fire.
* **Oracle** moved on 13\. The room named him its planner, and every recovery in the fight went through him — including his own, from −2: *"Drop me again, it was worth it."*

**No sheet changed among the returning four.** Every Session 16 movement is play, apart from Edge's arrival. **Oracle's two Rank 3.5 advances are undeclared for a third session.** ⟨Flag⟩

**Principal evidence gaps:**

* **Edge's mentor tag.** His roster tags include *Mentor (PC: Oracle)*, while his backstory says *"Oracle found him online… she called the Birds,"* which reads as Barbara Gordon. Nothing in Session 16 shows the PC Oracle had met him. ⟨Flag⟩ Not scored as a mentorship.
* **Edge's weapon.** The Cutting Edge grants \+1 ranged damage multiplier, and the roster's ×7+7 line does not appear to include it. ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ Scored on the printed line.
* **Oracle's Rank 3.5 advances are undeclared**, for a third session. §4.2, §4.5, §6.4 and §7.3 each carry a ⟨Flag⟩: one likely purchase would move all four.
* **The hand-waved ending.** The last minutes of the fight are summarised, not played: Dragonborne's flight, Edge's rear guard, the extraction, and the GM's reasons for Blitzkrieg's withdrawal (Intrepid's weight and the Force whip). They are scored as recorded and marked wherever they decide an entry.
* **The rewound clock.** To introduce Edge, Session 16 winds the clock back ten to fifteen minutes, from the team entering the warehouse to the team gathering outside it. The rewound version is treated as canon.
* **The device.** Keyed to Lock-Step's time manipulation, sought by the brood, and destroyed before anyone examined it. What it did and who wanted it are unknown. ⟨Volatile⟩
* **Unresolved threads.** The Destined errand, Lobo, the 24-hour feral clock, the fate of the three teenagers left in the warehouse, and the ring. None appears in Session 16, and nothing about them is scored.
* **Nightwing and Batman are off screen for a third session.** Nightwing acted offscreen, recruiting and briefing Edge; §9.8 and §9.9 are otherwise scored on implication.
* **Wonder Woman as the team's de facto mentor** is the user's reading. The GM has not declared it, so this document treats it as practice rather than appointment. The team briefed her after the fight.

**Scoring.** Every entry carries a cardinal score alongside its position, because ordinal rank alone cannot tell a rout from a photo finish. **Three native scales are used, chosen by what each axis actually measures**; the full rationale is in 10.0:

* **Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`** — Low means *absent*, never *harmful*.
  * Capability and exposure:
    * §3.6–3.9 – Team Internals, Command Chair & Pecking Order
    * §4 – Power / Ability – Combat
    * §5 – Power / Ability – Non-combat
    * §6 – Capability Robustness & Conditionality
    * §7 – Power / Ability – Overall
    * §8 – Fame (Exposure)
  * Anchors:
    * 0 none, completely absent capability
    * 5.0 competent baseline for a Rank 3 street-level hero
    * 8.5 elite for the tier
    * 10 best plausible at Rank 3\.
* **Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`** — These have a real negative pole: a person can be an active liability, not merely lacking.
  * Psychology, discipline, standing and opinion:
    * §1 – Psychology, Morality, and Discipline
    * §3.1–3.5 – Team Internals: internal reputation, influence, trust, cohesion, and low operational support burden
    * §9 – Reputation / Opinion
  * Anchors:
    * \+10 maximal asset
    * 0 genuinely neutral
    * −10 maximal liability.
* **Risk `−10.0 to 0.0`** — Always signed negative, because exposure is only ever a cost.
  * Threat exposure:
    * §2 – Enemy Risk
  * Anchors:
    * 0 \= none meaningful
    * −10 \= maximal, operations already underway.

505 scores total (101 scales times 5 PCs).

**Score Quick Recognition Pattern:**

* §2 – Enemy Risk is always negative, **`−10.0 to 0.0`** Risk.
* Everything else – If the score has a sign, it is **`−10.0 to +10.0`** Valence. Otherwise it is **`0.0 to 10.0`** Magnitude.

##

## **1\. Psychology, Morality, and Discipline**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

### **1.1 Current Psychological Stability**

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** The most settled person in the building: a purposeful role, accepted structure and no running quarrel with any authority. Session 14 was the test. He was held in an inescapable grab, drained from 180 Focus to 3, watched a teammate get one-shot and could not heal him, and then called Wonder Woman himself and reported the failure plainly. The ceiling is set by a hazard that never shows: Eidetic Memory means he does not get to forget the bisection, the jars or the body he failed to revive. Session 15 recorded the first weariness in his voice — *"It's no different than Nightwing"* and *"a big old mess like we usually do"* — logged as a sign, not scored as a crack.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.5.** The weariness continued as humour (*"Don't worry, the bar is low, it's alright"*) and as candour (*"No plan. Not on this one"*). The test was physical. A 72-point Banging Heads put him on the floor at −2, his healing factor got him up, and he ran back into the warehouse at a sliver of Health: *"Drop me again, it was worth it."* He then kept a teammate conscious for four rounds and moved Let's Go at the moment the extraction needed it. **Stability is what he showed; weariness is what he said.** Neither moves him.
2. **Edge** `+3.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Composed from his first line (*"I don't want to break your hope"*) to his last stand. A glitch bomb put him at exactly 0, his healing factor brought him back, and he held off Blitzkrieg alone for a round. Against it, his sheet and backstory carry the heaviest identity load on the roster: constant headaches since the powers arrived, eyes that glow, and a legend that he is a monster reborn that once *"cut down armies."* The roster's narrative profile frames him as *"still figuring out whether he gets to be Murphy King or has to be the monster."* **Second on one session of calm, carrying the largest unexamined question on the team.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** His composure has been a fixture of the campaign, and Session 14 found that part of it is not his. The mother box holds a *"Peter Pan think happy thoughts"* baseline in its host and damps the panic-fear and anger that would impair his logic. **The famous calm is partly a second party's thermostat**, and a pencil sketch of Azimuth was enough to override it for five minutes on an infirmary bed. Two things hold the score up: he worked out mid-attack that the panic was not his, and he recovers absurdly fast. Session 15 told him he and the box may no longer be distinct, that he is *"no longer merely human,"* and that Azimuth wants him alive. He named her Stella and went back to his build list. *"I might end up better off dead"* is the darkest line on his file, and he said it without visible distress.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+2.0.** Before the fight he was the room's optimist: *"I like being hopeful. I want to help people get their happy thoughts back."* Inside it, he was knocked to −5 in the Marvel round, spent Karma to get back to 13, moved to cover and kept working, then went to −30 — the deepest knockdown on his file. No panic and no episode from the box are recorded, and the log ends before any aftermath. Composure under fire is scored in 1.5 and 1.9. **Nothing in Session 16 tested the thermostat.**
4. **Intrepid** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Iron Will and Fearless are on the sheet, and for most of the campaign his interior surfaced only in asides — the Session 10 monologue and its self-medication ladder. Session 14 put it into words as a fear of being discarded, with four reversals of his own decision in one conversation. Session 15 was the quietest session in his file, and it held the first forward-looking line he has said: *"I don't even think I'm as strong as Superman. Not yet anyway."* Against it, *"I'm not really a thinker."*
   * **Session 16: \+0.5 → \+1.0 (+0.5).** The most punishing fight on his file: his Sturdy stolen, 72 and 60 from Dragonborne, down twice. Nothing in the log records fear of losing control or of being replaced. When Lock-Step asked whether he would stay out if teleported clear, **he refused to leave.** In Session 14 the same teammate flipped him four times in one conversation; in Session 16 he offered him an exit under fire and got a no. The rise is small because it rests on behaviour, not on anything he said.
5. **Lock-Step** `−4.5`
   * **All-time.** Mechanically the most armoured mind in the party and psychologically the most defended by avoidance. He is an orphan who says he is *glad* to have no parents, fills his time with dates and parties, distrusts adults by default, and has Berserk on his sheet. Session 14 logged the first slip in conversation, a rant at Wonder Woman with his rationality degrading. Session 15 put the most load on him of any session: he ran the team (*"it's tough"*), asked a villain to kill for him *"slurred in moral conflict,"* and had the happiest moment on his record when the ring went on. He stayed lucid, with no Berserk.
   * **Session 16: holds at −4.5.** The session opened with the longest anti-adult statement on his file — *"Apparently all the adults are trash,"* *"Adults just suck,"* and Iron-Side *"lived in the security room… I got stonewalled"* — delivered to a teammate he had met a minute earlier. Then, answering Quasar-Force, came the first explanation he has ever given of himself: *"I just hope that one day, you get my happy thoughts back. Adults being adults and actually being helpful. Once I get that back, I think I'll be a bright-eyed kid just like you… But until then, this is me."* That is insight, and it is scored in 1.2. It was followed at once by *"Maybe I just need another party,"* which is the old coping style. In the fight he was lucid and Berserk-free. **The structure is unchanged; for the first time he can describe it.**

### **1.2 Psychological Resilience vs Future Trauma (Trauma Processing & Recovery Rate)**

1. **Quasar-Force** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** The best demonstrated recovery curve on the team. Stabbed in Session 14 from 60 Health to −12, he was woken at 5 and pacing the air within the hour. The roster explains the curve, and the explanation is not entirely reassuring: the box is deliberately building his resilience to trauma and panic. **The resilience is real, it is engineered, and the engineer has PTSD.** Session 15 put that half under expert care. Taleria made the box three promises and named Azimuth's error (*"He treated her as machinery"*), and when Quasar-Force raised the box's trauma under an alien telepath's read, the box did not panic.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.5.** Two knockdowns in one fight. Between them the curve did what it does: kept upright by Let's Go at −5, he spent Karma to reach 13 Health, went to cover and was back to work in time to crush Stote unconscious. The second knockdown, to −30, ends his part of the log, so the second recovery is untested.
2. **Oracle** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** A steady base, a purposeful role and Determination, and caretaking is protective. The fracture line this axis has named for five revisions is specific: *if Endo is seriously hurt on Oracle's watch, Oracle is the one who breaks.* Session 14 came as close as it could without arriving, with Oracle grabbed, drained and unable to heal Endo at −12. **He has not talked about it.** Session 15 added a hardware fragility (the module faltered under temporal transport) and one line drifting toward fatalism.
   * **Session 16: \+6.0 → \+6.5 (+0.5).** **The fracture line arrived, and this time he had the tools.** Endo went to −5 in the first exchange and to −30 in the third. Oracle kept him in the fight with Let's Go for four rounds, put 15 Health into him mid-fight knowing it would not reach zero, and healed him to consciousness afterwards. In between, Oracle was knocked out himself and came back with *"Drop me again, it was worth it."* The scenario this axis predicted would break him is the one in which he did his best work. The other half point waits on the conversation about Session 14 that has still not happened.
3. **Edge** `+3.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** His backstory is a trauma with a clean before and after: an assault near him, the attacker's weapon sliced to pieces, then the headaches, the glowing eyes and the white hair. **His response was the healthiest one available — he went looking for help**, and when it came he trained *"all-out"* with Black Canary. In play he took 102 damage, went to exactly 0, got up on his own healing factor and held a doorway alone. What is not yet scored is the question his narrative profile says he is still deciding — Murphy King or the monster. That question is where future trauma would land.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** Iron Will, Fearless, Enduring Constitution, and a real willingness to take hits so others don't. The first data on how he metabolises defeat came in Session 14, and he did it by lowering expectations: nothing could have gone differently, and complaining gets you replaced. The trauma he is least equipped for is still the one most likely to arrive — he has come within one die roll of killing ordinary humans three times — and the only coping he has ever described is the Session 10 ladder from aspirin to buying off the street.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.5.** The heaviest physical beating on his file includes the first power ever stolen from a Titan, and the log records no reaction from him in either direction. Nothing was processed because nothing was said.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.5`
   * **All-time.** Lowest, and the mechanics agree with the fiction: Berserk is a hardwired route from trauma to violence, avoidance is his default, and every pressure on him delivers the same message, *you are dangerous and you don't belong.* After the Session 14 defeat he converted the loss into grievances, a joke and a request for souvenirs within minutes. Session 15 made him the author of something that has not happened yet — the Destined errand, *"cinder blocks in the ocean"* — and gave him the first admitted cost (*"it would hurt my soul"*) and the first adult approval he welcomed.
   * **Session 16: −6.0 → −5.5 (+0.5).** **For the first time he named the wound under the deflection, and he named it accurately:** adults who were meant to be *"actually being helpful"* and weren't, and the cynicism as the result — *"until then, this is me."* Naming is not processing, and his next sentence was *"Maybe I just need another party."* But for eleven revisions this axis has said that none of his responses touched the cause, and this one does. He was one-shot from 60 to −24 later the same afternoon; the log does not record how he took it.

### **1.3 Operational Longevity / Low Burnout Risk**

*(Position 1 \= most sustainable.)*

1. **Oracle** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** He sleeps, eats, cooks, patrols, reports and accepts the structure he is given, which removes the largest single source of teenage-hero attrition. **His one real risk is load, not morale.** He is the medic, the cook, the comms relay, the overwatch and Quasar-Force's unappointed guardian. Session 14 drained him to 3 Focus and cost twelve hours of sleep to reach 75\. Session 15 rested him by circumstance rather than design, and recorded the first morale-shaped doubt in his file.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.5.** The load got heavier and he carried it. The room named him *"our plan guy,"* and he was the only healer in a fight that put all five Titans on the floor — still healing when it ended. The plan question exposed a seam: asked for one, he had none (*"No plan. Not on this one"*). **The team's default answer to "what do we do" is one person, and he was tired before the fight started.**
2. **Quasar-Force** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Intrinsically motivated to an unusual degree, with no physical attrition curve: pristine cellular maintenance, frozen ageing, and a full 60 Health back in ten hours of sleep. **The failure mode is self-maintenance, not motivation** — with structure imposed from outside he is fine. Session 15's six days of building were restorative rather than draining. Back home the load rose: make-up classes, back-end analysis, and drones built for a teammate's streaming.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.0.** He went into the fight optimistic and came out of it at −30, which costs time rather than motivation. Nothing in Session 16 touched the structure question.
3. **Intrepid** `+2.5`
   * **All-time.** He loves the tank role, is good at it and heals between fights, so his physical attrition is low. The risk is psychological accumulation behind the physical durability, which surfaced out loud in Session 14\. Session 15 brought the first motivation from inside him (*"Not yet anyway"*) and 210 Health, which lets the accumulation stay hidden longer.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+2.5.** He took 290 damage by the log's count, and refused to be taken out of the fight. That is the tank's motivation working, and it is also exactly the accumulation this axis exists to watch.
4. **Edge** `+2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** The case for him rests on structure, and the case against on the body. **For:** an active mentor who volunteered, deliberate training, and a combat loop that costs almost nothing (§4.3). **Against:** *"constant headaches"* since the powers arrived — a chronic symptom tied to the power itself — and grades that *"were already bad"* before training took the time. One session cannot show an attrition curve. The headaches are the item to watch.
5. **Lock-Step** `−2.0`
   * **All-time.** The only member whose attrition risk comes from his relationship with adults: a file of structural grievances, school to the legal minimum, and *"keep it street level."* What changed across Sessions 13–15 is that the grievances kept being answered, with pay, Wonder Woman's commitment and, in Session 15, a ring and a succession that anchor his motivation inside hero work. The grievances also kept coming back.
   * **Session 16: holds at −2.0.** Both halves were at full volume. The grievance file got its longest entry — *"all the adults are trash,"* the stonewalling, *"there should be more supervision"* — and he described his own role as *"the if it all goes to hell guy."* Then it went to hell, and he did exactly that job, going back for the last teammate on *"just about the last of his focus."* **The motivation that matters held, and the attrition driver is louder than it has ever been.**

### **1.4 Personal Discipline Outside of Combat**

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** Structured mornings, breakfast for the house, patrols on schedule and reports filed. What sets him apart is that he holds a line under social pressure. The only deduction in sixteen sessions is the Session 12 all-nighter, and he managed even that through the proper channel. Session 14 added a correctly managed recovery under medical orders.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.5.** Session 16 had no downtime to test it.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** Rule-following but not self-regulating, and the gap between the halves defines his score. On duty he is excellent; off duty he needs structure from outside. Session 14 was four tasks at once and a sedative. Session 15 was the reverse: he stated his order of work three times and delivered three builds, in that order, inside six days. The standing deduction remains because the Watchtower supplied the structure.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+4.5.** No downtime. His pre-fight work was deduction and a proposed entrance plan, both scored elsewhere.
3. **Intrepid** `+4.0`
   * **All-time.** First up every morning, oatmeal, out on patrol while still wounded, and he defers to team decisions without sulking. Against him: no initiative, no questions when invited to ask them, and a private drift toward self-medication.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+4.0.** *"Nothing, I guess."*
4. **Edge** `+2.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** The one off-duty fact in his file runs both ways: he trained *"all-out"* with Black Canary, and his grades *"were already bad"* before that. On arrival he asked for the plan, then for the situation, which is the right order. Neutral-positive and provisional.
5. **Lock-Step** `−7.5`
   * **All-time.** He skips school, ignores summons, once posted a sticky note saying to contact him only if someone was dying, sedated a teammate twice, concealed an artifact from his mentor, and treats breaking and entering as unremarkable. In Session 14 his conduct is the log's likely reason the team's Watchtower access was limited to the guest quarters. In Session 15 he slept through Iron-Side's make-up classes, admitted not listening in briefings, and streamed a Justice League station without asking anyone. **Nearly all of his self-directed effort goes into his own audience.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −7.5.** No downtime. His one off-duty contribution was a briefing for the newcomer, and it was wrong on both facts it asserted. *"They just told him to fix it"* drew Quasar-Force's correction, *"You're mainly the one that told me to fix it."* And *"they didn't even fill him in"* was wrong too, though he could not have known it: Nightwing had briefed Edge on every villain in the warehouse.

### **1.5 Personal Discipline in Combat**

*Session 16 is the first fight since Session 14, and the hardest the team has taken: all five members went to 0 Health or below.*

1. **Oracle** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** He opens nearly every engagement by demanding surrender, holds actions rather than burning them, stays in position, reassesses targets on precognitive input, and heals rather than chasing kills. Session 14 was output under total neutralisation: grabbed, re-grabbed and drained, he fired whenever he had a target and spent turns on the escape only when the fog left him nothing to shoot. In Session 15 he positioned himself as the scout's healer before contact.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.0.** The opener was the plan executed exactly: Suppressive Fire on the agreed target, upgraded to a 6-1-6 by Charmed Life, for massive Focus damage and a stun. He kept Let's Go on the teammate who needed it for four rounds, then moved it to the one who could get everyone out. **Against:** knocked out in round two, he came back on his healing factor and ran straight back in at a sliver of Health — the team's only healer re-entering a room where a flying brick had just floored him. It produced the stun he wanted, and it is the riskiest thing on his file.
2. **Edge** `+7.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A complete first fight. **He named the target before contact and the plan was built around it.** He shot and then moved — after the 74 \+ 27 on Stote he *"retreats away"* — and spent two actions assisting Intrepid at the moments that mattered, one of which turned a miss into a Marvel. He used Slow-Motion Dodge to cancel a charging Marvel, picked the device rather than a body when the device was the objective, and finished holding a door. One session, which is why he sits below Oracle.
3. **Quasar-Force** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** The best target-discriminator on the team, and his objective play from cover is textbook. **The deduction is the single most consequential discipline decision of the campaign:** in Session 14 he left concealment to take an artifact from a goddess with an unlocatable assassin in the room, and one action took him from full Health to −12. The objective reasoning was right; the risk arithmetic was not.
   * **Session 16: \+6.0 → \+6.5 (+0.5).** **The Session 14 lesson, applied.** Hit for 38 and 27 in the Marvel round before he could act, he was kept on his feet by Let's Go, healed on Karma and went straight to the far corner, behind a shipping container, and fought from there. From cover he grabbed Argent through her re-raised force field, pinned Dragonborne and crushed Stote unconscious. Blitzkrieg found him anyway — *"What's ya doing, Hiding?"* — because phasers do not respect containers. Hiding was the right answer, and it was not a sufficient one.
4. **Intrepid** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** Willing discipline and indiscriminate tools. He takes positioning suggestions without ego and tells teammates when to take an opening. Against him: Crushing Grip has done nothing in two sessions, he once considered punching through a floor to chase a phaser, and Ground-Shaking Stomp does not distinguish a goddess from a security guard.
   * **Session 16: \+4.5 → \+5.0 (+0.5).** His cleanest fight in three sessions. He used Banging Heads on the two right targets and Smash only with an assist behind it, and the one stomp he threw reached only the enemy he meant. With his Sturdy stolen, he *"contemplates using ram without any of his damage reduction"* — and didn't. Staying when offered an exit is scored in 1.12.
5. **Lock-Step** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** The record against him is severe: two Berserk episodes, teammates teleported out of combat without their consent, a blind teleport alone into a room of ten monsters, a proposed arson and a captive beaten conscious. Against it, clean combats in Sessions 11–14, with Session 14 the best of them: in, strike, out, and targeting direction taken from four teammates.
   * **Session 16: −1.0 → 0.0 (+1.0).** Five clean combats now, and the first change to the item that has defined this axis for him. **Before moving anyone, he asked:** *"Who is comfortable where they are at and who wants to move a little bit."* He asked Intrepid directly whether he would stay out if teleported, accepted the refusal, and moved Oracle out of the building only once Oracle was unconscious. He offered handcuffs twice, and both refusals came from someone else's control. The one exception is that he moved Oracle *into* Argent's sphere with Teleport Together, and the log does not record whether Oracle agreed. He went down to a single Marvel-20 outside the building, with no Berserk at any point.

### **1.6 Operational Security Discipline**

1. **Oracle** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Textbook instincts without formal training. He shut down internal discussion in front of the Destined, keeps his reporting channel clean, and treats a teammate's misconduct as something a supervisor needs to know. In Session 14 he was the only person in the room who did not say a Justice Leaguer's civilian name aloud. In Session 15 his suggestion of a private call became a public broadcast because someone else made it one.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** Nothing to secure in a warehouse fight.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** The team's most active security practitioner and one of its two largest leaks. He built the code rotation that Fade-Out called *"a pain in the neck,"* and Fade-Out explained in the same sentence why it could not work against the system's designer. **The leak is a habit: he answers before deciding whether the asker should have the answer.** Session 15 brought the largest security improvement in the team's history — a signal mask he wears and a teleport shield he held against a teammate's suggestion to drop it — alongside the old habit (the Kwa story told *"to anyone else who would listen"*).
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.5.** Nothing leaked, and the circlet stayed on.
3. **Intrepid** `+4.0`
   * **All-time.** Secure by having nothing to spill and a Secret Identity to protect. In Session 15 he was streamed as the future Superman in his hero identity only.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+4.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `+2.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A public identity, though *"not very well known,"* and no lapses in his first session. There are no practices on file either. Neutral-positive until something tests it.
5. **Lock-Step** `−4.5`
   * **All-time.** Until Session 15 he was personally the hardest member to trace and institutionally the most corrosive: a concealed artifact, *"Nightwing can't be trusted,"* team decisions routed around, and *"Diana"* in a tower the Destined enter at will. **Session 15 was the largest single-axis fall in the document (−5.0).** He livestreamed the Watchtower, ran a week of lab coverage, and broadcast a surveillance operation with five juveniles' faces. He also confirmed his real name by text to an unknown sender, invited a telepath into his memories and proposed dropping the tower's new shield for a villain team.
   * **Session 16: holds at −4.5.** No broadcasting is recorded. His welcome to the newcomer included the tower's breach history and Iron-Side's place in it, which is team business told to a teammate; its accuracy is scored in 1.4.

### **1.7 Civilian Life Stability & Maintenance Load**

*How stable the non-hero life is, and how much overhead it imposes. High \= stable and low-maintenance.*

1. **Intrepid** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Secret Identity, school, and nothing else on the board. The absence of complications is the entire score, and he has held first since Exemplar II left.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.0.** Nothing touched his civilian life.
2. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** Stable inside the tower and structurally unable to live a normal civilian life outside it. **Extreme Appearance means he cannot walk into a shop unremarked**, and the roster records his civilian identity as *lapsed*.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+1.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** He has a public identity with low recognition, a school record that was poor before the powers arrived, and a visibly changed body — white hair, eyes that glow and constant headaches. He now lives at the tower, in Exemplar II's old room. Nothing on file describes his family. **Positive because there is still a civilian life to protect, and low because almost nothing about it has been described.**
4. **Quasar-Force** `−1.0`
   * **All-time.** Publicly identified, with parents at a known address in Slate Hill, a family in court-adjacent therapy, a personal maintenance load nobody else carries, and ⟨OOC⟩ ageing that has stopped. Session 14 added a New God; Session 15's circlet answers the detection half of that.
   * **Session 16: holds at −1.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−3.5`
   * **All-time.** No parents, no fixed address and a truancy record he keeps adding to, filled with dates and parties rather than anything load-bearing. **The instability is the thing he says he is glad about.** The weekly stipend is the one structure he asked for. Session 15 added a surname, a named best friend and a public channel, all anchored to fame.
   * **Session 16: holds at −3.5.** Nothing changed.

### **1.8 Adult / Guardian / Peer Support & Safety Net**

1. **Quasar-Force** `+9.5`
   * **All-time.** By a wide margin the best-supported person on the team. He has two engaged parents, a family therapy arc, a father at STAR Labs, Nightwing watching since before he had a codename, Guy Gardner, and Oracle as an unappointed guardian. Session 14 added Iron-Side's surgery, Wonder Woman's personal sanctuary offer and Doctor Taleria. Session 15 added a week of Taleria at his bench, a founder who came in person when called, and a Green Lantern who picked up mid-fight. **He is the only member for whom the League's support is personal rather than institutional.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+9.5.** Already at the top of the scale, and nothing new in a session spent in a warehouse.
2. **Edge** `+6.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** He arrived with a support network nobody else on the team brought. **Black Canary volunteered to train him** because his power is dangerous. **Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited him personally and briefed him on the villains before the fight** — the only Titan to get a briefing from him in three sessions. The backstory adds an "Oracle" who found him online and called in the Birds of Prey. ⟨Flag⟩ The roster tags him *Mentor (PC: Oracle)*, but the backstory says *"she called the Birds,"* which reads as Barbara Gordon. Nothing in Session 16 shows the team's Oracle knowing him beforehand. His family is not described.
3. **Oracle** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing is a mentor he actually uses and is the one most often absent; STAR Labs is an institutional origin. League hospitality and Taleria's interest raised the score in Session 14\. In Session 15 he said it first: *"It's no different than Nightwing."* **He is used to being the one who looks after people.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+5.0.** Nightwing was absent from the table for a third session and present behind it — he recruited and briefed the newcomer. Oracle's support in the fight was his team.
4. **Intrepid** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing, and a Secret Identity that implies an intact civilian family, with no detail on either. The one time he asked for something, in Session 14, a teammate called him a coward until he changed his mind.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−3.5`
   * **All-time.** An orphan who volunteers that he is glad he has no parents. He distrusts Iron-Side and Nightwing and keeps his confidence for Zatanna, who is not present. **Since Session 13, adults he distrusts have kept acting in his interest anyway.** Session 15 brought two firsts: he asked Iron-Side what to do and did it, and a cosmic order tested him, found him worthy and gave him a ring.
   * **Session 16: −4.0 → −3.5 (+0.5).** The loudest grievance on file, and beneath it the clearest statement of need he has made: *"Adults being adults and actually being helpful."* **Then, in the field, he used one.** *"Nightwing did say we can bring them back, so knock them out"* is the first time he has cited an adult's instruction to settle a question in a fight. Against it, the same breath accused Nightwing of failing to brief the newcomer, which was wrong.

### **1.9 Emotional Regulation / Impulse Control**

1. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** He has held lines twice against the most socially dominant person on the team without escalating, and rations healing by arithmetic while people bleed. Session 14 was regulation under helplessness. In Session 15 he was talked over on a public stream without rising to it.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.5.** Knocked out, up again and back in, and his only line about it was a joke at his own expense.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Regulated to a degree that would be impressive in an adult, and part of the reason is that the box damps his panic-fear and anger. **That is regulation and outsourcing at once, and this axis scores the outcome.** His deficit is attentional rather than emotional. His most heated moment in Session 15 was intellectual — pressing a Grey Lantern on principle until a teammate stopped him.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** He met Lock-Step's sarcasm with optimism and two knockdowns with calm.
3. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Calm, dry and polite through a briefing delivered mostly as complaint (*"Ok, that told me nothing"*), and no recorded flinch across 102 damage. The one deliberate display was a weaponised one: back up from zero, he *"uses his monstrousness to intimidate with edge."* **That is control of the frightening part of himself, used once and on purpose.** The risk this axis watches is the same part used once and not on purpose, and his whole profile is written around keeping it in.
4. **Intrepid** `+0.5`
   * **All-time.** He voices his fear of losing control during fights, and the Session 10 ladder is impulse management sliding downhill in one monologue. Session 14 added four reversals and an escalated jab, and Session 15 enthusiasm ahead of information (*"It's time to take them out\!"*).
   * **Session 16: holds at \+0.5.** No fear statements this time. He thought about ramming without damage reduction and didn't.
5. **Lock-Step** `−6.5`
   * **All-time.** **Berserk is loss of impulse control rendered as a game mechanic.** Around it sit sedating a teammate on impulse, beating a captive conscious and proposing arson. The violent impulses have stayed down in recent fights; the social ones have not. In Session 15 he started a stream inside a League station, shamed five teenagers on camera, and made his kill request *"slurred in moral conflict."*
   * **Session 16: holds at −6.5.** The Session 15 pattern, unchanged. The violent impulses stayed down through a fight that one-shot him; the social ones ran, and the session opened with an unprompted grievance delivered to a stranger. *"This is what happens when I stay with the team. I should have vanished,"* said seconds after a redirected shot hit him, is the honest version of the old impulse to leave. **He stayed.**

### **1.10 Moral Agency / Ethical Independence**

*Capacity for independent ethical judgement, as against deference and obedience. High \= reasons from his own principles and acts on them.*

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** **The clearest demonstrated moral agency on the team.** He refused to drug Quasar-Force when asked by the team's most socially powerful member, objected aloud to the prisoner humiliation, called Nightwing about the artifact knowing the cost, and refused Psyche's offer from inside his own head. In Session 14 he was the one who credited Wonder Woman's apology. In Session 15 he changed his mind about the progenitor on the evidence and went past the room (*"We really should help them find a different place"*). ⟨Note⟩ He was present for the Day 56 planning in which two teammates arranged a lethal outcome by proxy, and raised no objection.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+9.0.** Session 15 left a question — whether that silence was bandwidth or acquiescence — and **Session 16 did not answer it.** Nobody discussed lethal force. The Destined errand came up twice in passing, as Quasar-Force's *"murder hobos"* and Lock-Step's *"the other team's problem,"* and drew no objection from anyone. Oracle's one sentence about the warehouse's occupants is garbled in the transcript — *"we need to, hopefully, die, harming them?, dissuade them?"* — and it was Lock-Step who answered with knock-outs. **The question is carried, unscored, to Session 17** (§12).
2. **Quasar-Force** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** He reasons from an articulated frame rather than from instruction. In Session 14 he defended the box's personhood against the only qualified person in the room (*"She is still a person"*). In Session 15 he asserted himself four times — the tentacle, the shield, a principle argued with a Grey Lantern, and a proposed truth exchange — and also moved, on his own reasoning, toward lethal force by proxy.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.0.** He corrected the dominant teammate on the record (*"You're mainly the one that told me to fix it"*) and defended the adults the room was condemning (*"everyone is busy, and we're busy too"*). Both are independence, and both are continuous with what he already scores.
3. **Lock-Step** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Maximum independence: he acts on his own judgement and defers to nobody. His Session 13 position on adults sending children into world-ending situations was vindicated in Session 14, and he wrote the team's only mission-vetting proposal. In Session 15 he questioned a cosmic officer's premise and built the argument she adopted. **Then he abandoned his own stated principle under a clock** (*"we don't have the time to be democratic"*), which caps the score.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.0.** He put the line where it belonged for the warehouse — *"Nope, nope… knock them out"* — and did it by citing Nightwing, which is agency borrowing authority rather than replacing it. *"The other situation, is that other team's problem"* keeps the Session 15 exception exactly where he left it.
4. **Edge** `+4.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** So far the evidence of independent judgement is tactical rather than moral. He made the case for Argent first on his own reasoning, and declined the cynicism he was offered on arrival (*"I don't want to break your hope"*). The backstory's one moral fact is a choice: when his power appeared, he went looking for someone to teach him restraint.
5. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** *"Will go with any team decision"* — and in Session 14 he went with it and then argued that there had been nothing else to go with. Session 15 changed nothing: he accepted a cosmic officer's premise, voted to attack, and said *"I'm not really a thinker."*
   * **Session 16: \+1.5 → \+2.0 (+0.5).** **The first time he exercised a judgement against a teammate's offer and kept it.** Asked whether he would stay out if teleported clear, he refused to leave. It was a small decision, and it was his own.

### **1.11 Morality / Moral Alignment**

*Not judgement or discipline — simply how good this person is.*

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** He heals people who were trying to kill him minutes earlier, demands surrender before every engagement, attempted to revive a man he had no reason to save, and refuses to take part in cruelty, out loud. In Session 15 his instinct about a predator was elimination, and his conclusion, reached by listening, was relocation and protection.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+9.0.** Every point of damage he dealt was Focus damage, and his last act of the fight was healing the teammate who could carry the others out.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** He treats captured enemies as people, pulled bodies out of a gassed room, gave a statement against his own interest, and took a knife through the abdomen for a nation he has never visited. **The fatality on his record was an accident he has never tried to minimise**, and his stated rule is to kill only against lethal intent that cannot otherwise be stopped. Session 15 was the best moral record of his campaign, with one sentence set against it: *"I'm sure we would be more comfortable unleashing Fade-Out with his full lethal fury on the controller."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.0.** *"I want to help people get their happy thoughts back,"* said to a teammate who has lost his, is the moral centre of the session's first scene. *"We sent the murder hobos after the bad progenitor"* is the Session 15 sentence again, an hour later and in plainer words. **The line has not moved back, and the hold is the cost of that.**
3. **Intrepid** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** Straightforwardly decent. He takes hits meant for softer people, is afraid of hurting anyone, and said his reason for going to the Watchtower was *"I'm just going to protect you guys."* He sits below the top two only because his goodness is passive.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.0.** He stayed in the room where the others were being hurt.
4. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** The first recorded use of his power was protective: an attacker's weapon sliced apart during an assault. His first fight was spent taking a mind-controller off the board, shooting a device out of a villain's hand, and holding off Blitzkrieg alone so that Lock-Step could get Oracle and Quasar-Force out. ⟨Volatile⟩ The roster's legend is that what he was *"cut down armies."* **This axis scores the person in the room, and the person in the room used a lethal tool for protective ends.**
5. **Lock-Step** `−1.0`
   * **All-time.** Genuinely split rather than simply bad. **For:** handcuffs offered first, bloodless captures, a bank's worth of hostages teleported clear, and the Session 14 heal. **Against:** a fourteen-year-old teammate sedated twice, a restrained prisoner humiliated, and consent treated as a formality. Session 15 put the largest numbers on both sides: a species saved, and five released teenagers shamed on camera, two teammates' minds linked without consent, and a detainee's death commissioned so the Titans could *"keep our hands clean."* **On Kwa his argument was "they said they're people"; at home it was "it's not human."**
   * **Session 16: −1.5 → −1.0 (+0.5).** **The good got bigger in the place it matters most this week.** The warehouse held five infected human teenagers, and he insisted they be knocked out, offered them handcuffs twice, asked teammates before moving them, and carried four teammates out of the building. Against it, *"The other situation, is that other team's problem"* leaves the Session 15 errand unretracted, and *"I don't know why you keep wanting to do things the hard way"* was said to a teenager whose eyes had just rolled back under someone else's command. The inconsistency the last revision named — people when they are human, not when they are not — is still the shape of his morality.

### **1.12 Line-Holding Under Duress**

*Not what they believe — what survives contact with pressure.*

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** Four separate documented holds against four kinds of pressure — peer (the sedation request), group (the humiliation), loyalty (the call to Nightwing) and compassion (Psyche, inside his head). He has never folded. Session 15 added the first open question on his file: no objection when two teammates arranged a lethal outcome by proxy.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+9.0.** ⟨Note⟩ Still open, and still not scored (see 1.10).
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** He recited the conduct standard from memory, held his position against a Lex-Corp attorney, and held against expertise on the box's personhood. **What he does not do is convert holding a line into imposing one.** Session 15 had two holds against a teammate — the tentacle and the shield — and one shift: his line on force moved under a 24-hour clock, to lethal action by proxy.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** *"We sent the murder hobos after the bad progenitor."* The line that moved in Session 15 is still where the clock left it, and nothing new pressed on it.
3. **Intrepid** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** None of his lines was tested until Session 14, and then one broke four times in a single conversation under one teammate's single word. **"Goes with the team" and "cannot hold a position against a friend" were different findings, and the second was his.**
   * **Session 16: \+2.5 → \+3.5 (+1.0).** **The first held line on his file, and it was held against the teammate who broke him in Session 14\.** Offered an exit mid-fight, with his Sturdy stolen and his Health falling, he refused to leave and stayed refused. The log adds that when the team finally withdrew, he was one of those who *"would be objecting to retreat"* and was too unconscious to do it.
4. **Edge** `+3.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** His physical line held: knocked to exactly zero, up again, and holding Blitzkrieg off alone. No moral line was pressured. Restraint, the value his whole profile is built on, was not tested against a reason to drop it.
5. **Lock-Step** `−4.0`
   * **All-time.** **He has been the pressure the others hold against** — the sedation, the humiliation, the concealment and the non-consensual extractions were all his initiative. His early holds were self-interested. Session 15 gave him the first hold not in his own favour, on Kwa. Then came the collapse in its most literal form: *"No, I'm not coming around on lethal force"* in the room, and *"cinder blocks in the ocean"* in private.
   * **Session 16: −4.5 → −4.0 (+0.5).** He held the non-lethal line for the warehouse under the pressure of a fight that one-shot him, and he was not the pressure on anyone else: he asked before moving people. The Session 15 collapse is unretracted.

### **1.13 Judgment Under Stress**

1. **Oracle** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** He retargets mid-action on precognitive input, holds rather than burns actions, and made the coldest correct call of the campaign in Session 9\. In Session 14 he kept producing from inside a grab and made the failure call nobody else wanted to make. In Session 15 he turned a failed Logic check into a structured, updated 24-hour forecast that named the warehouse before anyone else did.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.5.** Asked for a plan, he said he had none, then proposed the one the team used: teleport in and overwhelm one or two fast. His module confirmed the newcomer's target. **The best call of his fight was the quietest.** With Quasar-Force at −15 and Lock-Step unconscious, he moved Let's Go to the teammate who could teleport and healed him, and the extraction happened. The low-Health re-entry is the counterweight (1.5).
2. **Edge** `+6.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** The best tactical read in the pre-fight conversation came from the person who had been on the team for ten minutes: take the mind-controller first, because she *"can turn 5 vs 5 into 6 vs 4."* Under fire he picked targets by threat, chose the device over a body when the device was the objective, and moved after shooting. **One session, and the read was right every time it was tested.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** His read of what matters is often the best in the room. His read of what it will cost has failed twice on the record — the Session 8 crush and the Session 14 break from cover, which was the worst of his campaign. Session 15 had no fatal error: he refused the tentacle, proposed truth verification and kept evidence discipline. Against it, he escalated an argument about exterminating humanity with a being who could have acted on it.
   * **Session 16: \+5.5 → \+6.0 (+0.5).** The deductions were good: that the progeny had not yet given the five *"full access to any new powers,"* and that the warehouse was *"probably the distraction they are staging."* So was the self-preservation — hit to −5, he went to cover. **Against:** the villains had a plan for him specifically, built on his fame, his fragility and his preference for grappling, and he did not have one for that.
4. **Intrepid** `+3.0`
   * **All-time.** He takes positioning guidance well and has visibly improved. He also committed a full action to a grab on a target whose force field had already negated a chain whip, and he still stomps rooms with ordinary humans in them. In Session 15 he took a cosmic officer's premise at face value and called for an attack before any facts were in.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.0.** *"Nothing, I guess"* for a plan. In the fight he took every assist offered and refused the one exit offered.
5. **Lock-Step** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** The old record — the blind teleport into ten monsters, the arson proposal, the captive beaten conscious, Berserk — has been climbing toward zero. The Session 14 healing hex was a cold, correct call. Session 15 held the best judgement sequence of his campaign, on Kwa, and then the Destined: a debt, and a private request to kill that Fade-Out can use against him for as long as he likes.
   * **Session 16: −0.5 → 0.0 (+0.5).** **Sound in action.** He pulled the downed healer out of the room, asked before moving anyone, and saved his last Focus for the last teammate. Against it, the pre-fight briefing he gave the newcomer was wrong on both facts it asserted. His first extraction also left him alone outside the building, where Blitzkrieg came out after him.

### **1.14 Justice League Vetting — Character & Conduct**

*Character side only; capability is 7.4. Scored as valence: \+10 \= would clear vetting and be championed by whoever sponsored him; 0 \= nothing for or against; −10 \= the record is disqualifying and reflects badly on the team that put him forward.*

1. **Oracle** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Exactly the profile a senior organisation wants. He reports honestly, defers to legitimate authority, refuses unethical instructions from peers, prioritises life over victory, and has generated no incident in sixteen sessions. **Session 14 added the entry a panel values most and sees least: he made the failure call himself.** Session 15's silence is the one line a panel might mark for follow-up.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.5.** A panel would find a fight conducted entirely in Focus damage and two teammates kept alive.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+4.0`
   * **All-time.** His conduct would genuinely impress a panel: voluntary police cooperation, unprompted intelligence sharing, evidence discipline and an exemplary stay as the League's guest. **He is held below Oracle by an open fatality investigation**, and since Session 15 by a sentence no vetting officer reads past: a fourteen-year-old endorsing Fade-Out's *"full lethal fury."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+4.0.** The same file, plus *"murder hobos."*
3. **Edge** `+3.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Clean conduct, a League-adjacent sponsor in Black Canary, and a recruiter currently wearing the cowl. **A panel would ask one question first: the legend that he is a reborn weapon that cut down armies.** A clean first session does not answer it, and nothing in his conduct raises it.
4. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Clean, obedient and uncomplicated. A panel reading Session 14 would find a candidate who argued, in front of the sponsoring Justice Leaguer, that more information would not have helped, and framed the risk as being replaced. **That is not misconduct; it is a candidate talking himself down in the interview.** *"Not yet anyway"* is the counterweight.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+2.0.** Nothing a panel would weigh.
5. **Lock-Step** `−8.5`
   * **All-time.** **The one file that is actively worse than no file.** Doctor Fate has publicly declared him a danger to the world. Beneath that are the sedations, the humiliated captive, the concealed artifact and two Berserk episodes; Session 14's lasso; and Session 15's two files — a species saved, a station livestreamed, juveniles shamed, a telepath invited into teammates' minds, and a killing solicited through a villain team.
   * **Session 16: holds at −8.5.** Session 16 adds the best combat-conduct page in his file — consent asked, handcuffs offered twice and four teammates carried out. A panel would read it after the solicitation, which is still in a villain's memory.

## **2\. Enemy Risk**

**Risk `−10.0 to 0.0`**

⟨Note⟩ **Session 16 is the first session in which a hostile force on the field was ordered to take the Titans themselves** — Blitzkrieg's *"my master's orders has changed to collecting you since you proved yourselves worthy"* and *"we need to collect these specimens"* — and the first in which villains explained, mid-fight, why they chose their first target. Both are scored in 2.2, the one axis that moved for all four returning members.

### **2.1 Vulnerability to Outside Subversion**

*(Position 1 \= most vulnerable.)*

1. **Lock-Step** `−10.0`
   * **All-time.** The only member with a **confirmed, completed hostile operation** run against him: a temporal artifact purpose-built to lure him, profile him, transmit his data to an unknown recipient and brick itself. Around it sit the recruitment attempts, the three-way pull between Doctor Fate, Zatanna and Constantine, the Chaotic / Supernatural / Hounded tags, and a power source that may have been made from a piece of him. For most of the campaign Ego 20 and Protection Hex shut the psychic route, and his vector was biographical and social. **Session 15 opened every front at once.** The progenitor entered his mind *"with ease even with his protection hex up"* — the first logged entry past the Hex — and then read his memories with his consent, coming out knowing about portal travel, the Grey Lanterns and his first name. A cosmic order tested him without warning and put a ring on him that he cannot charge until he remembers an oath. The Destined hold a debt, and Fade-Out holds his private, verbatim request to have a detainee killed, which Fade-Out received as an endorsement (*"About time you stepped up the leadership"*). Three outside parties ran operations on him in one session, which is what the −10.0 anchor describes: *maximal, operations already underway.*
   * **Session 16: holds at −10.0 (the floor).** Nothing new was run on him and nothing was closed. The briefing said the warehouse was trafficking something keyed to his time manipulation, and the brood spent the fight searching crates for it; that is targeting, and it is scored in 2.2. ⟨Volatile⟩ Nobody knows what the device did, and Edge destroyed it.
2. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** The strongest structural case in the document. An alien intelligence with a damaged personality, residual Apokoliptian corruption, its own survival drive and its own agenda lives inside his central nervous system and cannot be removed without killing him. ⟨OOC⟩ Built by New Genesis, rebuilt by Darkseid's scientists. Session 14 gave the threat a face, Azimuth standing over his unconscious body as an astral projection, and showed that the box tunes his emotional baseline by design and that a single image could override it. Session 15 answered two of the three findings in hardware: the circlet masks the New God signal Azimuth's search depends on, and Uncanny 3 raises every mental defence (Vigilance 18, Ego 20, Logic 20\) and adds Focus damage reduction 3\. He also raised the Azimuth memory himself under a telepath's read, and the box did not panic. What remains is still the second-worst profile on the board. The League's specialist cannot see where he ends and the box begins, the Grey Lantern's verdict is *"no longer merely human,"* and he still answers whoever asks politely. **The mask hides the signal and does nothing about his manner.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −9.5.** The Session 15 hardware had its first hostile test, and passed. Blood Blade's Swipe, which steals a power on a hit, landed a Marvel for 38 and then failed *"against his fortified mind."* Swipe is a power thief, not a telepath, so this confirms the mitigation rather than extending it. The same attack worked on Intrepid later in the fight.
3. **Oracle** `−7.0`
   * **All-time.** His cybernetics are a STAR Labs product he did not build and cannot fully audit, described in his own notes as a *Bio-Symbiotic Mutation Core, Host 'WADE' — Initiated & Maintaining*: the language of a relationship, not a prosthesis. In Session 12 something wrote into his predictive module, his own diagnostics reported no intrusion and no telepathy, and **the channel has never been fixed.** His Ego defence of 10 is the softest door on the roster. Session 15 put a telepath inside: the progenitor read his intent to hunt it (*"then you come to hunt us. I see it in your mind"*), with no roll or defence recorded. The module also *"did not adapt well"* to temporal transport, and it now resonates with Lock-Step's time-altering powers, which ties his cognition to a phenomenon nobody fully understands. What protects him is character: no divided loyalties, no factions courting him, and nothing hidden.
   * **Session 16: holds at −7.0.** The fight contained a telepath with some mind control, and no attempt on any Titan's mind is recorded. Argent was the team's agreed first target; Oracle's opening super-Marvel stunned her, Quasar-Force grabbed her, and Lock-Step knocked her out inside the first round. Dragonborne turned Oracle's Return Fire back onto a teammate, which is a counter to his mechanics (2.2), not a way into his head.
4. **Edge** `−5.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Three routes, none yet used.
     * **Psychic.** Mental defences of 15 to 16, with Uncanny 2 and Focus damage reduction 2: better than Oracle's Ego 10 and Intrepid's Vigilance 14, well short of the two members at the top.
     * **Biographical.** The widest route he has. His backstory is a legend that he is an ancient monster reborn, one that *"cut down armies"* and whose true name is lost. His powers arrived with constant headaches and eyes that glow, and his narrative profile says he is still deciding whether he gets to be Murphy King or has to be the monster. **Whoever arrives claiming to know what he is has a route nobody else on the team offers.**
     * **Social.** He is the newest member, he met his teammates minutes before the fight, and his strongest attachments are outside the team, to Black Canary and the Birds of Prey.

     Against all of it, nobody is known to be courting him, and his first session was clean. ⟨Volatile⟩ If the legend is literal, this is the axis it moves first.
5. **Intrepid** `−3.0`
   * **All-time.** Least vulnerable, and it is a genuine virtue rather than an absence: a mutant origin, no technology, no artifacts, no patrons, no factions, no secrets from the team, Iron Will, and the only Secret Identity on the active roster. His levers are personal and he named them himself: the fear of hurting people and, since Session 14, the fear of being replaced. **Mental defence was always going to be the way in**, and Session 15 proved it when the progenitor pulled him into its network with a 14-Marvel against his Vigilance of 14, and he could not shake it for the rest of the day. The same session gave him a new lever: a time traveller, a hive mind and a viral stream all called him Superman, and he answered *"Not yet anyway."*
   * **Session 16: −2.5 → −3.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** **The first Titan to have a power taken from him.** The Swipe that failed against Quasar-Force's fortified mind succeeded against him and stole Sturdy 3, so he lost his damage reduction mid-fight and Blood Blade gained it. He got it back only when an Edge-assisted Smash stunned her and broke her concentration. That is two sessions and two different hostile powers getting into him. He stays last because nobody is working on him; what is now proved is that anyone who tries can get in.

###

### **2.2 Target Priority for Hostile Organizations**

*(Lex-Corp, the Destined, the Marrow Syndicate, STAR Labs' leadership, the Breeder and its brood, and Apokolips-adjacent interests. Position 1 \= highest priority target.)*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−10.0`
   * **All-time.** At once the **evidence engine**, the **object of study** and **the quarry.**
     * **The evidence engine.** He built the case tying Lex-Corp to human experimentation, holds three redundant archives, runs the drone fleet and has made a statement on the record. In Session 15 he built the shield that shuts the Destined out of the Titans' floors of the tower, traced the limousine to STAR Labs' CFO, and kept the only evidentiary account against the organisation that holds the Breeder (*"we had more evidence before than we do now"*).
     * **The object of study.** He is New Genesis technology with a public face, a known father at STAR Labs, 60 Health, no healing factor and the build of an eleven-year-old. Doctor Varga wants him in a laboratory, the Prodigy breeder called this team *excellent progeny*, and a week of livestreamed workbench footage advertised his hardware to anyone watching.
     * **The quarry.** A New God who defected from New Genesis to Apokolips is searching for the object fused to his nervous system. ⟨OOC⟩ Taleria's reading is that Azimuth's experiment failed and the merge with Endo completed it — *"it wasn't his success, it was hers."* The circlet lowered the price of the quarry role in Session 15, and nothing else did.
   * **Session 16: −9.5 → −10.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** **A hostile team arrived with a doctrine for him, and said so.** Two of the brood opened on him in the Marvel round: Dragonborne missed, and Blood Blade hit for 38 and then 27 and put him at −5. Later, in their own words, they explained the choice: his fame, his known fragility, and his preference for grappling, which they called the thing most damaging to their tactics. In Round 3 Blitzkrieg's glitch bomb, which ignores damage reduction, put him at −30, the deepest knockdown on his file. Then the controller's orders changed to collecting the whole team. **His fame, his fragility and his fighting style are now an enemy's stated reasons, with an operation attached.** That is the floor.
2. **Lock-Step** `−8.0`
   * **All-time.** Someone spent a purpose-built temporal artifact on him, which is a demonstrated willingness to invest. Add the magical factions and the Marrow Syndicate's willingness to talk to him specifically, and he is a person several organisations have concluded is worth the money. Session 15 supplied the reason — his powers are time-altering and lantern-derived — and tied the old operation to a new one. Oracle's module put the five infected teenagers on course for the mob warehouse where his time device had been found and used as a lure, and he had just made himself the public antagonist of those same five, zooming in on their faces for his followers.
   * **Session 16: −7.5 → −8.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** The forecast was right about the warehouse, and the briefing said what was in it: illicit materials, including something keyed to his time manipulation. The brood spent the fight searching crates for it. Dragonborne threw the crate that held it, and Blitzkrieg had the device in her hand and was phasing for the wall when Edge shot it apart. **The same Marrow Syndicate warehouse has now twice held hardware built around his powers.** ⟨Volatile⟩ What the device did, and who wanted it, is unknown, and the device no longer exists. Then the order came to collect the team.
3. **Oracle** `−4.5`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs cybernetics are worth reverse-engineering, and the predictive module is a standing tactical nuisance to anyone fighting the Titans. In Session 13 it located an invisible attacker nobody else could find, and he shot her; on Day 56 it named the enemy's destination before the enemy reached it. Session 14 showed the counter: Ecliptica held him in a grab and drained him from 180 Focus to 3 without ever beating his defences. That lowered the market price, and it is scored in 6.4. He is less publicly indexed than the two above, which is the main reason he is not higher.
   * **Session 16: −4.0 → −4.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** **The brood planned around him.** In Round 2 Dragonborne teleported into the middle of the team and hit Oracle and Intrepid together for 72 each — the healer and the biggest body in one action. When Oracle's Return Fire triggered, Dragonborne's reaction turned it onto Lock-Step. Two of his tools were answered inside one action, and he was on the floor at −2. The collection order covers him too.
4. **Edge** `−4.0`

   * **Debut, Session 16\.** He was on nobody's list when the session began, and he ended it as the member who cost the brood its objective: he shot the device out of Blitzkrieg's hand and destroyed it. Blitzkrieg spoke the collection order while attacking him, and in the hand-waved ending he was one of the two Titans she could not carry out. Behind that sits a Monster origin that any organisation that studies metahumans would want a sample of. His identity is public but, in his own entry, *"not very well known,"* and that is what keeps him below Oracle for now.
5. **Intrepid** `−3.0`

   * **All-time.** A problem to be solved on the battlefield, not a target to be acquired: a Secret Identity, no technology, no unique intelligence and nothing to steal. Organisations planned *around* him rather than *for* him until Session 15, when a viral stream called him *"maybe the next Superman"* in a campaign where Lex-Corp is an active hostile organisation. ⟨OOC⟩ The time traveller's premise opened with green kryptonite *"headed for you, Superman."*
   * **Session 16: −2.5 → −3.0 (−0.5, exposure up).** **He is the Titan the brood came closest to taking.** He ended the fight unconscious at −49, and the standing order was to collect specimens. The hand-waved ending gives two reasons Blitzkrieg withdrew without him: he weighs over 300 pounds, and Lock-Step's Force-infused whip can hit her while she is phased. Lock-Step went back for him on the last of his Focus. His mass did what his Secret Identity always did and made him expensive to acquire. **The order covers him anyway.**

*⟨Note⟩ Lobo is still deliberately excluded.* He is hunting Shein Qua, not a Titan, and Wonder Woman took the engagement personally. Session 16 does not mention him. The team briefed Wonder Woman after the fight, and nothing she said back is logged.

###

### **2.3 Target Priority for Amanda Waller — Squad Value**

*Position 1 \= highest priority as a Squad conscript.*

*(Waller has already appeared in-fiction — Session 6, Argus Station: she moved Shadow-Boxer to Belle Reve and pointed Quasar-Force at Project Catalyst.)*

**This axis asks one narrow question: who does she put in a harness?** A deniable field slot on Task Force X, with a bomb in the neck, sent somewhere she cannot be seen to have sent anyone. The criteria are fit, not value — coercible, expendable, quiet enough that nobody makes noise if they don't come back, and morally flexible enough to do what's asked. **Everything else she might want from these five is scored separately in 2.4.**

1. **Lock-Step** `−9.0`
   * **All-time.** The textbook profile, and nothing else on the roster is close. He is powerful, chaotic, and morally flexible enough to sedate a teammate and humiliate a restrained prisoner. **He has no parents, so nobody files the missing-persons report.** He moves comfortably through a criminal-adjacent social network, and he carries a documented misconduct file she could use without inventing anything. His near-immunity to mental coercion is not a deterrent but a *specification*: she reaches for a bomb rather than a leash, which she prefers anyway. Session 15 produced the most Squad-relevant item any Titan has: a killing commissioned by proxy, deniably and through a villain team, *"so we could technically keep our hands clean."* That is Task Force X's operating model, and he designed it himself under a 24-hour clock. **The one thing keeping him off the floor is that he is no longer quiet**: a viral stream, an audience and a named best friend mean somebody would notice if he stopped posting.
   * **Session 16: holds at −9.0.** In the field he was the opposite of his file. He offered handcuffs twice before swinging, insisted on knock-outs because *"Nightwing did say we can bring them back,"* and asked a teammate's permission before teleporting him. **None of that is visible from where she sits.** What she would read is the Session 15 commission, which he mentioned again in passing as *"that other team's problem."*
2. **Edge** `−6.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The closest skill fit on the roster.** A dedicated marksman with Accuracy 4, a signature Stopping Power, Double Tap and Suppressive Fire is the Squad's oldest archetype. Add a Monster origin, a legend about a creature that *"cut down armies,"* a power that slices through objects by concentration, and an identity that is public but *"not very well known."* ⟨OOC⟩ In at least one recent continuity Waller runs a squad made entirely of monsters. What keeps him behind Lock-Step is the handle and the noise. He has no record, no debts and no misconduct file, and his mentor is Black Canary, which puts the Birds of Prey and a League-adjacent hero one phone call from the harness. **He fits the slot; he does not yet fit the leash.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** **A Squad slot is the worst possible use of him.** The homicide on file is real leverage and his power is obvious. Against it, he is the most photographed hero in Blüdhaven, publicly identified, reads as eleven, and has parents in Slate Hill who would go to every camera in the state. **Burning a cultivated asset to fill a field slot is bad tradecraft, and she knows it.** The stated ethic about killing acquired a footnote in Session 15 — *"we would be more comfortable unleashing Fade-Out with his full lethal fury on the controller"* — and a Waller file would quote that sentence in full.
   * **Session 16: holds at −5.0.** He referred to the Destined in passing as *"the murder hobos"* sent *"after the bad progenitor."* The file already holds the sentence that matters, and this one adds nothing to it.
4. **Intrepid** `−3.0`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity is genuine deniability — there is no public figure to be missed — and he is by a wide margin the most physically survivable person on the team, which matters on operations where attrition is the point. He takes orders without argument. What keeps him low is that Waller can buy muscle anywhere, and he offers her no handle: no record, no debts, no secrets from his own team. A telepath opened him with a 14-Marvel in Session 15, which means she could use a leash rather than a bomb.
