   * **Session 16: holds at −3.0.** Blood Blade's Swipe went through the same door as the Session 15 telepath. That is vulnerability, scored in 2.1; she still has nothing to hold over him.
5. **Oracle** `−2.0`
   * **All-time.** **The worst Squad fit on the roster.** He has a spotless record, no leverage of any kind, an Extreme Appearance that makes him unmistakable on any operation, and a STAR Labs provenance that guarantees institutional questions. Decisively, he has an instinct to report irregularities to Nightwing at once, including when a teammate begged him not to. Handed a total operational failure in Session 14, his first action was to ring the sponsoring authority and report it. She would get one conversation with him, and Batman would know about it that evening.
   * **Session 16: holds at −2.0.** Nothing new. The team briefed Wonder Woman the same afternoon.

### **2.4 Target Priority for Amanda Waller — Strategic Interest**

*(Everything that is not a field slot: asset cultivation, informant recruitment, containment files, laboratory interest, and leverage held in reserve. Position 1 \= highest priority.)*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** She has already tasked him once: the Project Catalyst nudge at Argus Station used a fourteen-year-old as an unpaid intelligence asset, and she got away with it. He is New Genesis technology bonded to a compliant minor and the best technical mind in Blüdhaven. She can reach his parents, his identity is public, an accidental homicide sits on file, and he gives information freely to anyone polite. Session 14 added a New God hunting him, Justice League protection at the Watchtower and a League specialist building him countermeasures; **an asset that an intergalactic-tier adversary wants is worth a great deal more than the asset itself.** Session 15 added three builds ARGUS would want the specifications for before it wanted the builder: a device that masks a New God signature, a teleport shield that *"will learn and adapt other teleportation methodologies,"* and a nanosuit upgraded with a League specialist. Too visible to strap a bomb to; perfect to *cultivate*. **It is the most valuable file in this document.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −9.5.** The file did not grow. A hostile team targeted him first for his fame, his fragility and his grappling, which is a combat finding rather than a new reason to want him.
2. **Oracle** `−7.5`
   * **All-time.** **Second because of Precognition.** A sixteen-year-old who sees twenty-four hours ahead is worth more to a planning cell than any brawler she could conscript, and the module has shown it can *receive* as well as forecast — the difference between a weather report and a conversation. The STAR Labs cybernetics are a second interest: the specifications, the failure modes and the name of whoever authorised implanting them in a minor. He is compliant with legitimate authority, so she can approach him through channels, which is cheaper than leverage. Session 15 produced his most operationally useful forecast — a probability, an actor, a destination and a collision window, updated live — and showed the module resonating with Lock-Step's time-altering powers. **A STAR Labs implant that reads time-wave phenomena is exactly what a planning cell would want dissected.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −7.5.** The module worked all afternoon as a tactical instrument — a Marvel on initiative, a recommendation on whom to take first, a stomp steered onto the right target — and produced no new class of capability.
3. **Lock-Step** `−7.0`
   * **All-time.** Until Session 15 he was a poor investment for anything but a harness: chaotic, unsupervisable, and carrying magical entanglements that would bring Fate, Zatanna and Constantine to her door. Her interest was containment — knowing where he is. **Session 15 made him three things she collects.**
     * **A cosmic-order asset.** He is a designated Grey Lantern successor wearing a Grey Lantern ring. ⟨OOC⟩ Time powers are on the GM's ban list, which makes a time-coded fifteen-year-old a containment priority in any version of ARGUS.
     * **An informant.** He has a working relationship with the Destined, and he owes them.
     * **A public voice.** He administers the team's social media, every member's channel included.
   * **Session 16: holds at −7.0.** A Marrow Syndicate warehouse was found trafficking a device keyed to his time manipulation. For ARGUS that is third-party confirmation of a containment case it had already opened, and it is already priced in. ⟨Volatile⟩ If the device's purpose comes out, this is the axis it lands on.
4. **Edge** `−6.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **A containment file before he is anything else.** A Monster-origin fifteen-year-old whose legend says he is an ancient creature reborn, who can slice through objects by concentrating, and whose powers arrived with chronic headaches and eyes that glow, is exactly the profile ARGUS opens a laboratory file on. He brings a second asset — the team's best ranged shot — and a route through Black Canary to the Birds of Prey, a network she would rather watch than fight. **He sits below Lock-Step because nobody has yet confirmed what the legend is.**
5. **Intrepid** `−2.5`
   * **All-time.** A mutant physiology file and nothing more, held above zero only because ARGUS keeps files on metas as a matter of routine. A viral stream calling a sixteen-year-old *"the next Superman"* (Session 15\) is the sort of claim ARGUS opens a file on.
   * **Session 16: holds at −2.5.** Nothing new.

### **2.5 Susceptibility to Enemy Legal & Bureaucratic Action**

*(Position 1 \= most exposed. Lex-Corp's counsel is already engaged: a cease-and-desist is on record, a wrongful-death threat is circulating via the deceased's uncle, and Nightwing operates under the Batman mantle with correspondingly higher scrutiny.)*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** Not close. **He caused a death**, gave a statement to police, is publicly identified, is the named custodian of contested evidence, and argued statutory law with a Lex-Corp attorney while unqualified to do so. He is a minor, which makes his parents the real defendants and his statements easy to characterise as unreliable. A "science advocate" is already seeding the press with a chemical-exposure, diminished-control narrative. He is not *a* legal exposure for this team; he is *the* case.
   * **Session 16: holds at −9.5.** He crushed Stote unconscious inside a telekinetic grab. Every Titan used force on the five under the rule Lock-Step stated (*"knock them out"*), and nothing in the log takes his use outside it.
2. **Lock-Step** `−8.5`
   * **All-time.** A defence attorney's gift. He sedated a fourteen-year-old teammate twice — an assault, with a witness who refused to take part — and humiliated a restrained captive while another teammate objected on the record. He breaks and enters habitually and concealed evidence from his own mentor. He has an income, a weekly stipend on a debit card issued by Iron-Side, which means a payer, a payment record, a bank relationship and an unanswered employment-law question about a fifteen-year-old. Session 15 added the largest movement on any risk axis that revision (−3.0, to −8.0):
     * **Solicitation.** He asked a villain team to break into a research facility and kill a detainee in its custody, and clarified the request in private. If the Destined act on it, he is an accessory before the fact, and the witness to the request is someone who can sell it.
     * **The five teenagers.** He broadcast the faces of five recently released sixteen- and seventeen-year-olds with captions implying they were reoffending (*"They did their time. What do they do? They go back and do more crime"*), when they had done nothing, and added *\#SomeKidsJustNeedAWhooping*.
     * **The STAR Labs employee.** He broadcast her face and her vehicle's movements.
     * **The Watchtower.** He livestreamed the interior of a Justice League facility.

     **He was the member who once said out loud that this team needs lawyers. He is now the member who most needs one.**

   * **Session 16: −8.0 → −8.5 (−0.5, exposure up).** **He met the teenagers he had published, and handcuffed one of them.** Argent is one of the five. He offered her the cuffs, she began to accept before her controller reasserted itself, and he knocked her out with 84 damage and a second strike and cuffed her. When the team withdrew, the hand-wave left *"the unconscious villains"* behind in a Marrow Syndicate warehouse, Argent among them, still cuffed. **A minor he publicly accused of reoffending, then knocked unconscious and left restrained in a crime syndicate's building, is the next exhibit.** The conduct was defensible — the offer came first, and the force was the agreed non-lethal standard — but this axis scores the paperwork. If the Destined act on his Session 15 request, the solicitation will sit beside it.
3. **Intrepid** `−2.5`
   * **All-time.** Ground-Shaking Stomp discharged into rooms containing ordinary humans is a mass tort waiting for one bad roll, and he has come within a hair of it three times. There is no casualty, no public identity and no assets, so his exposure is entirely prospective.
   * **Session 16: holds at −2.5.** He stomped once, in a warehouse holding only the enemy, with Oracle's module steering it, and it reached Dragonborne and nobody else. The tort stays prospective.
4. **Edge** `−2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Little on file. He is fifteen, his identity is public but *"not very well known,"* and he has no record. The one incident in his backstory is an assault near him that ended with the attacker's weapon sliced to pieces. His first session added the fight's standard use of force against the five — one Stopping Power put 101 into Stote in a single action — and one piece of property destroyed: the device, which was being stolen at the time. **What would make him a case is the lethal ceiling of a power that slices through objects by concentration**, and nothing in Session 16 came near it. He sits above Oracle because a public identity and a lethal-capable kit are prospective exposures Oracle does not carry.
5. **Oracle** `−0.5`
   * **All-time.** The cleanest record on the team, and actively exculpatory for everyone else. He demands surrender before engaging, heals opponents who were trying to kill him, revived a man Intrepid nearly killed, and attempted to revive the man Quasar-Force killed. In Session 14 he produced the most useful thing a witness can produce: a contemporaneous report of a loss, made voluntarily to the principal, on the night. **If this team is ever deposed, Oracle is the defence's best witness.** ⟨Note⟩ He was present for the Day 56 planning; a deposition would ask him what he heard, and his answer would help nobody on his own side.
   * **Session 16: holds at −0.5.** He used Suppressive Fire on minors under someone else's control, and every point of it was Focus damage, not Health. He again sat through references to the Destined errand (*"murder hobos," "that other team's problem"*) without comment, which is the Session 15 deposition question asked a second time.

### **2.6 Personal Leverage / Exploitability**

*What an adversary can hold over this person specifically. Position 1 \= most exploitable.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** Every category at once: parents at a known address, a public identity, an accidental death on record, an artifact fused to his nervous system that cannot be removed without killing him, and a habit of answering honestly whoever asks politely. **He is fourteen, so leverage on him is leverage on his family and the reverse.** Session 14 found the most precise lever: shown a pencil sketch of Azimuth, he went into a passed-through panic attack and spent five minutes curled up on an infirmary bed. Session 15 weakened it — he raised the trauma himself under a telepath's read and the box held — and added a lever of a different kind. The entity inside him has a name, and he loves her: *"second mother," "Stella," "It's two people's home."* **Leverage on one of them is now visibly leverage on both.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −9.5.** Nothing new was put in anyone's hands. What the brood showed — that his fame tells an enemy how fragile he is — is targeting, scored in 2.2.
2. **Lock-Step** `−8.5`
   * **All-time.** A misconduct file that would end him with every authority figure in his life, and magical entanglements pulling three ways. For a long time he was insulated by having nothing to seize. Session 13's stipend and the truthful account extracted under the Lasso of Truth in Session 14 chipped at that, and Session 15 removed it. **A villain holds his private request to have someone killed, in his own words, and he knows it** — the most direct handle anyone in this document has held on a Titan. Around it:
     * **What he owes.** A favour to the Destined.
     * **What he could lose.** A public audience, a reputation he is actively building, and a named best friend with a public profile.
     * **Who knows him.** At least one outside party knows him as *"Eric Williams, known as the Gray Lantern of Earth."*
     * **What he wants.** A ring he cannot charge and a succession he very much wants.
   * **Session 16: holds at −8.5.** He gave a stranger his grievances in his first minute with him — Iron-Side *"lived in the security room,"* the adults *"just suck"* — and then described exactly what he lacks: adults *"actually being helpful."* That is a lever stated out loud, but he said it to a teammate, not an adversary.
3. **Edge** `−4.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Three levers, all on the sheet or in the backstory.
     * **The identity question.** Anyone who claims to know what the monster is, or how to stop the headaches, is offering something he has wanted since the powers arrived.
     * **The people.** Black Canary, who volunteered to train him, and the Birds of Prey behind her. His family is not described.
     * **The obligations.** School, with grades that were *"already bad"* before the powers, and a public identity.

     Against them: no record, no debts, and nothing known to be concealed from the team.
4. **Oracle** `−3.0`
   * **All-time.** A clean record, no divided loyalties and nothing concealed. The one lever that seemed to fit him — a voice inside his own head presenting as a frightened sixteen-year-old and asking to be saved — was tested under the worst available conditions and did not move him. What remains is structural rather than active: cybernetics he did not build and cannot fully audit, the weakest mental defence on the team, and a proven route into his cognition that a less honest occupant could use.
   * **Session 16: holds at −3.0.** Nothing new. ⟨Flag⟩ Edge's roster tags include *Mentor (PC: Oracle)*, which would make the newcomer someone Oracle is responsible for. Edge's backstory reads as Barbara Gordon (*"she called the Birds"*), and nothing in Session 16 shows the two had met. Not scored.
5. **Intrepid** `−2.0`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, no assets, no factions and no secrets from his own side. His levers are personal and he supplied them himself: the fear of hurting people, which requires finding him first; the fear of being replaced (Session 14); and an ambition, *"Not yet anyway"* (Session 15), which is an offer waiting for somebody to make it.
   * **Session 16: holds at −2.0.** He refused an extraction under fire, which is scored in 1.12. It gives an adversary nothing new to hold.

### **2.7 Value to Enemy as Hostage / Leverage**

*How much would be surrendered to get this person back. Position 1 \= most valuable to hold.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−9.5`
   * **All-time.** The team would give up almost anything, and so would several institutions. He is fourteen, presents as eleven, is universally liked, and carries New Genesis technology that Batman, STAR Labs and ARGUS each have separate interests in. Holding him now means negotiating with six parties: the team, his parents, the Justice League (Wonder Woman came to the tower within minutes for a stranger's problem), Guy Gardner, Doctor Taleria, and Azimuth. **Nobody on this roster is worth more alive.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −9.5.** The brood's first two attacks of the fight went at him, and its orders later changed to collecting the team. Holding him is the outcome this axis prices, and Session 16 showed somebody trying for it.
2. **Oracle** `−7.0`
   * **All-time.** Taking Oracle removes the team's entire recovery capability as well as a person, and he is the most trusted and best-liked member, so any ransom put to this team would be paid. Session 14 ran the scale model without anyone having to take him: Ecliptica held him in place and emptied him, and the team's only casualty stayed down until a chaos mage improvised a heal. Session 15 added the forecast; the team spent Day 56 planning around his percentages.
   * **Session 16: holds at −7.0.** **The scale model ran again, at full size.** While Oracle was down at −2, nobody healed anybody. When he came back, he spent the rest of the fight keeping Quasar-Force and then Lock-Step on their feet with Let's Go, and he healed both afterwards. Every recovery the team made in Session 16 went through him.
3. **Lock-Step** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** No family to ransom and a damaged relationship with his own side, which until Session 15 was nearly the whole entry. What raised him was external: Doctor Fate, Zatanna and Constantine all hold positions on him, which makes him a bargaining chip in negotiations he is not party to. Session 15 added parties who would pay: a time-travelling order that designated him a successor, the people of Kwa who owe their existence to him, and a team that now runs on his decisions. **Part of the price of holding him is Grey Lantern attention, and nobody knows what that costs.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −6.0.** He was the team's way out. He teleported Oracle into Argent's sphere and then out of the building unconscious, and at the end he extracted all four teammates on the last of his Focus. Taking him would have left the other four in the warehouse, and that is scored in 3.3 and 4.9. The price to his outside parties is unchanged.
4. **Edge** `−5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** The team has known him for an afternoon, so its own attachment is new. **The value comes from outside.** Black Canary volunteered to train him, which brings the Birds of Prey and a League-adjacent hero to the table, and Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited him personally. An enemy holding him would be negotiating with the Bat-family and the Birds as well as the team, which is more institutional weight than Intrepid carries.
5. **Intrepid** `−4.0`
   * **All-time.** Universally liked and institutionally invisible. The team would come for him; nobody else would. After Session 15 his audience would come for him too, which is not the same as anybody paying.
   * **Session 16: holds at −4.0.** The team did come for him: Lock-Step went back in on the last of his Focus. That is the whole of the price, and it was paid on the day.

###

### **2.8 Consequence Severity in the Event of Compromise**

*Conditional severity, not likelihood — how bad is it if this person defects, is mind-controlled, or is turned by leverage. Position 1 \= worst outcome.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `−10.0`
   * **All-time.** **The worst single scenario in the campaign.** Psyche ran the scale model twice across Sessions 12 and 13, the second time to completion, bringing down the main containment field on the Celestial Core with his own telekinesis. A compromised Quasar-Force delivers the complete Lex-Corp evidence chain, three redundant archives, the drone fleet and its codes, access to three laboratories and the team's operational picture. It also delivers a body that can lift cruise-ship tonnage without a check, wrapped around an Apokoliptian-modified intelligence with its own agenda. Since Session 14 the compromise surface has been one hybrid, and only half of it is a fourteen-year-old. Session 15 added the tower's teleport shield to the payload, and the device that hides him from Azimuth, which a compromised Quasar-Force could simply take off.
   * **Session 16: holds at −10.0 (the floor).** Nothing in Session 16 changed the payload.
2. **Lock-Step** `−9.0`
   * **All-time.** Teleport Together means a compromised Lock-Step can put anything anywhere, including inside the tower, past every wall the team owns. Berserk has shown what he does without restraint, and Doctor Fate's stated objection is that his chaotic nature is a danger on a scale larger than Blüdhaven. Session 15 added a ring of unknown capability and a time-coded power source, a detailed knowledge of the Watchtower guest floor, and the team's entire public voice. **A compromised Lock-Step controls how the public sees the others.** He also showed that when rushed he will order a killing through intermediaries.
   * **Session 16: holds at −9.0.** Session 16 demonstrated the first sentence. He put Oracle inside Argent's force-field sphere, carried an unconscious teammate out of the building, and moved the whole team out of a hostile building one at a time. **The same power, turned, puts an enemy where the team sleeps.**
3. **Edge** `−7.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Third on arrival, and the reason is the legend.** Mechanically, a turned Edge is the team's best ranged shot — Agility damage ×7+7, Accuracy 4, a signature Stopping Power that put 101 into one target in one action — with a healing factor, Slow-Motion Dodge, and the only Heightened Senses on the active roster. He lives in the tower, in the bedroom that was Exemplar II's. Behind the kit is a power that slices through objects by concentration, a roster title, *The Reborn Calamity*, and a backstory about an ancient monster that *"cut down armies."* **If the legend is literal, the severity is not the kit but what the kit is attached to.** ⟨Volatile⟩ He ranks above Oracle because Oracle's payload is information and recovery, and Edge's is lethality.
4. **Oracle** `−6.5`
   * **All-time.** Underrated and severe. A turned Oracle hands the enemy **twenty-four hours of precognitive lookahead and a perfect eidetic record of everything this team has ever done or discussed**, then heals the people fighting it. The channel into his module is proven and unrepaired. What keeps him below the members above is auditability: the one demonstrated write came with an identifiable author who could be — and was — questioned about it afterwards. A telepath got into his mind in Session 15, which moved the likelihood in 2.1, not the payload.
   * **Session 16: holds at −6.5.** The payload is unchanged. Session 16 showed how much of the team's survival runs through his healing (2.7); a turned Oracle would withhold that as well as use everything else.
5. **Intrepid** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** A hostile Intrepid is a containment problem this team cannot solve with the tools it fields. He has 210 Health, DR 3, Healing Factor 7, Immovable, and an indiscriminate area attack. The Rank 3.5 advance widened the gap, and Session 15 showed that the door into him is mental.
   * **Session 16: holds at −6.0.** Session 16 ran a partial version of the problem. Blood Blade took his Sturdy 3, used it against him and put him down; Dragonborne, a flying brick on the other side, hit him for 72 and then put him down for 60\. **The team spent the afternoon unable to put down the one enemy built like him**, and Dragonborne left demoralised, not beaten. A turned Intrepid would be that problem with 210 Health and a healing factor.

*Read this axis against 2.1.* Among the returning four, Session 16 changed neither ordering. Lock-Step and Quasar-Force are first and second on likelihood and second and first on severity, so the two most dangerous people to lose are still the two most likely to be taken. Edge enters fourth on likelihood and third on severity. **He is not especially likely to be taken; what a taker would get is the open question**, and it is the same question his narrative profile says he has not yet answered: Murphy King or the monster.

## **3\. Team Internals**

### **3.1 Internal Reputation with Teammates**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** Universally trusted, and it is earned rather than granted. He feeds the team, heals it and keeps his word, and what actually generates respect is that he declines to do the wrong thing even when a teammate with more social pull asks him to. Lock-Step still comes to him for help *after* being refused, which is the clearest signal available. In Session 14 Quasar-Force named him first among the teammates he wanted at the Watchtower. In Session 15 Lock-Step took him — *"my boy"* — into first contact as the one person who could keep the scout alive, and the room turned to him when it ran out of ideas (*"Oracle, You got anything?"*). The one public jab came on Lock-Step's stream: *"the guy that always tells us what we do wrong and tries to fix it, but you know, sometimes, it just doesn't work."* **That is the joke you make about the person whose judgement you depend on.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+9.0.** The jab became a title. When the newcomer asked whether there was a plan, Lock-Step answered for the team: *"Oracle is our plan guy."* Oracle's own answer was *"No plan. Not on this one,"* and a few minutes later the team adopted his teleport ambush anyway. Already at the top of the band.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** Genuinely loved. Oracle calls him the only sane person in the group and cooks around his appetite, and Lock-Step, leaving the city once, asked *him* to look in on his friends. Session 14 rendered the affection as logistics: the team carried him home unconscious, Iron-Side sat with him through the night, and Lock-Step spent Focus he did not have to wake him. **The discount is structural:** the team talks to him as a pre-teen. Session 15 raised the intellectual respect (*"it's not like they have smarter people. We have Quasar-Force"*; to a villain, *"That's our smarts"*) and, for the first time, put an open question on the table about who is speaking when he speaks: *"Quasar, like what are you? Are you human or are you mother box?"* High affection, capped authority, and a new doubt.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.0.** The discount was spoken aloud to the newcomer: *"you see that kid that looks like he is like 12 right there? They just told him to fix it."* So was the correction, by Quasar-Force himself: *"You're mainly the one that told me to fix it."* The question about the box did not come up.
3. **Intrepid** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** No enemies, no complaints, no friction. He plays video games with Lock-Step, takes hits for everyone and goes along with team decisions cheerfully. He is well liked, and nobody defers to him about anything. Session 14 was the first time a teammate went after him rather than around him — Lock-Step called him a coward and would not let it go — and Intrepid escalated back, which nobody had seen him do. Session 15 reversed it in public: *"Sorry, Intrepid. I will never doubt you again,"* and to the stream, *"this guy is going to be Superman."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.5.** Lock-Step offered him an extraction under fire, he refused it, and Lock-Step respected the refusal and came back for him at the end. **The teammate who called him a coward in Session 14 asked his permission in Session 16\.** That is regard, and it is already priced at this level.
4. **Edge** `+5.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Welcomed before he had done anything — *"Oh, thank god, finally, we get somebody competent"* — and then he earned it in order: the target call the team adopted, two decisive assists to Intrepid, the shot that destroyed the device, and a round alone against Blitzkrieg so that Lock-Step could get the others out. He asked the two questions the team had not asked itself (*"Do we have a plan for when we enter this place?"* and *"Can you tell me the situation, please?"*), and his reply to Oracle's briefing — *"Ok, that told me nothing"* — is the kind of line a room keeps. **The regard is real, and it is an afternoon old.**
5. **Lock-Step** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** Split into two accounts that don't reconcile. **Professional regard is high and still rising.** He held combat discipline for four consecutive engagements, took direction from four teammates in one fight, configured a hex into healing hands to wake the casualty in Session 14, and in Session 15 led the team and was followed (Oracle: *"I'll follow your lead"*; Quasar-Force: *"Lock-Step is blessed"*). **Character regard is damaged**, and the file behind it is long: the sedations, the humiliated prisoner, the concealed artifact, the fight with Intrepid over the word *coward* in front of Wonder Woman, and the lasso session. Session 15 added the use of every teammate as stream content, talking over Oracle on camera, pressing Quasar-Force to accept a bodily intrusion, and asking a telepath to link two teammates' minds without asking them. The disapproval showed only in behaviour: Quasar-Force disagreed with him three times and won each time.
   * **Session 16: \+4.5 → \+5.0 (+0.5).** The professional account moved again. He knocked out the mind-controller after offering her the cuffs, teleported Oracle into her sphere, carried the unconscious Oracle out of the building mid-fight, asked Intrepid before teleporting him, and at the end extracted all four teammates on the last of his Focus. The character account added two wrong claims in front of the newcomer. Quasar-Force corrected one to his face (*"You're mainly the one that told me to fix it"*), and the GM contradicted the other — *"they didn't even fill him in"* — by revealing that Nightwing had briefed Edge. There was also one line aimed at his own side: *"This is what happens when I stay with the team. I should have vanished."* **The team has more reason to value him than it did, and the same reasons to discount him.**

### **3.2 Internal Influence with Teammates**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

1. **Lock-Step** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Raw, demonstrated influence. He moved the entire team to a party none of them wanted to attend, decided the artifact would go in the vault, and made the surrender ritual de facto team doctrine. He teleports people out of combat without asking, and the team argues afterwards rather than stopping him. Inside the Justice League's own station in Session 14 he assigned work and people, and all of it happened. Session 15 was the most complete demonstration of influence in the document: three teammates opened the Kwa scene ready to fight, and he turned all three to *talk first*; on Day 56 he hosted the refugee, called in the Destined, gave them their task and split the remaining work. **Groups follow whoever acts, and he acts.** The limit also has a name: Quasar-Force disagreed with him three times in Session 15 — on the tentacle, the shield and the plan to take over the hive — and won every time.
   * **Session 16: \+9.0 → \+8.5 (−0.5).** **He set the chair down himself.** Asked for a plan by the newcomer, he pointed at Oracle — *"Oracle is our plan guy. I'm just the if it all goes to hell… that's my element"* — and the plan came from Oracle and Edge. His influence showed in the rule of engagement, where his correction of Oracle's garbled line (*"Nope, nope, Nightwing did say we can bring them back, so knock them out"*) became the standard, and in the exit, which he ran. It also met a refusal: he offered Intrepid an extraction, and Intrepid said no. **He asked rather than told, which is to his credit in 1.5 and costs him here.** Still first.
2. **Oracle** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** His moral and logistical influence became directive influence several sessions ago and has stayed there. He calls Lock-Step in when he needs him, tells the team not to discuss internal matters in front of the Destined, called Nightwing about the artifact knowing how Lock-Step would react, rescued a briefing somebody else was losing, and answered for the whole team when Nightwing asked how it was doing. In Session 14 he said the team should be resting more, and the team rested. In Session 15 his forecast set the room's reading of the situation, and he handed direction to someone else explicitly for the only time on record (*"I'll follow your lead"*).
   * **Session 16: \+7.0 → \+7.5 (+0.5).** The room turned to him for the plan, and after *"No plan. Not on this one,"* he supplied one: a teleport ambush to overwhelm one or two of the five quickly. The team adopted it with Edge's choice of target, which his module endorsed (*"Mind control bad, yeah"*). In the fight his Let's Go decided who stayed on their feet, and he moved it from Quasar-Force to Lock-Step at the moment the extraction needed it. **He takes second back from Quasar-Force on score, not on a tie-break.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Substantial and self-limited. His analysis sets the targets, his intelligence sets the plans, and he assigns roles; Session 13 produced the first two orders he ever gave inside a fight, both obeyed without discussion. His influence is strongest where it is technical and weakest where it is social, and he rarely spends it against a teammate. When he does, it works. In Session 14 he turned a personal invitation from Wonder Woman into a team invitation. In Session 15 he set the order of work at the Watchtower, kept the tower shield up and refused the tentacle against the de facto leader, handled the team's calls to the League, and cast the vote that brought in the Destined (*"I say get them involved"*). **He beat the de facto leader in direct disagreement three times in one session, without raising his voice.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** He shaped the plan without authoring it. His drones found the five spread through the warehouse searching crates, and his deduction that the progeny had not yet given them full access to their new powers set the team's expectations. His sneaky-entry proposal lost to Oracle's ambush. He corrected Lock-Step on the facts, to his face, and it cost him nothing.
4. **Edge** `+5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Influence within minutes of arriving.** Minutes after meeting the team he named the first target and the reason — Argent first, because a mind-controller *"can turn 5 vs 5 into 6 vs 4"* — and the plan was built around it. He also made the team answer the two questions it had skipped, a plan and a situation report. One adopted call is not standing, and it was ratified by Oracle's module rather than won in argument, which is why he starts here and not higher.
5. **Intrepid** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** *"Will go with any team decision."* He once offered to squeeze objects until they break as his contribution to a security discussion. Charming, and not persuasive. In Session 14 he stated his own preference, to stay behind, and reversed it four times in one conversation under one teammate's single word. In Session 15, asked for an idea, he said *"I got nothing."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.0.** Asked whether there was a plan: *"Nothing, I guess."* Asked by Lock-Step whether he would stay out if teleported clear, he said no, and stayed. **It is the first time on record that his answer to Lock-Step stood**, but it was a decision about himself, so it is scored in 1.12 rather than here.

### **3.3 Internal Trust / Reliance**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

*Not affection — who the team actually depends on.*

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** Everyone relies on him for something they cannot do without: healing, food, honest reporting, and the medic station holding. He is the only member whose absence would change every other member's day. Session 14 showed what his absence looks like without his being absent: held immobile at 3 Focus and unable to heal the casualty, he left the team to improvise a replacement inside an hour. In Session 15 the Kwa plan attached him to the scout because he is the one who keeps people on their feet, and on Day 56 the team planned around his percentages.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+9.0.** **The reliance ran the whole fight.** Every recovery in Session 16 went through him: Let's Go kept Quasar-Force conscious, then moved to Lock-Step for the extraction, and he healed both afterwards. He was also the only teammate Lock-Step pulled out mid-fight. Already at the top of the band.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** The team routes intelligence, analysis, tactical calls and evidence custody through him without discussion. Session 14 showed the reliance surviving his worst session: with him unconscious, Lock-Step spent Focus he did not have to wake him, because the team needed him working. **You do not spend your last resource waking somebody you can do without.** Session 15 closed the one recorded reservation — the tower is teleport-shielded, and the shield held against the Destined — and ran most of the team's outward-facing work through him. Lock-Step's instructions to him were requests for things only he could do.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.0.** The drones were the team's eyes before the door opened, and his deduction about the progeny's powers was the team's intelligence. In the fight he was down in the first exchange and out for the last four rounds, and the team kept him within range of Let's Go rather than let him go. The reliance is unchanged; his availability is scored in 4.1.
3. **Intrepid** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** Relied upon absolutely for exactly one thing: nobody worries about whether Intrepid will be where the damage is. In Session 14 his tools failed him for the first time — Crushing Grip did nothing against a force field, and a stun took him out of the last two rounds — and nobody blamed him, which is itself the reliance. In Session 15 he tailed the limousine by air without being asked twice.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.5.** He was where the damage was — 72 and 60 from Dragonborne, 37 and 43 from Blood Blade, and his Sturdy stolen — and he refused to be moved out of it. The reliance is exactly what it was.
4. **Lock-Step** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** Professionally indispensable and personally unreliable, and the team behaves accordingly: they hand him negotiations and then discuss him behind his back. **Oracle went around him to Nightwing once and would do it again.** The professional half has improved sharply since Session 13\. In Session 14 he became the only person other than Oracle who has ever restored another person's Health. In Session 15 the team relied on him to lead, and on Kwa that was the right bet. His personal reliability is still the problem: he slept through class, didn't pay attention in briefings, livestreamed without asking and linked two teammates to a hive mind, and part of the team's security came to rest on a favour he owes to villains.
   * **Session 16: \+4.5 → \+5.5 (+1.0).** **The team's way out was him, and it worked.** He moved Oracle into Argent's sphere, carried the unconscious Oracle out of the building in Round 2, offered Intrepid an exit and respected the refusal. In the hand-waved ending he got all four teammates out: Oracle and Quasar-Force while Edge held off Blitzkrieg, then Edge, then a last trip back for Intrepid on almost the last of his Focus. **The team relied on him for the way out, and he delivered all of it.** The personal half did not change: two of his pre-fight claims were wrong, and the Destined favour is still outstanding.
5. **Edge** `+5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** The team had known him for minutes, and by the end it was relying on him for the exit. He held off Blitzkrieg alone for a round so the extraction could happen, assisted two of Intrepid's attacks — turning one of them from a miss into a Marvel — and destroyed the device that was the brood's objective. **The trust was earned in one fight and has not yet been tested by anything else** — a briefing, a disagreement, or a quiet week.

### **3.4 Team Cohesion Contribution**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

1. **Oracle** `+9.0`
   * **All-time.** He is the reason this is a household rather than a group of people in a building. Breakfast is timed around who wakes when and adjusted on request, and the medic station is also the place people end up talking. In Session 14, in a room full of people blaming a Justice Leaguer, he was the one who said out loud that she had admitted the mistake was hers, and that this counted for something. In Session 15 he supplied the session's one moment of pure levity: *"I'm Batman\! Wait, no, I'm Batman\!"*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+9.0.** To a newcomer who had just been told the adults were all trash, his first words were *"Don't worry, the bar is low, it's alright."* After the fight he healed the teammates he had kept upright. The standing contribution is unchanged.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** Universally liked; he thanks people constantly and defuses rather than escalates. He absorbed the discovery that he had been drugged without turning it into a war. **He is the person nobody on this team wants to be in conflict with.** In Session 14 he spent that capital on the group for the first time, getting a personal sanctuary widened into a team invitation. In Session 15 he celebrated a teammate's honour with more enthusiasm than the teammate (*"Oh she called you a grey lantern\!"*), spent his own deduction to help Lock-Step recall the oath, built him two drones, and disagreed with the most socially dominant person on the team three times without a moment of friction.
   * **Session 16: \+7.0 → \+7.5 (+0.5).** **He drew out the most honest thing Lock-Step has ever said about himself.** Against a run of *"Adults just suck,"* he answered: *"I like being hopeful. I want to help people get their happy thoughts back. You are not keeping your happy thoughts anymore."* Lock-Step replied with the first explanation of his own cynicism on record — *"Once I get that back, I think I'll be a bright-eyed kid just like you… But until then, this is me"* — and Quasar-Force's answer to that was a yacht party. He also corrected Lock-Step's version of who had told him to fix things without any friction at all.
3. **Intrepid** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** No friction with anyone. He plays video games with Lock-Step, takes the hits, and never sulks about a decision going the other way. The only friction on his file came in Session 14 and was defensive: called a coward, he jabbed back at Lock-Step for always teleporting out of combat, retreated into *"he was just joking,"* and went anyway, to protect people. In Session 15 he laughed at the jokes, backed the scout and caused no friction. His cohesion is passive and reliable.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+5.0.** He agreed with Lock-Step's welcome (*"Yeah"*), caused no friction, and declined an exit without making it an argument.
4. **Edge** `+4.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A good first afternoon. He met a complaint about adults with tact (*"I don't want to break your hope," "I'm sorry to hear that"*), asked for a plan and a situation report without making anyone look bad for lacking one, and gave Oracle's apocalyptic briefing the reply it deserved without starting anything. In the fight he backed two of a teammate's attacks, turned one of them from a miss into a Marvel, and covered everyone's exit. **No friction, and nothing yet that binds the group**; one afternoon is not a household.
5. **Lock-Step** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** **The team's largest source of both morale and conflict.** He brings the popcorn, the licorice, the parties and the video games, and he got everybody paid. In Session 14 he healed the casualty when nobody else could, and the same evening escalated a teammate's private self-criticism into a challenge to his manhood in front of an outside authority. Session 15 was mostly morale — he publicly elevated Intrepid, built a channel for every member, held a team huddle and called for *"a group discussion"* for the first time — against talking over Oracle on a stream, using Quasar-Force as content, pressing a bodily intrusion, linking two teammates' minds, and bringing the Destined, who have embarrassed this team before, into the tower as allies with a debt attached. **The morale he generates is real and has always come with a bill, and part of the bill is now owed to villains.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.5.** Both columns grew. **For:** a warm welcome to the newcomer, the first explanation of himself he has given a teammate, a consent question in the middle of a fight, and the extraction. **Against:** the newcomer's first minute on the team was spent hearing that *"all the adults are trash,"* that Iron-Side *"lived in the security room,"* and a version of the team's history that Quasar-Force had to correct. Mid-fight he blamed the team for his own position (*"This is what happens when I stay with the team. I should have vanished"*). They balance.

### **3.5 Low Operational Support Burden**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

*What the team spends to keep this person in the field: gear, medical draw, money, transport, cover maintenance, and consequence cleanup. **Not** how sustainably they occupy the role (1.3) and **not** how stable their private life is (1.7) — this is the squad's logistics bill. High \= cheap to field, or a net contributor.*

1. **Intrepid** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** He costs almost nothing. He absorbs the worst punishment on the team and then repairs it himself, with no technology, no artifacts, no patrons and no consumables, and his Secret Identity maintains itself by simply not being spent. **He is the cheapest sustained combat capability this team fields.** The Rank 3.5 advance (Session 15\) gave him 210 Health, H-DR 3, F-DR 1 and Healing Factor 7, and with Exemplar II gone he became the cheapest member outright.
   * **Session 16: \+7.0 → \+6.5 (−0.5).** **This time, he cost an extraction.** He went down twice, the second time to −49, and the hand-waved ending says Blitzkrieg could not carry him out; Lock-Step went back for him on almost the last of his Focus. His healing factor did the rest. He is still first, by half a point.
2. **Edge** `+6.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The second-cheapest member on arrival.** He heals himself — he came back from exactly 0 on his own healing factor — uses no technology, and his iconic weapon is Constructible, so the team keeps no armoury for him. His training is paid for by Black Canary and his recruitment was done by Nightwing. Against that, he is fifteen with a School obligation and grades that were *"already bad,"* he lives in the tower, and his legend is a cleanup item nobody has priced. **Cheap to field; not yet a net contributor.**
3. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** Low draw and real overhead. He is the medic, so he never consumes medical support, and he heals himself. Against that, he has **no mobility power at all**, his cybernetics are a STAR Labs maintenance dependency he did not build, and Extreme Appearance means errands that need an unremarkable face fall to someone else. Session 14 exposed what the cost structure hides: drained to 3 Focus, he needed twelve hours of dedicated sleep to reach 75 of 180, and for that whole period the team had no recovery capability of any kind. **The bill for Oracle is rarely money and never medicine. It is time, and it arrives all at once.** In Session 15 the module failed under temporal transport and recovered without intervention.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.5.** He was knocked out by 72 physical damage, was up a round later on his own healing factor, and finished the fight with enough left to heal two teammates. The time bill was not presented. The mobility bill was: Lock-Step moved him into the fight's key position and later carried him out of the building.
4. **Lock-Step** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** **Cheap to field and expensive to keep.** He supplies his own transport, summons his own weapon, has no address to maintain and no civilian identity that needs cover. The bill arrives elsewhere. At 60 Health with H-DR 0 (1 under the Hex) he is a heavy medical draw whenever anything lands, and his conduct generates cleanup the team pays for. Session 15 made him more expensive to keep than he had ever been: a favour owed to the Destined, publicity the others have to live with, posts shaming five teenagers, and an unchanged medical draw. **He saved the team a fight on Kwa and ran up a tab at home.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.0.** The draw arrived exactly as described. One glitch bomb took him from 60 to −24, and Oracle healed him back up. He then paid in more than anyone, extracting all four teammates. The Destined favour is still outstanding, and the five teenagers he published are now three unconscious in a mob warehouse and two at large.
5. **Quasar-Force** `−2.5`
   * **All-time.** The largest medical draw on the roster: 60 Health and no healing of his own after fifteen sessions. The −12 in Session 14, from full Health in a single action, cost a surgical extraction, an overnight watch, most of Lock-Step's remaining Focus, a League medical robot, a sedative and twelve hours of mandated sleep. He needs a drone fleet with a charging rotation, laboratory access at three sites, and imposed structure to keep him eating and sleeping, and his public identity and legal exposure are standing costs. Session 15 ran the bill the other way (+1.5): Sturdy 3 and Uncanny 3 for damage reduction on both tracks, a signal mask so the team no longer relocates around him, a teleport shield for the Titans' floors, and two drones built for a teammate.
   * **Session 16: −2.0 → −2.5 (−0.5).** **The hardware did not stop the draw.** He went to −5 in the opening exchange, spent Karma to reach 13, and was kept conscious by Let's Go. Then a glitch bomb, which ignores damage reduction, put him at −30. Oracle put 15 Health into him mid-fight knowing it would not bring him back, kept him within range of Let's Go, and healed him to consciousness afterwards. **The medic spent more of the fight on him than on anyone else.**

*⟨Note⟩ The anchor is half-restored.* (The Session 15 edition's note on this axis was cut off mid-sentence; this replaces it.) Exemplar II, first here in Session 14, was the only net contributor this axis has had — self-funded, self-housed and self-healing — and the four he left behind are all costs, ordered by how large a cost each one is. Edge arrives second at \+6.0: self-healing and self-armed, with his training paid for by someone else, but housed by the team and fifteen. **He is cheap, not free.** And in Session 16 the cheapest member cost an extraction.

###

### **3.6 Command Chair — External Appointment**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*(Who Nightwing, Batman, Iron-Side, or the League would install.)*

1. **Oracle** `8.5`

   * **All-time.** The obvious appointment. He is sixteen, mature, medically indispensable and already Nightwing's most reliable channel. He will report honestly against a teammate's wishes and complies with imposed structure. He also carries **Precognition, precisely the power an appointing authority wants sitting in a field-leader's chair.** When Nightwing needed a Titan in a room with a STAR Labs CFO, he arranged it for Oracle, and in Session 14, when the operation failed, Oracle was the one who rang the client; appointing authorities select for people who report losses without being asked. Session 15 added his best forecast. ⟨Volatile⟩ A time traveller from Kwa looked at the team and saw Batman in him. That is not an appointment; it is recorded because another of her identifications, Lock-Step as a lantern, turned out to be literally true within the hour.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.5.** No authority appointed anybody. ⟨Note⟩ The one external act of Session 16 did not go through him: Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited the newcomer and briefed him on the five villains, and the team did not know. It reads as a recruiter briefing his recruit rather than a change of channel, and it is not scored.
2. **Quasar-Force** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Strong indicators and hard blockers. He is the historical primary Nightwing correspondent, the evidence custodian, the one who cites conduct policy from memory, and the team's point of contact with the League; when Lock-Step asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was, Wonder Woman pointed at him. The blockers have nothing to do with competence. He is fourteen and presents as eleven, an open fatality investigation is unresolved, and ⟨OOC⟩ an Apokoliptian-modified intelligence is bonded to his brain with a New God hunting it. Session 15 added a Grey Lantern calling him *"no longer merely human"* in front of the team, and his endorsement of lethal force by proxy on the record. **Being the person an institution protects is close to the opposite of being the person it puts in charge.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** Nothing moved. A hostile team now opens on him first, which is a reason for institutions to protect him, not to install him.
3. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The only member an outside authority chose for anything this revision** — Nightwing, as Batman, recruited and briefed him — but the appointment was to the team, not to its chair. He is fifteen, an afternoon into the job, and has Logic −1. His mentor is Black Canary, whose opinion would carry weight with the League. He sits just above Intrepid on the strength of the briefing and the first-day call.
4. **Intrepid** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** Brave and reliable, with no command indicators: Logic 0, no communications role, and no questions when Nightwing invited them. In Session 14 he argued, in front of the sponsoring Justice Leaguer, that nothing could have been done better. A time traveller's Superman reading in Session 15 is a matter of fame, scored in §8, not of anyone's appointment.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.5.** Nothing moved.
5. **Lock-Step** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** He has told Iron-Side to her face that her school is fake, told the room that Nightwing can't be trusted with their security, tried to conceal a dangerous artifact from him and sedated a minor teammate. In Session 14 he named the organisational failure a League founder then conceded, and he was also put in the Lasso of Truth by that founder. In Session 15 an external order did appoint him — to the succession of a Grey Lantern, the first time an outside order has chosen him for anything — but none of the authorities this axis lists would do the same. Iron-Side pinched him for not listening in briefings, the Watchtower was livestreamed without anyone being asked, and the person who praised his leadership was Fade-Out. **The one institution that chose him is not one his mentors can see.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** His opening statement to the newcomer was a case against every authority on this list: the adults are *"trash,"* Iron-Side *"lived in the security room,"* the League should be helping, and Nightwing *"didn't even fill him in"* — which was wrong. Nothing in it would change an appointing authority's mind, and nothing he did in the fight would reach one.

### **3.7 Command Chair — Internal Emergence**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*(Who the team converges on without anyone appointing anybody. Iron-Side deliberately declines to appoint a leader or impose a chain of command, so this path is currently the live one.)*

1. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **Emergence follows initiative, and nobody else on this team reliably supplies it.** Even inside the Justice League's station in Session 14 he was the one assigning work and allocating people. His authority has a known weakness: it decays with absence, as it did across Sessions 12 and 13\. In Session 15 the team converged on him from the first minute to the last — the approach on Kwa, the Watchtower huddle and stream, and on Day 56 the host who put the question to Iron-Side, brought in the Destined and split the work — with Oracle's *"I'll follow your lead"* and Fade-Out's *"About time you stepped up the leadership."* Under pressure he governs by decree (*"we don't have the time to be democratic"*), and the one teammate who argued with him on the merits won all three times.
   * **Session 16: 8.5 → 8.0 (−0.5).** **He handed the plan to Oracle in front of the newcomer**: *"Oracle is our plan guy. I'm just the if it all goes to hell… that's my element."* What he kept was the part he named. Once it went to hell he ran it: the cuffs offered twice, Oracle moved into the sphere, the consent question (*"Who is comfortable where they are at and who wants to move a little bit"*), and the whole extraction. **Still the team's emergent leader in a crisis, and by his own description no longer its planner.**
2. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** The default in a crisis. He is the one who says surrender first, holds the line at the medic station and shuts down loose talk in front of enemies. Across two sessions without a leader in the room he simply *was* the team lead, and for four revisions this document said that if Lock-Step drifts, Oracle inherits. His emergence has always been by gravity. Session 15 was the first session in which he explicitly handed the lead to someone else. ⟨OOC⟩ The player was overloaded.
   * **Session 16: 6.5 → 7.0 (+0.5).** The room turned to him for the plan, by name, and he supplied one after first saying there wasn't one. His module ratified the newcomer's target, his opening shot executed it, and his Let's Go decided who was still standing at the end. **Nobody handed him the chair, but Lock-Step pointed at it.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** In practice he sets objectives, deconflicts targets, manages the operational picture and holds the institutional memory of the conduct standard. His emergence is functional rather than social: the room follows his analysis without treating him as its leader. Session 13 was the first session in which his habit of hedging cracked. In Session 15 he passed Oracle: he set the order of work at the Watchtower, ran every external communication and made the security calls, and each time one of those collided with the leader's preference his view prevailed. He was still positioned as the little brother (*"Is she going to listen to you? Like no offense? We're like kids"*).
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** He did the intelligence work — the drone sweep and the deduction about the progeny's powers — and proposed a sneaky entry that the team did not take. Then he was the brood's first target and spent most of the fight down.
4. **Edge** `4.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** On his first afternoon he asked the room for a plan, got *"No plan,"* and supplied the target. **The newcomer's call became the plan within minutes of his arrival**, ratified by Oracle's module. One call is not emergence, and he directed nobody in the fight; four is the credit for asking the right question and answering it.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`

   * **All-time.** The roster notes that he tries to lead and is not always the right man for it. The attempts are visible and they don't land. In Session 14 he reversed his own stated position four times while somebody else's opinion did all the work, and in Session 15 he said *"I'm not really a thinker."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** *"Nothing, I guess."*

*Closest call:* Oracle over Quasar-Force on a tie at `7.0`, broken on Session 16 evidence: the room turned to Oracle for the plan.

### **3.8 Command Chair — Overall**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*(Weighted toward emergence, since no appointment currently exists.)*

1. **Oracle** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** The only member with no hard blocker on either path: first on appointment, and first in practice in every session the incumbent missed. At Session 14 his lead over second place was the third-widest first-to-second gap in the document. Session 15 took 0.2 off on the emergence figure alone, with Nightwing and Batman absent for a second straight session and Iron-Side and Wonder Woman present and appointing nobody.
   * **Session 16: 8.0 → 8.2 (+0.2).** The emergence figure recovered: the team's emergent leader pointed at him for the plan, and the plan was his.
2. **Lock-Step** `5.1`
   * **All-time.** Dominant on emergence and disqualified on appointment. He holds the chair in all but name, and he remains the least likely person on the roster to be handed it. Session 15 was the first session in which holding the chair produced a result nobody else on the team could have produced (Kwa) and a decision nobody else would have made (the Destined).
   * **Session 16: 5.2 → 5.1 (−0.1).** The tenth is the plan he handed over. He keeps second because in a crisis the room still moves when he does.
3. **Quasar-Force** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Top-three on both paths and blocked on both for reasons that have nothing to do with capability: his age and presentation, the open fatality investigation, and ⟨OOC⟩ the box a New God is hunting. **He is the most likely person to be *running* the team while someone else *leads* it**, and Session 15 was the literal case.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** Nothing moved.
4. **Edge** `2.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Third on appointment, fourth on emergence, and an afternoon into the job. The early signs are good — the right question, the right target, and a recruiter who briefed him personally — and there is not yet a record to weigh them against.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** Neither path is open to him, and he has not asked for either.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** Nothing moved.

*Volatile:* Lock-Step over Quasar-Force by 0.1. **This is the third consecutive session without Nightwing on screen, and in this one he acted on the team from offscreen.** Operating as Batman, he recruited the newcomer and briefed him on the five villains, and the team did not know. After the fight the team briefed Wonder Woman, as it did in Session 15\. ⟨Note⟩ The user's reading that Wonder Woman is becoming the team's mentor is still not GM-confirmed, and this document treats it as practice rather than declaration. **Two external authorities are now acting on this team, one through the front door and one through the newcomer, and neither has formalised a chain of command.**

### **3.9 Overall Pecking Order**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

1. **Oracle** `8.7`
   * **All-time.** The highest reputation, moral authority, indispensable support, and a willingness to overrule that has stopped being occasional. Nightwing routes through him, and the room defers to him when it is not arguing. Session 14 added two quiet markers: he made the failure call, and the youngest member asked for him by name. Session 15 added a public jab and an explicit deferral, and removed a distinction: he is no longer the only person on this team who has successfully told Lock-Step no.
   * **Session 16: 8.5 → 8.7 (+0.2).** The jab became a job title — *"Oracle is our plan guy"* — from the person whose standing is closest to his, in front of the newcomer. He then took the hardest single hit of the fight, came back with *"Drop me again, it was worth it,"* and decided who was conscious at the end. **The gap to second widens from a tenth to three tenths.**
2. **Lock-Step** `8.4`
   * **All-time.** The highest raw influence on the team. He sets group direction, treats others' consent as negotiable and is not meaningfully checked for it. The ceiling has always been that the adults manage him rather than listen to him. Session 15 closed him to a tenth of first: he ran the session, carries a ring and is the team's public voice, while adults managed him (the pinch on the neck), a villain praised him, and a fourteen-year-old overruled him three times.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.4.** He ceded the plan and kept the crisis, and for the first time he asked a teammate before moving him, instead of treating consent as negotiable. **The standing is unchanged. The description of it — *"I'm just the if it all goes to hell"* — is his own, and it is new.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.2`
   * **All-time.** Indispensable capability and the team's actual brain, socially positioned as the little brother. Session 14 pushed both halves at once: a founding member of the Justice League offered him sanctuary, and he was carried home unconscious and summarised as *"a Quasar-Force type of thing."* In Session 15 he won all three of his disagreements with the leader, ran most of the team's external relationships and came home with three new pieces of hardware, while the room still talked about him as *"our smarts"* and asked whether he was human or mother box. **Value very high; status capped by the way the room talks to him.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.2.** The cap was stated to the newcomer in so many words — *"that kid that looks like he is like 12"* — and he answered it himself, on the facts, without heat.
4. **Edge** `4.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Ahead of Intrepid on his first afternoon.** He was greeted as *"somebody competent"* before he had done anything, and by the end of the fight his call had set the plan, his shot had ended the brood's objective, and his round alone against Blitzkrieg had made the extraction possible. Standing is not built in an afternoon, and he has not yet been in a room where the team disagreed.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Beloved and essential in the line, and weightless in decisions. The only decision he made in Session 14 was reversed four times in one conversation, under one teammate's single word. In Session 15 he was publicly elevated (*"I will never doubt you again"*) and, ten days later, said *"I got nothing."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** The first decision on record that he held against Lock-Step — staying in the fight — was about himself. It counts in 1.12. It does not yet count here.

## **4\. Combat Power / Ability / Value**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

***A note on this section this revision.*** **Session 16 is a full fight, seven rounds and a hand-waved ending, and the hardest the team has taken.** It is the first combat since Session 14, and every member went to 0 Health or below at least once:

- **Quasar-Force** twice: −5 in the opening exchange, kept up by Let's Go, and −30 in Round 3\.
- **Oracle** once: −2 in Round 2, back up on his healing factor the next round.
- **Intrepid** twice: −3 in Round 4, back up on his healing factor the same round, and −49 in Round 6\.
- **Lock-Step** once: one-shot from 60 to −24 in Round 4\.
- **Edge** once: exactly 0 in Round 7, back up on his healing factor.

The opposition was five teenage villains carrying the Breeder's brood, under a controller's orders and fighting with what the log calls *"outstanding teamwork"*:

- **Blitzkrieg:** phasing, teleporting, and glitch bombs that act as a ranged Disrupt Person and ignore damage reduction.
- **Argent:** telekinetic force fields, telepathy and some mind control.
- **Stote:** heightened senses, wall-crawling and electricity.
- **Blood Blade:** a magnetite weapon, explosive energy, and **Swipe**, which steals a power on a hit.
- **Dragonborne:** a flying brick with a damage-reflecting Counterstrike.

Argent, Stote and Blood Blade were knocked out and left in the warehouse. Dragonborne was demoralised and fled, Blitzkrieg withdrew on orders, and the device the brood came for was destroyed. No sheet changed except by Edge's arrival, so every Session 16 movement in this section comes from the fight. It was also the first test of several things this document has been carrying:

- **The Session 15 hardware.** Quasar-Force's fortified mind turned away Swipe; his Sturdy 3 was irrelevant against a glitch bomb.
- **The Session 14 counter-build against Oracle** (grab and drain) was not used. The brood beat him another way: 72 physical damage, and a reaction that turned his Return Fire onto a teammate.
- **The healing backup** (Lock-Step's hex-heal) was not needed. The monopoly carried the fight.
- **The Force whip** was never swung at a phaser. The hand-waved ending credits it as one reason Blitzkrieg withdrew. ⟨Volatile⟩

### **4.1 Durability, Defense & Sustain — Health / Physical**

1. **Intrepid** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Best in every column: the largest Health pool on the team (210 since the Rank 3.5 advance), H-DR 3, physical defences of 20/20/20, a Healing Factor that returns 7 Health every turn, Immovable and Enduring Constitution. In Session 14 he took a Marvel-boosted area attack from an Olympian goddess in the chest and was upright the following round. **He is the only member who can absorb a bad round and simply keep standing.**
   * **Session 16: 9.8 → 9.5 (−0.3).** **290 damage by the log's count, more than his whole Health pool.** Dragonborne landed Marvels for 72 and 60, Blood Blade hit for 19, 17, 37 and 43, Blitzkrieg for 28, and Counterstrike returned 14 more. Swipe took his Sturdy 3 for two rounds, removing his damage reduction and the house-rule boost to his defences. He went down twice: to −3, up again on his own healing factor in the same round, and then to −49. He is still first by a distance. The three tenths are for the first fight in which the best body on the team was not enough to stay standing.
2. **Edge** `6.2`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** 90 Health, H-DR 2, physical defences of 19/19/15 from Evasion and Sturdy 2, a Healing Factor of 3, and **Slow-Motion Dodge**, a free reaction that puts trouble on any attack against his Agility defence. It worked three times in four: it evaded Argent's push-back, stripped the edge from a Dragonborne Marvel, and with a Karma point made a glitch bomb miss; it failed once, against Stote. He took 102 damage across the fight — 22 from Stote and glitch bombs for 37 and 43 that ignored his damage reduction — went down at exactly 0, and got up on his healing factor. **Ahead of Oracle's identical 90-point pool on two more points of physical defence and a dodge that works.**
3. **Oracle** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** 90 Health looks fragile and isn't. **Real Jinx imposes trouble on every direct attack against him, permanently**, stacked with H-DR 3, defences of 17/17/16, Charmed Life, Danger Sense, a Healing Factor of 3 a turn, and Healing Hands, which converts his own Focus into his own Health one for one. In Session 14 Ecliptica held him for most of the fight and never attacked his body: his physical durability was not beaten, it was declined. ⟨Flag⟩ His two Rank 3.5 advances are undeclared, and anything they bought is not scored.
   * **Session 16: 6.5 → 6.0 (−0.5).** **This time it was tested, and it lost.** Dragonborne hit him for 28 in Round 1 and then, teleporting into the middle of the team, landed a Marvel Banging Heads for 72\. Real Jinx put trouble on both, and neither missed. He was down at −2. His healing factor got him up the next round, and he ran back into the warehouse on a sliver of Health. The ending was good, but it came from his healing, not his defences.
4. **Lock-Step** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Feast or famine. Protection Hex routes everything to Ego 20, which is functionally unhittable against most opposition. When something does land, 60 Health with H-DR 0 (1 under the Hex) evaporates: Omega Grade took 75% in a single attack, and Dragonborne once left him at 10\. His Healing Factor at Resilience 2 barely helps. **His best armour is discipline**, and in Session 14 he teleported out after every strike and finished the fight untouched. Melee 7 (Session 15\) lifted his Melee defence to 17 without changing the famine.
   * **Session 16: 5.0 → 4.5 (−0.5).** **The famine, in one roll.** He had done what his discipline says — struck and teleported clear — and was outside the warehouse when Blitzkrieg teleported out after him and threw a glitch bomb that ignores damage reduction. It rolled a Marvel-20 before edges and took him from 60 to −24 in a single attack, the worst single hit on his file. A teleporting phaser is the one opponent his discipline cannot outrun. Earlier, Dragonborne had also turned Oracle's Return Fire onto him for 13 Focus.
5. **Quasar-Force** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** 60 Health, no healing factor and no self-heal. He has finished sessions at 1, 4, −11 and −12 Health — the last from completely undamaged, in a single action, from an attacker nobody could locate. **There is no version of this axis where a character who can be removed from full to unconscious by one strike is not last.** What keeps him alive is a high Melee and Agility defence with Evasion, and the ability to simply not be within reach. Session 15 put Sturdy 3 in the nanosuit, for H-DR 3 and physical defences of 19/19/15.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** **Neither knockdown was decided by the Session 15 upgrade.** The brood opened on him. Dragonborne missed against *"great agility and augmented nanosuit,"* and Blood Blade landed a Marvel for 38 and a reaction strike for 27 through his damage reduction, to −5. Kept up by Let's Go, he spent Karma to heal 18, went behind a shipping container and kept working. In Round 3 Blitzkrieg's glitch bomb, which ignores damage reduction, put him at −30, the deepest knockdown on his file; the log adds that *"Karma would be of no help, he hits before his edge."*

*Roadmap note:* ⟨Ruling⟩ Quasar-Force spent Rank 3.5 on Iconic Items, and the roster's house rule bans telekinetic or elemental protection alongside more than two levels of Sturdy, so with Sturdy 3 in the nanosuit Telekinetic Protection is closed to him. **Session 16 is the case for the other route.** His first knockdown came after damage reduction had done its work, and his second from an attack that ignores it. Energy Absorption, on the Rank 3→4 plan, is a reaction that converts Health damage — *after* damage reduction — into Focus, so it answers both. It remains the single highest-leverage purchase on the team's board.

### **4.2 Durability, Defense & Sustain — Focus / Mental**

*Focus pool, Focus damage reduction, mental defences, and demonstrated resistance to psychic attack.*

1. **Lock-Step** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The best demonstrated mental defence on the team. His Ego defence is 20 at baseline, and **Protection Hex routes every incoming attack and effect through it**, so there is no soft angle to attack. His F-DR 3 is tied for the highest on the team. Argent's mind control failed against him twice, and Dargrave's mind-read failed outright. The one crack is a 90 Focus pool that Omega Grade showed can be drained fast — and, since Session 15, the first logged entry past the Hex: the progenitor entered his mind *"with ease even with his protection hex up."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** Argent was in the fight and made no recorded attempt on him; she was out inside the first round. The only Focus damage he took came from his own side — Oracle's Return Fire, redirected onto him by Dragonborne for 13\.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.8`
   * **All-time.** A 180 Focus pool, tied for the largest on the roster, and a record that cuts both ways: Viral catalogued his mind as very resilient after failing to get in, while Psyche made contact at will across Sessions 12 and 13\. The Session 14 image was a big house with an unlocked door. Session 15 put a lock on it: the circlet's Uncanny 3 gives **Vigilance 18, Ego 20 via Integrity, Logic 20 and Focus damage reduction 3**, with *"mental defense fortification / augmentation to make it harder for enemy telepaths to mess with his mind,"* at no Focus or action cost.
   * **Session 16: 8.5 → 8.8 (+0.3).** **The lock held on its first test.** Blood Blade's Swipe, a power theft that rides on a hit, *"fails against his fortified mind."* Later in the fight the same power stripped Intrepid's Sturdy. One test is not a record, which is why Lock-Step keeps first. *Closest call:* Lock-Step over Quasar-Force by 0.2, on demonstrated record against a specification that has now passed once.
3. **Edge** `5.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A middle profile: Focus 90, F-DR 2 from Uncanny 2, and mental defences of Vigilance 15, Ego 16 and Logic 16, with Wisdom covering his Logic of −1. Nothing in Session 16 attacked his mind. He has damage reduction on the track where the two members below him have none or one; he has neither the Hex nor the circlet of the two above.
4. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** The most lopsided profile in the section: Focus 180 and Vigilance 16 against **Ego defence 10** and F-DR 0\. Session 14 emptied him without using the soft door — Ecliptica's elemental grab, four brain drains, 180 Focus down to 3\. **F-DR 0 against a repeating drain is an open tap.** In Session 15 a telepath got in without visible effort (*"then you come to hunt us. I see it in your mind"*). His Ego defence is ten points below the two members at the top.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.0.** Nobody attacked his mind or drained his Focus. The counter-build was not used; the brood went through his body instead (4.1). ⟨Flag⟩ Two advances are undeclared, and Uncanny would address this directly.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 11 and Logic 11 are the weakest pair of mental defences on the team, partly offset by Iron Will and Fearless, which are real mechanical resistances, and F-DR 1\. He survives mental pressure by being stubborn rather than defended. In Session 14 an area attack stunned him for two consecutive rounds, and in Session 15 the progenitor pulled him into its network with a 14-Marvel against his Vigilance of 14: *"neither can kick it out of their minds for the rest of the day."*
   * **Session 16: 3.0 → 2.5 (−0.5).** **The first power ever taken from a Titan was his.** The Swipe that Quasar-Force's fortified mind turned away succeeded on him and stole Sturdy 3, and Blood Blade fought the next two rounds with his damage reduction. That is two sessions and two hostile powers getting in through the same side.

### **4.3 Stamina & Resource Efficiency**

1. **Intrepid** `9.2`
   * **All-time.** 90 Focus and almost nothing to spend it on. Clobber, Banging Heads, Crushing Grip and Immovable are free; only Smash and Ground-Shaking Stomp cost. His Healing Factor returns 7 Health a turn against a 210-Health pool. He can fight all day, and does.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.2.** His healing factor took him from −3 to \+4 in the same round, and he swung again; later it worked on him from −49 while he lay unconscious. Focus was never the constraint.
2. **Edge** `7.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The cheapest kit on the team to run.** Suppressive Fire, Double Tap and Slow-Motion Dodge cost nothing, and only Stopping Power costs Focus, at 5\. On a 90 Focus pool with a healing factor, he can shoot every round of any fight the team has had. Session 16 was seven rounds of exactly that: three Suppressive Fires, three Stopping Powers, and a dodge whenever one was needed. Second on arrival, behind a man with 210 Health and seven a turn.
3. **Quasar-Force** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** The largest Focus pool on the team at 180, driving a cheap core loop (Grab 5, Attack 5, Barrier 5, Crush 10). **His limiting resource isn't Focus, it's Health.** Session 14 was the purest demonstration, ending with a nearly full Focus pool and −12 Health. Session 15's F-DR 3 and Sturdy 3 protected both pools a little more. Energy Absorption, on the Rank 3→4 plan, would transform this axis outright.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.5.** The demonstration ran again: knocked out twice, never short of Focus.
4. **Oracle** `5.0`
