   * **All-time.** 180 Focus, and he is the team's battery: every point of Healing Hands is a point of Focus, spent alongside Let's Go, Never Surrender, Return Fire and Precognition. In Session 9 he deliberately under-healed a teammate to keep reserves for a raid. Session 14 took the decision away from him: drained to 3 of 180 by an enemy reaction he could not escape, he could not restore a teammate at −12, and twelve hours of sleep returned him to 75\. **An opponent worked out that draining him is worth more than hitting anyone.**
   * **Session 16: 4.5 → 5.0 (+0.5).** **The battery lasted the fight.** He fired Suppressive Fire, which costs nothing, in seven of his eight turns. He ran Let's Go on Quasar-Force twice — from −5 in the opening exchange, and from Round 4 to the end — put 15 Health into him mid-fight, and moved Let's Go to Lock-Step for the extraction. After the fight he still had enough to heal Lock-Step twice and Quasar-Force to consciousness. Nobody drained him, so the counter is untested. What Session 16 showed is the budget holding under a medical load that put every member of the team below zero.
5. **Lock-Step** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** A 90-point pool, tied for the shallowest, against the hungriest demands: Protection Hex at 10, Powerful Hex at base cost plus 5, Chain Strikes at 5, and teleports at 10 to 15\. F-DR 3 helps, but he is a sprinter. In Session 14 he ran himself dry on purpose, on somebody else's behalf. His ring has a charge he cannot yet refill — a second shallow pool rather than a deeper one.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** **The sprint, start to finish.** Blink, a Powerful Hex for Teleport Together, Chain Strikes in three different rounds, a teleport out with Oracle and a teleport back. After the fight Oracle had to heal him before he could teleport the others, and the last trip, for Intrepid, used *"just about the last of his focus."* The pool was exactly as deep as the job and not a point deeper.

###

### **4.4 Crowd Control & Area Denial**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The most flexible controller by a distance. Telekinetic Barrier spans two spaces per rank, absorbs anything doing 10 or less indefinitely, and lets him choose which side of it a target ends up on. Telekinetic Grab has a **break-out target number of 20**, which most opponents cannot roll. He blocks doorways, escapes and lines of fire, and funnels enemies into the front line. Session 14 showed the ceiling: a barrier on a display case was beaten by a teleport gate opened *inside* the case. His answer, the tower shield (Session 15), is scored in 4.6.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** From behind a shipping container on the far side of the warehouse, he grabbed Argent through a force field she raised against it, and she failed to break out with her own telekinetic strength. Later he pinned Dragonborne, who spent his last reaction of the round to escape. **The grab held the brood's first target in place until Lock-Step finished her.** He was then down for the last four rounds, and nobody else on the team controls anything.
2. **Lock-Step** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Broad and inconsistent: ice control, paralysis riders on chain-whip hits, a Powerful Hex sleep effect that took Aegis-Girl out of a fight entirely, and Teleport Other for removing enemies or extracting allies. In Session 14 the whip's rider stunned Ecliptica and broke her grab on Oracle. The output depends on the hex configuration and the die.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.5.** He spent his hexes on movement rather than control; the one Powerful Hex of the fight put Oracle inside Argent's sphere. No rider effects are recorded.
3. **Intrepid** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Denial by physics. Ground-Shaking Stomp prones everything within reach plus rank on a Fantastic, Banging Heads takes two, Crushing Grip holds one, and Immovable makes him a wall that cannot be repositioned.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.5.** Banging Heads took two at once and collapsed Argent's force field. He stayed in place when Argent's telekinetic sphere pushed Oracle out. His one Stomp reached only Dragonborne.
4. **Oracle** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Zone control by threat rather than by physics. Suppressive Fire stuns on a Fantastic, Return Fire punishes anyone who declares against him, and his held overwatch actions have frozen reinforcements in a hallway. Session 14 was the counter-case: a man who denies ground by being dangerous to approach is worth very little once he has been grabbed.
   * **Session 16: 4.5 → 5.0 (+0.5).** **The stuns landed.** His opening Suppressive Fire on Argent became a super-Marvel (6-1-6) through Charmed Life, for massive Focus damage and a stun. Dragonborne was stunned twice more, by a Marvel for 34 in Round 3 and by 17 in Round 5, and in Round 7, at 11 Focus, a 15-point hit demoralised him and he fled. **Suppressive Fire removed the brood's strongest body from the fight without beating its Health.**
5. **Edge** `4.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** He carries the same Suppressive Fire at ×7 rather than ×4, so his stuns would come with far heavier Focus damage behind them, but he has no other control: no grab, no barrier and no area effect. In play his Suppressive Fire did 49, 27 and 31 Focus, and none of it rolled the Fantastic that stuns. Last on arrival, a step behind the man whose power he shares.

### **4.5 Grappling & Restraint**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **Telekinetic Grab's break-out target of 20** makes it soft removal rather than a hindrance. He has held Aegis-Girl, held White-Out pinned while himself at −11 Health, held Argent until she surrendered, and held Stote through repeated escape attempts. Session 14 added contested *objects*: the Diadem of Selene and the Aegis Fang moved out of the thieves' reach, and the Sphere of Harmonia taken out of Ecliptica's hand. He also cuffs people at range, once including a sleeping target without waking him.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** The same two opponents again, and the same result. He grabbed Argent through a re-raised force field and held her against her own telekinetic break-out, and he grabbed Stote and crushed him unconscious for 35\. Dragonborne was pinned and escaped, and the crush on him did nothing. **The brood said in the fight that grappling was why it went for him first** (2.2).
2. **Lock-Step** `6.7`
   * **All-time.** The handcuff ritual is restraint as doctrine, and it works: Nova and Ghost-Ape cuffed themselves voluntarily. His chain-whip paralysis riders have landed repeatedly, and in Session 14 one broke a goddess's hold on a teammate. Melee 7 (Session 15\) adds to both halves of the axis: holding someone, and getting out of being held.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.7.** He offered the cuffs twice. **Argent started to accept** before her controller reasserted itself, and Dragonborne called it *"a good idea, but something inside us is telling us not to."* The doctrine reached the people and could not reach the thing commanding them. He cuffed Argent once she was unconscious.
3. **Intrepid** `6.3`
   * **All-time.** Crushing Grip at ×7 \+7 since the Rank 3.5 advance, single-target and without finesse. For twelve sessions nothing got out. Then it failed twice: Umbra escaped with ease in Session 13, and in Session 14 he grabbed Ecliptica cleanly and **could not overcome her force field.** The grab still works; the thing it is meant to do afterwards has failed against both opponents above street tier the team has met.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.3.** He grabbed nobody. He did collapse Argent's force field with Banging Heads, which is the answer to the Session 14 failure that his grab never found.
4. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A marksman with no grapple tools and Melee 0\. On the other half of the axis, his Melee defence of 19 makes him hard to grab, and nothing in his kit would break a hold once one landed. Just above Oracle on that defence.
5. **Oracle** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** A ranged attacker with no grapple tools; Suppressive Fire pins a position, not a person. Session 14 scored the other half of the axis. Ecliptica grabbed him in round three, lost the grab to a teammate's stun, grabbed him again in round four, and still had him when she left: *"Oracle has no ego or logic based powers that project strength so he only has his melee of −1 to use for escaping the grab."* The house rule that lets a mental ability substitute for Melee on an escape is the most favourable ruling in the campaign for a character like him, **and he owns nothing it can apply to.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.5.** Nobody grabbed him. ⟨Flag⟩ **Two advances have been banked since Session 14 and are still undeclared, for a third session.** If one bought an ego- or logic-based power that projects strength, this score is wrong by several points and the counter-build finding in §10.4 closes.

###

### **4.6 Objective Control**

*Taking and holding ground, protecting a point, denying a location.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** Barriers spanning two spaces per rank, placed at will, absorbing anything that does 10 or less. **Nobody else on the team can create terrain.** In Session 14 the objective was three portable artifacts, and his method — pick the objective up and put it where the enemy is not — was the correct answer to a theft; Phase Object beat it. ⟨Note⟩ The only artifact the team retained was the one buried in his abdomen. Session 15 added **the first denial capability on this team that works while nobody is standing on it**: a tower shield that *"will learn and adapt other teleportation methodologies,"* which denied the Titans' floors to the Destined in play.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.3.** The objective was a device in a crate, and he was down before it surfaced. The shield is at the tower, not the warehouse.
2. **Intrepid** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **Immovable is objective control as a physical law.** He stands where the objective is, cannot be repositioned, and clears everything that comes near with Ground-Shaking Stomp. Session 14 was the case where it did not apply: the objectives were portable, and the enemies phased through floors.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** Argent's telekinetic sphere pushed Oracle out and did not move him. He held his ground until he went down, and refused to be moved off it even by his own side.
3. **Edge** `7.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **He decided the objective.** The brood came for a device. Dragonborne found it and threw its crate at him, the crate broke, and Blitzkrieg picked the device up and phased to the wall, one turn from leaving. Edge took a Stopping Power shot at it with trouble for disarming a hand-held item, hit, and destroyed it. At the end of the fight he held off Blitzkrieg alone for a round so that Lock-Step could get the others out. Denial at range, and a rear guard: third on arrival.
4. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Control by threat rather than by presence: held overwatch actions that froze reinforcements in a hallway, and Real Jinx making approach a losing proposition. In Session 12 he interposed against Star Heart to keep the Celestial Core's containment field standing. Session 14 reversed the polarity: for most of the fight the enemy made *him* the objective and held him in place.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** His Suppressive Fire took Dragonborne, the member of the brood who actually found the device, out of the fight by demoralising him. That is control of the objective by removing the person who wanted it, and it is scored in 4.4 and 4.14.
5. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Superb at *contesting* objectives and poor at holding them. He arrives instantly anywhere and leaves the same way. Session 14 was four turns of in, strike, out against a target he could not finish. ⟨Note⟩ In Session 15 he proposed dropping the tower's new shield *"for a few seconds"* to let a villain team in; that is scored as judgement in 1.6.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** He held nothing and contested everything.

### **4.7 Reaction / Response Speed**

*Initiative, reactions, interrupts, and acting before the enemy does.*

1. **Oracle** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Not close. The module's Danger Sense guarantees him a turn in the bonus round of every combat, and he runs Combat Reflexes, Return Fire, Charmed Life and Precognition 1 — he acts on information the enemy has not generated yet. Session 14 was the best pure demonstration, even in a loss: the module gave him the lead to dodge an invisible attacker, his Return Fire went off as a reaction and hit her, and he still took his own action. ⟨Note⟩ In Session 15 the module *"did not adapt well"* to temporal transport, and recovered.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** He took the bonus round as always, and his first action of the fight was the super-Marvel stun on the agreed first target. **Session 16 also showed the first counter to his reactions.** When Return Fire triggered on Dragonborne's Banging Heads, Dragonborne spent his own reaction redirecting it onto Lock-Step. A reaction can be answered by a reaction.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Blink as a reaction negates an attack outright, and Quick Phase via hex adds another interrupt layer. Session 14 was four turns of teleport-in and strike, three of them with a teleport straight back out, and he was never punished for any of them.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** In one turn he Blinked to Oracle, moved him into Argent's sphere, and knocked her out. The one time he was punished, he was outside the warehouse and a teleporting enemy reached him first (4.1).
3. **Edge** `7.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Combat Reflexes, Situational Awareness and **Slow-Motion Dodge**, a free reaction that puts trouble on any attack against his Agility defence. It worked three times in four: the push-back evaded, a Dragonborne Marvel stripped of its edge, and a glitch bomb made to miss with Karma's help. Third on arrival, behind two members whose reactions act or move rather than only make an attack harder.
4. **Quasar-Force** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 5, permanent flight, and Evasion converting Agility attacks onto a high Melee defence. Fast in position, with no reaction powers to spend. Session 14 showed the cost: a character with no interrupt has no answer to being struck from concealment. The circlet (Session 15\) raised his Vigilance *defence* to 18, which makes him a harder target, not a faster actor.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.5.** **He rolled the first Marvel initiative of his career** and spent the bonus round being hit. Dragonborne and Blood Blade had Marvels too, and they had planned for him. With no reaction to spend, his only answer was Karma after the fact.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Agility 0 and a Vigilance defence of 14\. He is the last to act and the hardest to move once he has. In Session 14, stunned in round six, he was still stunned in round seven when the fight ended.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** His one reaction-economy moment was Karma spent to take the Marvel off Blood Blade's attack; it still did 43 and put him down.

### **4.8 Personal Mobility & Positioning**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Flight 2 is unmatched: combat speed at base × rank, non-combat at **base × rank × 50**. He hovers permanently out of melee reach, once moved a van fifteen squares vertically while carrying it, and nobody else operates in three dimensions by default. Session 14 was the reminder that positioning is a choice, not a property: two perfect rounds from concealment, then he flew into the open. On Kwa (Session 15\) he hovered twenty metres off and went higher when the progenitor reached for him.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** He used it well, and it was not enough. After the first knockdown he went to the far side of the warehouse, behind a shipping container, and worked from there. Blitzkrieg found him (*"What's ya doing, Hiding?"*) and a round later moved into range and dropped him. At −15, kept up by Let's Go, he stayed outside the warehouse and inside Oracle's range: positioning as life support.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** The best *tactical* repositioning in the party, if not the fastest. Blink as a reaction negates attacks, Teleport 1 covers rank × 10 in combat, and Teleport Blind via hex reaches places he has never seen. Session 14 was four insertions and three clean extractions without one counter-attack landing. He volunteered as scout on Kwa for exactly this reason.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** Blink, a teleport into the middle of Blood Blade and Dragonborne, and a teleport out after each strike. Blood Blade switched targets in Round 1 *"because of Lock-Step's teleporting nature."* The one position that failed him was the one he chose to rest in.
3. **Intrepid** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Flight 1 at base × rank. He flies, and has used it to get around an ice barrier and down into an alley. Agility 0 caps the finesse; in Session 14 he considered punching through a floor to chase a phasing enemy, which is the exact shape of his mobility problem. In Session 15 he tailed a limousine across Blüdhaven by air.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** He walked up to Dragonborne and Stote and punched. No flight is recorded.
4. **Edge** `2.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No movement power. Agility 7 makes him quick on foot, and he moved the way a marksman should, shooting and then stepping away, from Stote and later from Blitzkrieg. His roster role is *Precision Marksman & Spatial Infiltrator*, and nothing on the sheet yet supports the second half. ⟨Volatile⟩ His powers summary calls the weapon *"spatial-severing."*
5. **Oracle** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** No mobility power at all: Agility 4 and one STAR Labs acrobatic roof-jump. He walks, and it has cost the team tempo more than once; in Session 14 both of his moves ended with him grabbed the moment he arrived. On Kwa he said it himself: *"I'll have to run. I got a long run to get over there."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.5.** He ran back into the warehouse on his own feet. His other two moves of the fight were Lock-Step's: into Argent's sphere, and out of the building unconscious.

### **4.9 Team Mobility & Positioning**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.8`
   * **All-time.** Overwhelmingly. He is the team's strategic mobility layer. Teleport Together has inserted the entire team into a fortified lab, extracted Intrepid and Oracle mid-fight, lifted Robin off a sacrifice altar, cleared a bank of hostages and driven a civilian home. In Session 13 he teleported into a STAR Labs facility he had never seen **using a drone camera feed for visualisation**. ⟨Volatile⟩ In Session 15 the progenitor told him *"you may, Eric. Your abilities"* about temporal travel; time powers are on the GM's ban list, and that is not scored.
   * **Session 16: 9.5 → 9.8 (+0.3).** **Three different uses in one fight.** An insertion: Blink to Oracle, then a Powerful Hex for Teleport Together that delivered Oracle *inside* Argent's telekinetic sphere. A mid-fight rescue: the unconscious Oracle carried out of the building in Round 2\. And the whole extraction: Oracle and Quasar-Force, then Edge, then a last trip back for Intrepid on almost the last of his Focus. He asked first, too: *"Who is comfortable where they are at and who wants to move a little bit."*
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Not transport, but the highest-value *positioning* asset. The telekinetic nudge that converts a teammate's miss into a hit has been decisive in at least nine engagements. He places Oracle where Oracle asks to be placed, guided Intrepid to the square from which one Stomp removed six monsters, and funnels enemies with barriers. Session 14 added object logistics to personnel logistics.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.5.** One aid, on Edge's Suppressive Fire against Argent, and a grab that held the first target in place for Lock-Step. Then he was down.
3. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** Positioning as an information service: precognitive warnings broadcast before contact, and the explicit guidance that set up Intrepid's best attack of the campaign. In Session 14, from inside a grab, he guided Lock-Step's swing onto Ecliptica rather than onto Intrepid. On Day 56 (Session 15\) the service was the forecast that pointed the team at the warehouse.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.0.** The forecast was right about the warehouse. In the fight his module turned Intrepid's stomp, which reached only Dragonborne, into a hit.
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** He moves himself only.
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** He moves himself only. The rear guard that let Lock-Step move everyone else is scored in 4.6 and 4.19.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`. Nothing in Session 16 separates them, and on an unbroken tie the newcomer is listed second.

###

### **4.10 Single-Target Damage**

*Multiplier-plus-add convention; expected values use a Marvel-die mean of 4.33 and exclude Fantastic doubling, edges, assists, and Focus investment.*

| PC | Attack ability | Multiplier \+ add | Avg damage / hit | On a Fantastic |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Intrepid** | Melee | ×7 \+7 | **37.3** | 49 (98 doubled) |
| **Edge** | Agility (ranged) | ×7 \+7 | **37.3** | 49 (98 doubled) |
| **Quasar-Force** | Logic | ×7 \+7 | **37.3** | 49 (98 doubled) |
| **Lock-Step** *(whip)* | Melee | ×6 \+7 | **33.0** | 43 (86 doubled) |
| **Oracle** | Agility | ×4 \+4 | **21.3** | 28 (56 doubled) |
| *Lock-Step — unarmed* | *Melee* | *×3 \+7* | *20.0* | *25 (50 doubled)* |

1. **Intrepid** `9.2`
   * **All-time.** A ×7 multiplier, tied for the highest on the team, and **Smash is the differentiator: \+1 to the add for every 2 Focus spent, with a built-in edge.** His 90 Focus is otherwise nearly idle, so the conversion is close to free. He has the highest floor and the highest realistic ceiling, and he has delivered most of the knockouts on named opposition; in Session 14 a Marvel on Umbra removed the hammer-wielder in one action. Melee 7 (Session 15\) lifted the add to \+7.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.2.** Thirty-two to Blood Blade with Banging Heads, 28 to Dragonborne with a punch, 32 more with Smash, and a Smash Marvel in Round 4 that put Blood Blade at 17 and stunned her. He knocked out nobody this time. **The fight's three knockouts went to Lock-Step, Quasar-Force and Edge.**
2. **Edge** `9.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **×7 \+7 at range, on Agility 7 with Accuracy 4, tied for the best base line on the team.** Two powers turn it into damage per action that nobody else delivers at range. Stopping Power, his Signature Attack and so always carrying an edge, doubles on a Fantastic and grants a second attack on the same target; Double Tap doubles and adds bleeding. In play one Stopping Power took Stote from full Health to 19 — 74 on the Marvel and 27 on the follow-up, **101 in one action** — and another Marvel knocked out Blood Blade. He trails Intrepid only on Smash's scaling add.
3. **Quasar-Force** `8.7`
   * **All-time.** ×7 \+7 off Logic 7, tied for the best base line, delivered at five spaces per rank. Telekinetic Attack doubles and stuns on a Fantastic, and Grab into Crush chains in a single round. He is held back by what he targets (Melee and Resilience defences) and by Crush needing the grab to land first. In Session 14 he attacked nobody at all.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.7.** One crush, for 35, knocked out Stote after Edge's 101\. The crush on Dragonborne did nothing. He spent the fight's opening exchange being hit and its last four rounds down.
4. **Lock-Step** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** **The ×6 is not his, it is the whip's.** His base multiplier is ×3 and he carries no Mighty; the Vibranium Chain Whip supplies the rest, and Summonable makes disarming him impractical. What he does hold is **the highest Fantastic rate in the party**: Chain Strikes carries an edge, Signature Attack adds a second, and Combat Trickery guarantees a 1 on the Marvel die once per battle. In Session 14 his damage was shunted to Focus and negated by a force field. Session 15 added Elemental Infusion (Force), which under the house rule lets the whip hit a phasing target.
   * **Session 16: 7.5 → 7.8 (+0.3).** **The spike landed on people this time.** Chain Strikes with Combat Trickery did 84 to Argent, and a second strike knocked her out. In Round 3 a Marvel with multiple edges did 50 to Blood Blade, and a follow-up 19 more. ⟨Volatile⟩ The Force whip was never swung at a phaser in the log; the hand-waved ending credits it as one of the two reasons Blitzkrieg withdrew.
5. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** ×4 \+4, the lowest on the team, and **first for Focus damage rather than Health**: Suppressive Fire and Return Fire attack a different resource entirely. In Session 14 Ecliptica recovered the Focus damage he dealt by draining him. A damage type the enemy can refund is a damage type with a counter.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** The Focus damage did its job — the stuns and the demoralise that drove Dragonborne off are scored in 4.4 and 4.14 — and nobody refunded it.

*Closest call:* Intrepid over Edge at 1–2, on identical base expected damage. **Smash's scaling add and its built-in edge are the margin, and the Cutting Edge's \+1, if it applies, would erase it.**

### **4.11 Area-of-Effect Damage**

1. **Intrepid** `9.6`
   * **All-time.** Not close. Ground-Shaking Stomp checks Melee against the Agility defence of every target within reach plus rank in spaces. It has removed six monsters in a single attack, then four more, then a room of mobsters, then every Lex-Corp guard in an alley. Banging Heads takes two at full damage. With Exemplar II gone, he is the only member with a real area attack.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.6.** He used both. Banging Heads in Round 1 collapsed Argent's force field and did 32 to Blood Blade. The Stomp in Round 5 reached only Dragonborne, became a hit on Oracle's module assist, and did 11\.
2. **Lock-Step** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Elemental Burst is functionally single-target. Whatever area effect he brings comes out of a Powerful Hex configuration and costs him a turn to set up. The Force infusion adds reach against phasers, not area.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Quasar-Force** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** **Effectively zero.** Telekinetic Toss damages the thrown person and one target, and that is the whole list. It is the most conspicuous gap in his kit, and nothing on his Rank 3→4 plan closes it.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Oracle** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Single-target ranged only.
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Single-target ranged only: Suppressive Fire, Double Tap and Stopping Power each take one target. The newcomer did not fill the gap this axis has been describing.

*Tie:* Oracle over Edge at `0.5`. Nothing in Session 16 separates them, and on an unbroken tie the newcomer is listed second.

*The second-widest first-to-second gap in the section is still here, at 6.6, behind only Healing's 7.0.* **This team's entire area-damage capability is still one person's stomp.** Session 16 was five enemies spread through a warehouse, the shape of fight an area attack is for, and the one stomp reached one of them.

###

### **4.12 Ranged Combat / Engagement**

1. **Edge** `9.4`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **A second ranged specialist, and the better shot: first on arrival.** Accuracy 4 to Oracle's 1, a ×7 line to Oracle's ×4, three ranged attack powers — Double Tap, Stopping Power as his Signature Attack, and Suppressive Fire — and the Cutting Edge, a constructible weapon nobody can take off him for long. His best shot of the fight was not damage. With trouble for disarming a hand-held item, he shot the device out of Blitzkrieg's hand as she reached the wall, and destroyed it. **He also answers, on paper, the counter that beat Oracle in Session 14\.** Heightened Senses 2 compensates for a lost sense and gives a double edge to perceive, which is the profile that, in Exemplar II, still saw the room when invisibility and fog had beaten every instrument. Untested.
2. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** For fifteen sessions the team's dedicated ranged specialist: Accuracy 1, Suppressive Fire stunning on a Fantastic, Return Fire punishing anyone who declares against him, and held-overwatch discipline in every engagement. His misses hit walls. Session 14 showed the quality — the predictive module let him hit an *invisible* opponent — and the first hard counter: once the Vapors of Galator blocked line of sight past five squares, he could not shoot an enemy he could not see, from inside a grab he could not leave. **A ranged specialist with no mobility is a turret, and Session 14's opponent fogged the turret and then bolted it to the floor.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** **He loses first without losing anything.** Suppressive Fire in seven of his eight turns, the super-Marvel that stunned Argent, two stuns and a demoralise on Dragonborne, and one Return Fire turned against him. The newcomer is simply the better shot. The obscurement counter is now answered on paper by a teammate rather than by him.
3. **Quasar-Force** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** **Everything he does happens at five spaces per rank**, which makes him the most consistently ranged member of the team without a single ranged *attack* trait: Telekinetic Attack, Grab, Barrier and Toss all reach, off Logic 7\. Session 14 was an entire session conducted at range on objects, including lifting an artifact out of a goddess's hand from across a gallery.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.5.** He grabbed Argent from behind a shipping container on the far side of the warehouse. Range did not protect him: Blitzkrieg came into range and threw.
4. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** The chain whip has reach and Elemental Burst projects, but his kit wants him in the target's face, and he obliges. In Session 14 he went in on each of his four turns and three times returned at once to a position from which he could not be answered. The Force infusion sits on a reach weapon, not a ranged one.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** In, strike, out, three times.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** No ranged option whatsoever. He flies to the problem and hits it.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.

### **4.13 Support — Ally Buff**

*Powers and actions that make teammates measurably better.*

1. **Oracle** `9.7`
   * **All-time.** The team's only dedicated buffer, and the margin is enormous. **Let's Go** kept Quasar-Force conscious and acting at −11 Health. **Never Surrender** pulled Intrepid out of a demoralised stun against Omega Grade. **Danger Sense guarantees him a turn in the bonus round of every combat**, usually spent on somebody else. In Session 14, from inside an inescapable grab at 3 Focus, he guided Lock-Step's whip onto Ecliptica. Session 15 added a GM-granted channel: his module resonates with Lock-Step's time-altering powers, which gives him *"more easy justification to do assist directions to give Lock-Step edges."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.7.** **Let's Go was the hinge of the fight.** It held Quasar-Force up at −5 in the opening exchange, held him again from Round 4 to the end at −15 after 15 Health went in, and then moved to Lock-Step for the extraction. His module turned Intrepid's stomp into a hit, and he aided Edge's Suppressive Fire on Argent. ⟨Volatile⟩ The Lock-Step resonance channel was not used.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** No buff *powers*, and nonetheless the second-best buffer on the team. The telekinetic nudge that turns a teammate's miss into a hit is a pure action-economy gift, decisive in at least nine engagements. Session 14 produced three in two rounds, including one that made Intrepid's punch land on Umbra *without wasting the Marvel Intrepid had already rolled*. In Session 15 he used deduction to hand Lock-Step an edge on recalling the Grey Lantern oath.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.5.** One aid, on Edge's Suppressive Fire. The nudges need him upright, and he was upright for the first half of the fight only.
3. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No buff powers, and two assists that each decided something. In Round 3 his aid helped Intrepid's Smash land 32 on Blood Blade. In Round 4 Intrepid rolled a Marvel and missed, and Edge's aid turned it into a Marvel hit: Blood Blade dropped to 17, was stunned, lost concentration on Swipe, and **Intrepid got his Sturdy 3 back.** Third on arrival.
4. **Lock-Step** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Powerful Hex can be configured into buff-adjacent effects, and Teleport Together is force projection of a kind. It is real but occasional, and it always costs him the turn. Since Session 15 he has been the *recipient* of the most specific buff channel on the team.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** He aided Edge's Suppressive Fire, and put Oracle back inside Argent's sphere after it had pushed him out.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Immovable holding a line and a body absorbing hits meant for softer people are genuine passive support, not buffs. Session 14 brought the first two active assists of his campaign: he guided Lock-Step's whip from a miss into a hit and told him when to take the opening.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** He aided Edge's Suppressive Fire on Argent.

###

### **4.14 Support — Enemy Debuff**

*Effects that degrade enemy capability rather than removing enemy Health.*

1. **Oracle** `8.8`
   * **All-time.** **Real Jinx imposes trouble on every attack made against him, permanently, with no action cost and no roll** — a debuff applied automatically to every enemy who engages him. In Session 14 it made Ecliptica's fourth brain drain miss. Suppressive Fire stuns on a Fantastic, Return Fire punishes anyone who declares against him, and his Focus damage degrades enemy capability directly. ⟨Against⟩ Ecliptica recovered the Focus damage he dealt by draining him, so his damage type is a resource an enemy can refund.
   * **Session 16: 8.5 → 8.8 (+0.3).** **The debuff that ended the fight was his.** The opening super-Marvel stunned Argent. In Round 3, on his way back from being knocked out, a Marvel for 34 stunned Dragonborne, and in Round 5 another stun followed. In Round 7, with Edge's 31 having left Dragonborne at 11 Focus, his 15 demoralised him; Dragonborne missed his next attack, said *"We need to leave\!"*, and fled. **The brood's strongest body left the fight on Focus damage, not Health.**
2. **Lock-Step** `8.4`
   * **All-time.** The broadest active debuff kit on the team: paralysis riders on chain-whip hits, a Powerful Hex sleep effect that removed Aegis-Girl from a fight entirely, ice control, and Disrupt Person. In Session 14 the Marvel-boosted whip stunned an Olympian goddess and broke her grab on Oracle. His effects are bigger than Oracle's and need rolls and setup that Oracle's do not. The Force infusion (Session 15\) extends the whip's riders to phasing targets.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.4.** His contribution was removal rather than degradation — Argent knocked out, Blood Blade cut down — and no rider effects are recorded.
3. **Edge** `7.8`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Oracle's tool at nearly twice the multiplier.** Suppressive Fire at ×7 turns his Agility into Focus damage: 49 to Argent with the whole team aiding, 27 to Blood Blade, and 31 to Dragonborne, which set up Oracle's demoralise. He also tried his Monster trait — he *"uses his monstrousness to intimidate"* — on the two still standing, and *"they don't budge."* No stun yet; a Fantastic on that line would be the largest single debuff on the team.
4. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Control as debuff, and the numbers are brutal: **a grab break-out target of 20** makes Telekinetic Grab a soft removal rather than a hindrance. He has held Aegis-Girl, White-Out, Argent and Stote out of fights for multiple rounds. Barriers deny positioning, and Telekinetic Attack stuns on a Fantastic.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** The grab held Argent, the brood's mind-controller, through the first round. The pin on Dragonborne did not hold.
5. **Intrepid** `5.3`
   * **All-time.** Ground-Shaking Stomp prones everything within reach plus rank on a Fantastic, which is mass debuff by physics. Crushing Grip holds one, and Banging Heads staggers two. In Session 14 he held a goddess in place for a round and did nothing to her while doing it.
   * **Session 16: 5.0 → 5.3 (+0.3).** **He took his own power back.** In Round 4 his Edge-assisted Smash Marvel stunned Blood Blade, and the stun broke her concentration on Swipe, which returned his Sturdy 3\. Banging Heads in Round 1 had already collapsed Argent's force field. Two debuffs, both on the right target.

*Closest call:* Oracle over Lock-Step by 0.4, up from 0.1. For two revisions this axis said *"one more session like it and this flips."* Session 16 was the test, and it went the other way: **Lock-Step's debuffs are larger, but Oracle's ended the fight.**

### **4.15 Healing**

1. **Oracle** `10.0`
   * **All-time.** **The only 10.0 awarded in the document.** He is the sole member with a general capacity to restore another person's Health: Healing Hands converts his Focus into their Health one for one, and Let's Go and Never Surrender reverse conditions nobody else can touch. He healed Quasar-Force back from −11, revived the man Intrepid nearly killed, and attempted to revive the man Quasar-Force killed. In Session 9 he rationed his own reserves, a triage decision nobody else on the roster is equipped to make. In Session 14, drained to 3 of 180 Focus, he could not heal a teammate at −12: **the monopoly failed once, under load, at the moment it was the only thing that mattered.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 10.0.** **The monopoly carried the fight.** Let's Go kept Quasar-Force acting at −5, and later at −15, and then moved to Lock-Step for the extraction. He put 15 Health into Quasar-Force mid-fight, knowing it would not bring him back. After the fight he healed Lock-Step so that Lock-Step could teleport the others, healed Quasar-Force to consciousness, and healed Lock-Step again. Nobody else restored anyone's Health. **This is the session the axis exists for, and it went the way the score says.**
2. **Lock-Step** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** For three revisions this document scored Powerful Hex's healing potential as theoretical upside. **Session 14 used it.** With Quasar-Force at 0 two hours after the fight and the medic empty, he configured Powerful Hex into healing hands and brought him to 5, the second person on the roster ever to restore another person's Health. Three caveats keep it at 3.0: a hex configuration rather than a dedicated power; *"the minimum amount allowed that will wake him up"*; and a stated motive that was not compassion (*"Really need him to start working on this 'phase stuff'"*).
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** ⟨Volatile⟩ Not used, for a second session. Nobody needed the backup, because the healer was never drained, and every point of Lock-Step's Focus went into teleports, which was the better use of it. The question this entry has carried since Session 14 — whether the backup recurs — stays open.
3. **Intrepid** `2.7`
   * **All-time.** Healing Factor, the best regeneration on the team, returning a Resilience-rank of Health every turn — 7 since the Rank 3.5 advance. Self only.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.7.** It worked exactly as written: −3 to \+4 in the same round, and −49 to −42 to −35 while he lay unconscious.
4. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Healing Factor at Resilience 3, self only. It got him up from exactly 0 in Round 7, and he held the rear guard on it. Behind Intrepid on rate.
5. **Quasar-Force** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** **Zero.** No healing factor, no self-repair and no regeneration of any kind in combat, and he is the only member with none. Outside combat the box's bio-mechanical constructs heal him fast — three hours from −12 to consciousness, and a full 60 in ten hours of sleep — but only the in-combat half has ever decided anything.
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.0.** He healed himself 18 in the opening exchange, but with Karma, under a rule every character has; that is not a capability. Zero for a sixteenth session.

*Structural note.* One healer and one improvised backup. **Session 16 tested the healer at full load, and he held.** It did not test the scenario the single-healer finding in §10.4 is about: the counter-build that drained him in Session 14 was not used, and the backup was not needed. If a mentalist takes Oracle out of a fight, the team still has no recovery mechanism *during* the fight. ⟨Note⟩ Edge brings a second healing factor, not a second healer.

### **4.16 Ability to Avoid Collateral Damage**

1. **Oracle** `9.0`

   * **All-time.** Ranged precision with Accuracy 1 and a Charmed Life edge. He moves nothing, shakes nothing and lifts nothing, and his misses hit a wall.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** The one stray shot of the fight was his, and an enemy aimed it: Dragonborne redirected his Return Fire onto Lock-Step. It is not scored against him.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **The most interesting entry on this axis.** His powers are inherently surgical: firearms plucked individually off gang members, a van lifted without disturbing an alley, unconscious bodies extracted from a room filling with gas, a sleeping man cuffed without being woken. Session 14 was three turns of precision work in a gallery of irreplaceable objects, without breaking one. Against it, the largest destructive ceiling on the team. **The control is genuinely there; the judgement occasionally isn't.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** Two grabs and a crush, each on a person, in a warehouse full of crates. No collateral is recorded.
3. **Edge** `7.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A marksman's profile: every attack he made was one shot at one target, and his most delicate one was aimed at a device in a villain's hand. Against it, the roster describes his weapon attacks as *"spatial-severing,"* and his backstory begins with an attacker's weapon sliced to pieces. **Precise in his first fight; the ceiling of what he cuts is unknown.**
4. **Lock-Step** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Teleportation is clean, the whip is single-target and his ice constructs are localised. Session 14 was four insertions and three extractions in a room full of exhibits without a stray effect. Against: he has proposed setting fires in bushes as a plan, and Berserk removes every restraint at once. His Session 15 collateral was reputational, and it is scored in 2.5 and §9.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.0.** Clean, and no Berserk.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** His signature attack is, by definition, an indiscriminate area effect, and a ×7 multiplier and 301 pounds arriving by air into an enclosed room is a structural event. In Session 14, in a gallery holding three Themysciran relics, he did not use Ground-Shaking Stomp once. Whether that was restraint or aperture was unresolved.
   * **Session 16: 2.0 → 2.5 (+0.5).** **He used the stomp, and it hit only an enemy.** In a warehouse full of crates, with four teammates and a device everyone wanted somewhere on the floor, his one Ground-Shaking Stomp reached Dragonborne and nobody else, with Oracle's module steering it onto him.

### **4.17 Lethal vs Non-Lethal Precision**

1. **Oracle** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** The only member whose primary attacks **cannot kill**: Suppressive Fire and Return Fire deal Focus damage. On top of that he carries Healing Hands, Let's Go and Never Surrender, so he can un-kill other people's mistakes, and has.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** Every point he dealt was Focus, and he is the reason the fight with Dragonborne ended in a retreat rather than a body.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** **He holds a throttle nobody else has.** Half of his ×6 comes from the whip, so dropping it halves his multiplier — a deliberate, mechanical non-lethal setting, used in Session 11 and announced out loud. Session 13 ran the doctrine twice under pressure and it held both times. Against: he was beating a captive conscious in Session 9, and Berserk suspends the doctrine entirely. **Session 15 gave the doctrine an exception clause.** *"No, I'm not coming around on lethal force,"* he said in the room; moments later, in private, it was *"dealt with, cinder blocks in the ocean,"* justified by *"it's not human."*
   * **Session 16: 7.0 → 7.5 (+0.5).** **The doctrine was stated, applied and held under the heaviest fire the team has taken.** When Oracle's line about the five came out garbled (*"hopefully, die, harming them?, dissuade them?"*), Lock-Step corrected it: *"Nope, nope, Nightwing did say we can bring them back, so knock them out."* He offered the cuffs twice, knocked Argent out with 84 and a second strike, and cuffed her. The exception clause is still on file — *"The other situation, is that other team's problem"* — which is why half a point comes back rather than the whole point.
3. **Edge** `6.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Half of his offence is non-lethal by construction: Suppressive Fire deals Focus damage, and he used it three times. The other half is the hardest-hitting ranged line on the team: Stopping Power put 101 into Stote in one action, and a Marvel knocked out Blood Blade. Nobody died. The unanswered question is the power behind the weapon, which slices objects by concentration, and a backstory monster that *"cut down armies."* **He has a throttle — choosing Suppressive Fire — and nobody has yet seen him at full.**
4. **Quasar-Force** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** The finest control on the team, the clearest stated ethic, and **the team's only confirmed kill.** Crush is an all-or-nothing damage reaction applying ×7 \+7 to whatever is already held. His precision is high; **his calibration against unknown durability is the failure mode.** His stated rule, per the roster, is to kill only against lethal intent that cannot otherwise be stopped. Session 15 added *"we would be more comfortable unleashing Fade-Out with his full lethal fury on the controller."* **The old risk was calibration; the new one is his willingness to delegate.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** **The calibration held on a person he had crushed before.** Stote, at 19 Health after Edge's shots, took a 35-point crush and was knocked out, not killed. He also described the Destined errand as *"the murder hobos"* sent *"after the bad progenitor."* The delegation is unchanged.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** Every large swing is a near-death event against ordinary humans — a coin flip on manslaughter — and he knows it, which is exactly why he is afraid all the time. In Session 14 a Marvel on Umbra for massive damage produced a knockout rather than a body.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** His opponents were powered teenagers, not ordinary humans, and the heaviest hit he landed was 32\. The coin was not flipped.

### **4.18 De-escalation / Conflict Termination Without Force**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.7`

   * **All-time.** **The most underrated thing he does, and it is genuinely excellent.** He opens engagements offering handcuffs and telling people they don't want to kill anyone; Nova and Ghost-Ape cuffed themselves rather than fight him. He talked the Burnout Kings into standing aside and negotiated the Destined out of the lobby. Session 15 was the single best de-escalation this document has recorded. He paused a team that had talked itself into a fight, found the missing premise (*"do we even know if they're hostile?"*), walked up to an apex predator's leader and asked it a question, and worked the conversation to terms both sides accepted. The officer who had built the scenario told him: *"This was a test to see what you would do… Your choices are interesting."* Against it, from the same session: the Day 56 proposal that *"we might need to break him,"* handed to a villain team.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.7.** **The offer reached the people and lost to the thing controlling them.** Offered the cuffs, Argent *"seems to start to 'accept'"*, and then her eyes rolled back as if she had received a renewed command to kill. Offered again, Dragonborne said *"It is a good idea, but something inside us is telling us not to."* No words were going to work against a controller, and he said them anyway, twice — then *"I don't know why you keep wanting to do things the hard way."*
2. **Oracle** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** He demands surrender before nearly every engagement, without exception and without being told to. His version is less charismatic than Lock-Step's and applied more consistently. In Session 14 he rang the client, opened with the loss, and credited Wonder Woman for taking responsibility. On Kwa (Session 15\) his first read was elimination, and he talked himself out of it on the evidence.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** No surrender demand is recorded this time; Lock-Step made the offers. His garbled line about the five — *"hopefully, die, harming them?, dissuade them?"* — ended on *dissuade*, and he did not contest the correction to knock-outs.
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Compassion as a termination tool, effective in a narrow band and remarkable inside it. Argent surrendered while he was holding her. Cassidy Yanks gave her real name because he was kind to her. World Ender's whole villain persona collapsed under sincerity. In Session 14 he defused the panicking box by acknowledging its fear. Session 15 was split: *"an exchange of truth"* and *"I don't think we should just beat him up"* about Lobo, against a debate he escalated with the one being present who could have acted on it.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** He read the situation correctly before anyone tried: *"We probably can't talk to these guys, because they are being controlled."* He did not try, and he was right.
4. **Edge** `3.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** His instinct before the door opened was a plan, not a fight: *"Do we have a plan for when we enter this place?"* His one attempt to end things without force in the fight was the other kind — the Monster trait used to intimidate the last two standing — and *"they don't budge."* Fourth, on one good question and one intimidation that failed.
5. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** On the record, Shadow-Boxer would have got nothing out of him but fist. In Session 14, the one conflict he was personally involved in, he escalated, lost, and retreated with *"he was just joking."* In Session 15 he was the member most ready to attack a species the team then saved (*"It's time to take them out\!"*).
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.

### **4.19 Rescue / Civilian Protection**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.8`
   * **All-time.** The best rescue tool on the team by a distance. He teleported a bank's worth of hostages clear, lifted Robin off a sacrifice altar, extracted teammates mid-fight and drove a civilian home. In Session 13 he freed three mind-controlled hostages at once by knocking out the thing holding the leash. **Teleport Together is the only power on this roster that removes a person from danger instantly.** Session 14 added the first medical rescue by anyone but Oracle. Session 15 was the largest rescue in the campaign, done without a power: the planet Kwa has a people on it because he argued for them.
   * **Session 16: 9.7 → 9.8 (+0.1).** **Two rescues of teammates, and the first with consent asked.** In Round 2, with Oracle unconscious at −2 in the middle of the brood, he teleported out of the building with him. In the hand-waved ending he got all four teammates out of a building two villains were still in. Before either, he asked: *"Who is comfortable where they are at and who wants to move a little bit."* The tenth is small because the axis is already near the ceiling.
2. **Oracle** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Rescue after the fact and by prevention: he revives, he heals, and he demands surrender first precisely so that nobody needs rescuing. In Session 12 he put his own body between Star Heart and the Celestial Core's containment field. Session 14 was the first session he could not do it: held and empty, he watched the person he would have reached first stay on the floor.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.5.** The Session 14 failure did not repeat. The person he would have reached first went down twice, and both times Let's Go reached him.
3. **Quasar-Force** `8.4`
   * **All-time.** He pulled unconscious bodies out of a room filling with toxic gas, moved a juice van away from buyers instead of destroying it, and cut Doctor Varga out of webbing. He proposed the parasite screening that caught three infections. In Session 14 he broke cover to deny a goddess the artifact that could have located Themyscira and took a blade through the abdomen for it. Since Session 15 the tower shield protects at the scale of a structure.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.4.** Nobody was rescued from the brood this session (note below). His screening precedent — *"we were able to cure the other kids"* — is still the hope for the five.
4. **Intrepid** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** He absorbs the damage that would otherwise land on someone softer, and Immovable makes him a wall people can stand behind. In Session 12 he interposed for Doctor Varga deliberately and unprompted. On Kwa he offered the scout *"You could always use my body as a shield."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** He stayed where the damage was and refused to be moved out of it, which is the body-as-shield offer made good.
5. **Edge** `6.8`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The rear guard.** With Oracle near collapse, Quasar-Force at −15 and Lock-Step needing turns to ferry them, he held off Blitzkrieg alone for a round so that the extraction could start. Close behind Intrepid on one act of protection.

*⟨Note⟩ The civilians in this fight were the opponents.* The five were infected teenagers under a controller's orders, and Lock-Step's rule — *"we can bring them back, so knock them out"* — made the brief a rescue as much as a fight. Three were knocked out and left in the warehouse, one of them cuffed; two left on their own feet. **None was secured, cured or brought back**, and the log does not resolve the 24-hour clock from Session 15\. By the end, the team itself needed rescuing.

### **4.20 Combat Synergy / Combined-Arms Compatibility**

*How much better this person makes everyone else's kit work.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **The telekinetic nudge that converts a teammate's miss into a hit is the most efficient action economy on the team**, decisive in at least nine engagements. He guided Intrepid to the exact square from which one Stomp removed six monsters, places Oracle where Oracle asks to be placed, and builds barriers that funnel enemies into the brawlers. Session 14 added preserving somebody else's critical rather than replacing it. Every one of those actions is worth more in someone else's hands than in his own.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** The grab that held Argent was the platform for her knockout: Lock-Step put Oracle into her sphere and finished her while she was held. He also aided Edge's shot. Then he was down.
2. **Oracle** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** He heals, buffs, revives and positions, and takes a turn in every bonus round. **He raises everyone's floor where Quasar-Force raises their ceiling.** In Session 14 he contributed synergy from inside a grab at 3 Focus. Session 15's Grey Lantern resonance is **the first synergy on this roster that exists because two characters' powers are compatible, not because one of them is generous.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.3.** His Focus damage and Edge's stacked: Edge's 31 left Dragonborne at 11, and Oracle's 15 broke him. His module steered Intrepid's stomp. Let's Go is synergy in its plainest form, and it kept a teammate below zero on the board for half the fight.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** Enormous positive potential and a recurring negative. He inserts the whole team into places it could not otherwise reach, and he also removes people from fights without asking. Session 14 was the most-assisted performance on record at the time: four pieces of teamwork from four teammates in one combat. **A character who absorbs help from every teammate in a five-round fight is a combined-arms platform whether or not he thinks of himself as one.** In Session 15 he connected two teammates to an alien hive mind without asking them.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.2.** The recurring negative half-recurred: he asked Intrepid before moving him, and moved Oracle into Argent's sphere with no recorded ask. That move was combined arms in a single turn — his teleport, Quasar-Force's grab and his own whip. He also aided Edge's Suppressive Fire.
4. **Edge** `8.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **His first attack for the team was aided by all four of his new teammates** — Intrepid, Oracle, Lock-Step and Quasar-Force on one Suppressive Fire at Argent, for 49 Focus. Nobody had to be told the newcomer's shot was the one to back. He returned it: two aids to Intrepid, one of which turned a Marvel miss into the hit that gave Intrepid his Sturdy back; Focus damage that set up Oracle's demoralise; and the target call the plan was built on. Fourth on arrival, close behind three veterans.
5. **Intrepid** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** He holds the line so that four fragile people can operate behind it: passive synergy of high value, with historically little interaction with anyone's specific abilities. Session 14 added the first active thread, guiding Lock-Step's whip and telling him when to swing.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.5.** His Banging Heads collapsed Argent's force field, he aided Edge's first shot, and the two best moments of his fight came on Edge's aid. **The new member's kit and his fit together from the first afternoon.**

### **4.21 Overall Combat Value / Contribution**

*Not raw power — contribution to the team winning and surviving. This axis and the next are deliberately different questions, and the difference is the point.*

1. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** **He is the reason nobody has died.** Let's Go held Quasar-Force conscious at −11, Never Surrender pulled Intrepid out of a demoralised stun, Healing Hands resets the party between engagements, Real Jinx absorbs enemy actions passively, and Danger Sense gets him a turn in every bonus round. Session 14 showed his absolute floor: grabbed, blinded and drained to 3 Focus, he still dodged an invisible attacker, landed Return Fire, made Real Jinx eat a brain drain and redirected the swing that freed him. Session 15's resonance with the team's teleporter added a tenth.
   * **Session 16: 8.8 → 9.0 (+0.2).** **He opened the fight, kept it going and ended it.** The super-Marvel stun on the agreed first target came first. Knocked out by 72 in Round 2, he was back in Round 3 with *"Drop me again, it was worth it"* and a stun on Dragonborne. Let's Go kept Quasar-Force on the board for the second half of the fight, the demoralise in Round 7 sent Dragonborne running, and his healing afterwards made the extraction possible. ⟨Flag⟩ Two advances, still undeclared.
2. **Edge** `8.7`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Second on arrival.** He named the first target. He knocked out Blood Blade, the member of the brood who had stolen Intrepid's power. He put 101 into Stote in one action, so that Quasar-Force's crush could finish him. He dealt 107 Focus damage across three Suppressive Fires, the last of which set up the demoralise. He turned a Marvel miss into the hit that returned Intrepid's Sturdy. He destroyed the device the brood had come for, and he held the rear so the extraction could happen. Down once, at exactly 0, and back up.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.4`
   * **All-time.** Insertion and extraction nobody else can provide, the decisive blow on more named villains than anyone, and a surrender ritual that has ended fights before they started. Session 14 was his best sustained combat performance: five disciplined rounds against the hardest target the team has faced, the stun that freed the healer, four assists accepted and converted, and the post-combat heal. He remains docked for the two Berserk episodes and the non-consensual extractions. Melee 7 and a whip that reaches phasing targets (Session 15\) widened what he can contribute.
   * **Session 16: 8.1 → 8.4 (+0.3).** He knocked out Argent in the first round — the plan's target, finished — and put 69 into Blood Blade in Round 3\. He took the healer out of the fight unconscious, and brought everyone home at the end. Against it, he was one-shot from full while alone outside and was out from Round 4 to the end. **No Berserk, and the one extraction of a conscious teammate was asked first.**
4. **Quasar-Force** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** Control that removes named opponents for multiple rounds, mobility that repositions the whole team, an assist economy that converts teammates' misses into hits, barriers that shape the battlefield, and joint-highest base damage. **What caps him is not contribution but survival: he cannot stay standing long enough to deliver a full engagement's worth of it.** Session 15 moved the floor (H-DR 3, defences 19/19/15, the best passive mental profile on the team) without solving it.
   * **Session 16: 8.4 → 8.2 (−0.2).** **The cap, demonstrated by an enemy that planned for it.** In four turns he grabbed Argent through her force field and held her for the knockout, pinned Dragonborne, crushed Stote unconscious, and kept working from cover at 13 Health. Then a glitch bomb took him out for the last four rounds. **The brood said in the fight that he was its first target because of what he does when he is standing.**
5. **Intrepid** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** He absorbs the damage that would kill anyone else on the team, and he ends fights outright: Session 14 opened with a Marvel on Umbra that removed the hammer from the board in one action. Then the aperture closed — a missed assisted target, a grab whose payload could not beat a force field, and a stun he could not shake. Session 15's sheet (210 Health, 20/20/20, Healing Factor 7, the only area attack left on the roster) lifted him to 8.0, untested.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** He absorbed 290 damage, collapsed Argent's force field, punched Dragonborne for 28, took his own power back with a Smash Marvel stun, and refused to leave. He knocked out nobody and went down twice. **The fight's worst damage went to him, which is what he is for, and none of its knockouts did.**

*Volatile:* positions two through five are separated by 0.7, **and the newcomer is second.** Session 16 moved Oracle up and Quasar-Force down for the same reason: one stayed in the fight and one could not. The inversion with 4.22 holds at both ends.

###

### **4.22 Overall Combat Power / Potency**

*Raw capability to impose an outcome by force, as distinct from 4.21's contribution. Compare the two orderings.*

1. **Intrepid** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** Elite physical durability, elite area damage, elite single-target damage, elite stamina, and the best objective-holding on the team via Immovable: **the highest raw combat output on the roster.** It fell in Sessions 13 and 14, both times on the same component — Crushing Grip landed cleanly on two opponents and accomplished nothing either time — and the Rank 3.5 advance reversed the decline. The number of things he can do with his weight is unchanged; there is simply more weight.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.3.** The weight showed: 290 damage absorbed. So did the limit: a power thief took his Sturdy and put him down, and a flying brick of his own class hit him for 72 and 60 and was never put down in return.
2. **Edge** `7.9`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **A specialist's potency.** Tied for the best base damage on the team and the best ranged attack on it, a free Focus-damage line at ×7, a dodge that strips Marvels, a healing factor and the only Heightened Senses on the roster. Against it: 90 Health, no area attack, no control, no movement power and middling mental defences. ⟨Volatile⟩ The legend and the Cutting Edge's unapplied \+1 are both upside the score does not count.
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** Best crowd control, best grappling, best personal mobility, best combat synergy, best objective creation, and joint-highest base damage. Against it: paper Health, no healing of any kind, effectively no area damage, and a demonstrated one-strike removal from full to unconscious. **His ceiling is the highest on the team, and his floor is the reason it is rarely reached.** Session 15 was the first revision in which the floor rose.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.8.** The ceiling showed in four turns, and the floor in two strikes.
4. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Best mental durability, best team mobility, best de-escalation, best rescue and the highest Fantastic rate in the party, plus second place on Healing since Session 14\. He is held down by a Focus pool tied for the shallowest, fragile Health, damage that belongs to the whip rather than to him, and Berserk. He passed Oracle on this axis in Session 14, and since Session 15 he carries the one weapon on the roster that reaches a phasing target.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.5.** The whip landed on people this time — 84 and a knockout on Argent, 69 on Blood Blade — and a single glitch bomb removed him from full. Both halves, as scored.
5. **Oracle** `7.2`
   * **All-time.** First on more of the twenty-two combat sub-axes than anyone else. What sinks him on raw potency is three narrow weaknesses — Focus durability, Stamina and Grappling — which Session 14's opponent hit with two effects, a grab and a drain. **A character whose weaknesses are narrow and known is a character an opponent can build against, and somebody did.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.2.** He is first on seven combat sub-axes after Session 16 — Reaction, Ally Buff, Enemy Debuff, Healing, Collateral Avoidance, Non-Lethal Precision and Combat Value — having lost Ranged to the newcomer. The brood did not use the counter-build; it knocked him down with 72 physical damage, and he got up. ⟨Flag⟩ Two advances are banked and undeclared. If either buys a mental power that projects strength, or Uncanny, this entry is understated.

**Read this against 4.21 and the inversion holds.** Oracle is last here and first there; Intrepid is first here and last there. **Edge is second on both, the only member whose contribution and potency agree.** The spread on this axis is 1.1 points top to bottom, as it was in Session 15\.

## **5\. Non-Combat Power / Ability / Value**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*Session 16 was a fight with a short planning scene outside the warehouse, so this section moved less than any other in the revision: three axes among the returning four. The new evidence is mostly Edge's. Heightened Senses 2 restores, on paper, the counter-infiltration and innate-senses capability that left with Exemplar II, and his Ego 4 is the second-highest social ability score on the team. His Logic of −1 is the lowest on the roster, and it puts him level with or below Intrepid on every Logic-driven axis. Where the two tie on those axes, the tie is broken on Logic.*

### **5.1 Leadership / Organization**

*Running things, as distinct from being followed — see 3.6–3.8 for command.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** **Organisationally the best on the team, and long unwilling to command.** In Session 14 he started the entire Titans Tower security programme, specifying the requirement — block teleportation and phasing while preserving the League transport already in the building — and recruiting the one specialist on the station qualified to build it. Session 15 was the first session in which he also ran things: a six-day engineering programme delivered in priority order in someone else's facility, then a layered surveillance operation on Day 56 — two drones in his normal pattern, a plate trace to a named STAR Labs executive, a stealth drone left in a warded garage, a tail on the drop-off, and a scout sent ahead to the warehouse on the strength of Oracle's forecast.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.3.** The organising he did was intelligence: the drone sweep that found the five searching crates, the deduction about the progeny's powers, and a proposal for a sneaky entry that the team did not take.
2. **Oracle** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** He runs the household on a schedule, staffs the medic station, keeps the patrol rota and files honest reports. He has acted unilaterally on principle, which is where organisation becomes leadership, and he has run two sessions outright. In Session 14 he made the client call nobody else wanted to make. In Session 15 his organisational contribution was the forecast, which gave the room a probability, an actor, a timeline and a destination to plan around.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.2.** The plan came from him, after he had first said there wasn't one, and it is scored in 3.2 and 3.7.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** He leads by acting first. For most of the campaign he organised nothing, and the team followed him because he moved. Session 14 brought real staff work in a facility he did not control, and the team's only mission-vetting proposal. Session 15 was the largest movement on this axis: preparation before first contact, a team huddle at the Watchtower that diagnosed the gap correctly (*"it's not like they have smarter people… they just have better resources"*), a week-long social-media operation run as a production, and on Day 56 a division of labour across three parties under a 24-hour clock. He also called, for the first time, for *"a group discussion"* before a decision. Against: the decision the discussion produced.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** He handed the plan to Oracle and ran the exit. The organising he did before the fight was complaint rather than organisation — about Iron-Side, the League and the adults — and two of its claims were wrong.
4. **Edge** `4.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The newcomer made the team plan.** *"Do we have a plan for when we enter this place?"* and *"Can you tell me the situation, please?"* were the two questions the team had not asked itself, and when the plan came together, its target was his. One afternoon, Logic −1, and no record of running anything yet.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** The roster notes that he tries to lead and is not always the right man for the job; the attempts are visible and nothing lands. His most useful organisational remark came a day late in Session 14 (*"did we do any research on the items we were protecting at all"*), and the answer was that they had. Asked for a plan on Day 56: *"I got nothing."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** Asked for a plan: *"Nothing, I guess."*

### **5.2 Engineering & Crafting / Fabrication & Repair**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.8`

   * **All-time.** Uncontested. Logic 7 with Brilliance 4 gives an effective \+11, on top of Inventor, Scientific Expertise and lab access at three sites. He runs and modifies the drone fleet, rewrote its security after the Destined breach, and once built four new drones in a three-day downtime window. Session 15 was the most productive week of engineering in the campaign: a signal-masking circlet of *"Metamaterial-infused Hastelloy C-22"* with *"new-god based circuitry"* that also provides Uncanny 3; an anti-teleportation device that defeated the Destined's transport in play; a nanosuit upgrade to Sturdy 3 with the League's specialist; and two drones for Lock-Step's streaming. **Three GM-approved builds from one week of downtime is the strongest engineering evidence this document has had for anyone.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.8.** No downtime and no builds. The circlet passed its first field test (4.2).
2. **Oracle** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 3 with Tech Reliance and Eidetic Memory, and he understands the STAR Labs systems he is built from well enough to interface with them. He has not been shown building anything. In Session 14 his hardware was named as a component of somebody else's build. **He is not the engineer; he is part of the bill of materials.** Since Exemplar II left, the gap from first to second on this axis has been 5.3 points.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0\. He recognises when technology is valuable and cannot do anything with it himself, but he knows who to ask: *"Quasar, can you make her a helmet? For telepathy?"* was the right request to the right person.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0\. His offer to a security discussion was to squeeze objects until they broke, and in Session 14 he said, accurately, *"I don't have any tech skills."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Logic −1. His iconic weapon is Constructible, which describes the item, not a skill.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`, broken on Logic (0 against −1).

### **5.3 Cyber & Information Warfare & Security**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** He rebuilt the drone fleet's security architecture after the Destined breach, rotated every code, established three geographically separate archives, and fields a communications-blackout electronic-warfare suite he has used operationally. Nightwing credited him by name. Fade-Out conceded the code changes *"were a pain in the neck"* and explained why it did not matter (*"Dick Grayson designed the system"*), and the correct response, changing the physics rather than the credentials, is exactly what he did. Session 15's circlet is information security at a level nobody on this team had operated at: passive cloaking and active jamming that hides a New God signature from a New God. ⟨Note⟩ In Session 14 the supervillains did not appear on the drones' infrared at all.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.3.** **The Session 14 infrared blind spot did not recur.** His drone's infrared found the five spread through the warehouse searching crates before anyone went in. One success against these opponents does not close the gap against the ones who beat it.
2. **Oracle** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** He interfaces with STAR Labs systems and monitors media, but carries Tech Reliance as a vulnerability rather than a skill and has built nothing. In Session 15 the module was logged for the first time as a network interface (*"I'm actually interfacing with the internet right now"*) running alongside his precognition — not intrusion, but information gathering through a channel no teammate has.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0\. On the *security* half of this axis he is the team's single largest liability (§1.6). Session 15 showed that the *information warfare* half is his: a broadcast set trending on command with an Ego check of 20, a week-long influence operation run with a co-producer, and a seeded channel for every teammate. **That is audience manipulation at scale.** His security record is why this is 3.0 and not higher.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** No broadcast is recorded.
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0, and his contribution to the post-breach security discussion was to offer to squeeze objects until they broke.
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Logic −1, and nothing on the sheet or in the log touches this axis.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`, broken on Logic.

### **5.4 Field Investigation, Forensics & Intelligence Gathering**

*On-scene work, canvassing, tailing, source handling, and human intelligence.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** A laboratory mind doing field work by remote, and doing it well: stealth drones with hyper-spectral and infrared reconnaissance, an electronic-warfare blackout suite, and the tradecraft to phone in a false noise complaint so a patrol car would occupy a front gate. In Session 13 he read a technopathic intrusion off nothing but four drones drifting out of position. In Session 15 he took first, partly on the vacancy, with the best remote surveillance of his campaign — the juveniles watched, the plate traced to STAR Labs' CFO, a stealth drone in a warded garage, a tail on the drop-off — and **he stated the evidentiary status of each finding accurately every time.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.5.** The drone sweep before entry, and the deduction that the progeny had not yet given the five full access to their new powers. Both were right.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.2`
   * **All-time.** Elite human intelligence and nothing else. He got the nightclub location out of a mob lieutenant over Polish cake, the midnight deal location out of a captive, and the warehouse intelligence out of the Burnout Kings. People tell him things they would tell nobody else on this team. Session 15 held the tradecraft failure of his campaign: he broadcast a surveillance operation to the public, zooming on the targets' faces and telling viewers *"Stay tuned."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.2.** No sourcing. His briefing to the newcomer contained two errors of fact — that Nightwing had not filled Edge in, and that the adults had told Quasar-Force to fix things — which is the opposite of intelligence, but it was talk among teammates.
3. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Sent to look at a museum in Session 13, he produced what a professional would: every entrance, an underground approach nobody asked about, an accurate security posture, and an independent sighting of somebody casing the building. Vigilance 6 is the highest on the roster, and precognition is reconnaissance by another name. Session 15 brought the most operationally specific forecast of his campaign, and on Kwa the question that determined the progenitor's threat model.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** The forecast was right: the five went to the warehouse it named. His own briefing of the situation to the newcomer drew *"Ok, that told me nothing."*
4. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The instrument is excellent and the operator is new.** Heightened Senses 2 lets him sense four times as far as normal, gives him a double edge on Vigilance checks to perceive, and lets him listen to a person's heartbeat to see whether they are lying — *"as reliable as a traditional lie detector."* Against it, Logic −1 and no investigative record. He also arrived briefed: Nightwing had told him about the five, which the team did not know.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** He found the Insidious nest by walking around and looking, which is a real contribution and an unskilled one. In Session 14 he gave Wonder Woman an accurate description of the stolen artifacts. In Session 15 he tailed a limousine across the city by air without being spotted.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** Nothing changed.

### **5.5 Lab Investigation, Forensics & Data Analysis**

*Bench work, document analysis, data synthesis, and evidence handling.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 7 with Brilliance 4, and a results record nobody else approaches: checks of 20, 21, 22, 24 and 25\.
