He decoded the containment release mechanisms, found and interpreted the medical report on the bane derivative and its non-terrestrial component, analysed the temporal artifact down to its purpose and its transmission, and built the whole Lex-Corp evidence chain. In Session 14 he worked out that the mother box's *"Oh No"* was a panic-driven reproduction of his own mother's voice. **That is forensic reasoning applied to the inside of his own skull.** In Session 15 he connected Kwa to the present the moment Shein Qua spoke, and he still holds the team's memory of where the evidence chain stands.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** The deduction about the progeny's powers was bench reasoning done in the field, and it held up.
2. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** **Eidetic Memory is a serious analytical instrument**: perfect recall of every document, scene and conversation the team has encountered. In Session 14 he produced the names of what had been stolen while the room was still arguing. Session 15 produced a genuine analytical product: the module's conclusion that Lock-Step's powers are *"based on lantern technology / magic,"* linked to Oa and the Grey Lanterns, and work *"by unconsciously altering time waves"* — **the first explanation anybody on this team has produced of what Lock-Step actually is.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** Nothing new. ⟨Volatile⟩ The device the brood came for was keyed to Lock-Step's time manipulation, which is the module's subject, and it was destroyed before anyone could look at it.
3. **Lock-Step** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0\. He recognises when data matters and can do nothing with it.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `0.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 0, and no engagement with the analytical side of any case.
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `0.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Logic −1, and no engagement with the analytical side of the case. He destroyed the one piece of physical evidence the fight produced, which was the right call in the moment and is scored in 4.6.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `0.5`, broken on Logic.

### **5.6 Law & Legal Skill / Ability**

*A live operational front: a cease-and-desist on record, a threatened wrongful-death suit, an open police investigation, Lex-Corp counsel engaged, a juvenile-privacy exposure and a solicitation — to which Session 16 adds a minor left cuffed in a crime syndicate's warehouse.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** A diligent amateur, and diligence counts for a lot here. He quotes the Public Conduct and Powers-Use Standard from memory, keeps genuine chain-of-custody discipline, and walked into a police station to give a statement rather than being summoned. Against that, he argued statute with a corporate lawyer while unqualified, and as a minor he has no standing of his own. He took first in Session 15 on the vacancy left by the team's only professional investigator. **Against, in the same session:** he endorsed sending a villain team to break into a research facility. **The only member of this team who understands evidence voted for the burglary.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** Nothing changed.
2. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** The only member who worked out that this team needs professional lawyers and said so out loud. Session 13 added two protective instincts — that employing minors is unlawful in most states, and that a fourteen-year-old cannot consent to agreements adults float over his head — and Session 14 a standing pre-acceptance questionnaire. In Session 15 he analysed the juveniles' release and the garage break-in correctly, and then contracted the breaking and entering out with a killing attached. **Knowing where the line is and paying someone to cross it is still, technically, knowing where the line is.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** He stated the rule of engagement correctly — Nightwing *"did say we can bring them back, so knock them out"* — and then left a cuffed minor in a mob warehouse. The exposure is scored in 2.5.
3. **Oracle** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** No formal knowledge and correct instincts. Demanding surrender before engaging is, among other things, the legally correct posture, and he does it every time without being told. In Session 14 he reported a loss immediately, in his own words, to the principal.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** No engagement with the subject whatsoever.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `1.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No engagement with the subject. His one legal fact is a public identity that is *"not very well known."*

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `1.0`, broken on Logic.

### **5.7 Medical & Medical-Diagnostic**

1. **Oracle** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** The team medic in every sense that matters. He triages, treats, rations and makes the hard calls: in Session 9 he deliberately under-healed a teammate to preserve reserves for a raid. He heals Quasar-Force to full before every deployment, revived the man Intrepid nearly killed, and attempted to revive the man Quasar-Force killed. The cooking belongs on this axis too. Session 14 tested the supply chain rather than the capability (§4.15).
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** **The triage call of the session was his.** He put 15 Health into Quasar-Force at −30 knowing it would not bring him back, kept him within range of Let's Go, and then moved Let's Go to Lock-Step at the moment the team's exit depended on Lock-Step being conscious.
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Not a clinician; a strong diagnostician. He interpreted the medical report on the bane derivative, identified the non-terrestrial component nobody else spotted, and proposed the airborne-spore screening that caught three infections. In Session 14 he diagnosed his own panic response as foreign while it was happening. In Session 15 he was a co-investigator on his own biology with the League's specialist, and he remembered that *"we were able to cure the other kids"* — **the precedent that turns five infected teenagers into patients rather than casualties.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.5.** The five are still infected, and three of them are unconscious in a warehouse. The precedent has not been applied.
3. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Practical pharmacology first: he sedated a teammate twice at a dose that worked without visible harm. Session 14 brought the first therapeutic act of his career, a hex configured into healing and calibrated to *"the minimum amount allowed that will wake him up."* Dosing to the endpoint you want is the same skill as the sedative, pointed the other way for once.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** Not used.
4. **Intrepid** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** Aspirin, ice cream, and a working knowledge of what hurts.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `1.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A healing factor that works on him alone, and nothing for anyone else.

*Tie:* Intrepid over Edge at `1.5`, broken on Logic.

### **5.8 Stealth & Infiltration Capability**

1. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Teleportation *is* infiltration. He goes through walls, fences, guards and shielding, and he inserts the whole team behind a defended perimeter. Session 14 was the purest concealment-by-relocation on record: four insertions, three withdrawals, and not one enemy action directed at him. Against: he botched a blind teleport in Session 1, and he is loud by temperament. Session 15 took half a point for the man rather than the power: after a viral stream and a week of broadcasts he is the most recognisable face on the roster after Quasar-Force.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** Blink and teleport, in and out, all fight.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** Personally the least stealthy person on the roster — he glows, hovers and has a public identity — and **the owner of the team's entire infiltration toolkit**: stealth-mode drones with hyper-spectral vision, infrared reconnaissance, an EW blackout suite, and fake-noise-complaint tradecraft. Session 15 gave him the one concealment that matters most to him, a New God signal mask, and the best covert surveillance on his file.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.8.** He proposed the sneaky entry, and the team chose the ambush. Inside, he hid behind a shipping container, and Blitzkrieg found him: *"What's ya doing, Hiding?"*
3. **Edge** `4.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** His roster role is *Precision Marksman & Spatial Infiltrator*, and the sheet supports the first half. He has no stealth powers, white hair and eyes that glow, and a public identity. Agility 7 and Situational Awareness are the case for him; his real contribution to this axis is the opposite capability — making it hard for anyone else to sneak — which is scored in 5.9.
4. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Tactical gear and Vigilance 6, no stealth powers, and an Extreme Appearance that means anyone who sees him remembers him. He cased a museum for a full day without being made, which is the whole of the credit.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Six foot four, 301 pounds, a floor-length red cape and a metallic gold "I" on his chest. His Secret Identity protects his civilian life, not his approach. In Session 15 he tailed a limousine across the city without being spotted, in a red cape at altitude.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.

*⟨Note⟩ The team's counter-infiltration capability is back, on paper.* Last revision this note recorded that Heightened Senses 2 left with Exemplar II and that nothing on the four remaining sheets did what it did. **Edge carries the same power:** enemies have double trouble on checks to sneak past him. Nothing tested it in Session 16\. §10.4 carries the finding as answered on paper.

### **5.9 Sensory & Environmental Awareness**

1. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 6 with a Vigilance defence of 16, Danger Sense guaranteeing him a turn in every bonus round, Precognition 1 for a 24-hour lookahead, Intuition, Charmed Life, Real Jinx and Combat Reflexes. **He literally sees things before they happen.** Session 14 showed both halves: the module gave him the lead to dodge an attacker he could not see, and then he failed to pinpoint Veil, could not see through the fog, and was the only person in the room who did not know an astral figure was standing over Quasar-Force. **Precognition tells you what is about to happen. It does not tell you what is in the room.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** The forecasting half was right about the warehouse, and the module kept its Marvel on initiative.
2. **Edge** `8.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The best innate senses on the team, at the score Exemplar II left at.** Heightened Senses 2: four times normal range, the ability to compensate for a lost sense with the others, a heartbeat lie-detector, a double edge on Vigilance checks to perceive, and double trouble for anyone sneaking past him. Situational Awareness and Combat Reflexes sit beside it. Against it, a Vigilance of 3\. Second on arrival: **he tells you what is in the room, which is the half Oracle's module does not.**
3. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 5 and a sensor suite that outperforms most innate senses: hyper-spectral drones that defeated Shroud's darkness outright, a 21 on a camera sweep, a technopath detected from the drift of his own drones, and tactile awareness of objects at range through telekinetic contact. Session 14 was the first time the instrument model lost to an innate one — the villains did not show on infrared. He also has one sensory channel nobody else has: the box speaks to him.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** The infrared worked this time, and showed the five searching crates.
4. **Lock-Step** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 2, a Vigilance defence of 15 from Uncanny 3, and a genuinely good suspicion instinct: he identified the mentalist among five costumed villains, and flagged Iron-Side's convenient absence during the Destined incursion. He did not know his own building had a library.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Vigilance 3, a defence of 14, and no sensory powers. A 19 on museum watch is the only thing this axis has ever credited him with. He did know about the library.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** Nothing changed.

*⟨Note⟩ The best innate senses on the team are back.* Last revision ended this axis by saying that the team that next faced Veil or the Vapors of Galator had nobody who could see the room. **It now has one person who might**, and nobody has yet tested him.

### **5.10 Social Engineering, Espionage, Grifting & Interrogation**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 7 — the highest social ability score on the team by three points — and a record to match. He got the nightclub location out of a mob lieutenant over Polish cake, talked the Burnout Kings into standing aside, negotiated the Destined out of the lobby, and has produced repeated bloodless captures. **He is the only member of this team who is good with people.** His one genuine loss came in Session 14, when he tried to grift souvenirs off a founding member of the Justice League and ended up in the Lasso of Truth. Session 15 was the best social performance of the campaign across four very different targets: an alien apex-predator telepath worked to a settlement, a cosmic officer who had built the scenario to test him, a public audience set trending on an Ego check of 20, and Fade-Out brought in to do the job. **In Session 15 he was good with non-people as well.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** The cuffs almost worked on a mind-controlled telepath: Argent *"seems to start to 'accept'"* before her controller reasserted itself. With his own side, he got the most honest exchange of his file out of a conversation he started as a rant.
2. **Quasar-Force** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Not manipulation; arguably its opposite, and it works anyway in a narrow band. Compassion, reciprocal information-sharing and transparent sincerity get wronged people talking, and they produced the Guy Gardner channel. In Session 14 they turned a League specialist from an examiner into a collaborator inside one conversation. Session 15 produced *"an exchange of truth… with telepathy"* and got Wonder Woman and a Green Lantern to answer within minutes on a Saturday. Against hostile professionals he gets red-herringed. Ego −2; he cannot deceive and does not try.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.5.** He judged correctly that the five could not be talked to, and did not try.
3. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Ego 4, the second-highest social ability score on the team**, and a Monster trait that gives him an edge to intimidate. He used it once, on the two villains still standing, and *"they don't budge"* — against opponents whose will was not their own. With his new teammates he was tactful under a stranger's rant (*"I don't want to break your hope"*) and direct when it mattered (*"Ok, that told me nothing"*). Third on arrival, on the ability score and the trait rather than on a record.
4. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 0 and an Ego defence of 10\. He speaks plainly, demands surrender and coordinates clearly, and twice — in Sessions 12 and 14 — he did the most effective social act in the room by naming the thing everyone was talking around. On Kwa he conducted the interrogation that mattered, in two questions. Honest rather than persuasive, and occasionally that is the more useful of the two.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** *"Don't worry, the bar is low, it's alright"* was the right line for a newcomer; his briefing of the situation was not.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** Ego 0 and no social traits. His own contribution to one interrogation debrief was that Shadow-Boxer would have got nothing but fist from him. The most socially deft line in his file is *"Sometimes,"* his answer when the progenitor asked whether he was Superman.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** *"Yeah."*

### **5.11 Domestic Utility & Skills**

1. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** He is the household. He cooks buffet-style, adjusts the menu on request, times breakfast around who wakes when, and personally makes sure the fourteen-year-old actually eats. Nobody else contributes anything comparable, and when the household moved to a Justice League guest suite with a robot doing the catering, he was still the one telling people to rest.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** The session ends with the team going to the underground bunker to recover, on his heals.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Enormous utility and near-zero chores. Telekinesis is the ultimate domestic force multiplier: he moved Exemplar's entire office single-handedly and works objects at range by reflex. ⟨Partially OOC⟩ His suit self-cleans, self-repairs and manages waste, and in Session 14 it sealed a stab wound and cleaned the blood off itself before he reached the infirmary. He will not cook.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Cocoa Puffs, Instagram Day, popcorn with red licorice for a stakeout, and a standing promise of black licorice for Quasar-Force. Genuine morale value and zero reliability. His Session 14 contribution was establishing that the Justice League's space station has Wi-Fi.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** *"Maybe I just need another party."*
4. **Edge** `2.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** He has moved into bedroom 3, the room that was Exemplar II's, and nothing about his life in the tower is on record yet. A student with School as an Obligation and grades that were *"already bad."*
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** He makes his own oatmeal, breaks things, and offers to squeeze household objects until they fail. Pleasant, willing, and hazardous near anything fragile. He knows where the library is.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **5.12 Teaching & Mentorship**

1. **Oracle** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** He teaches by structure and by example. He runs the routine everyone else lives inside, he showed that a person on this team can say no to Lock-Step and survive it, and he is the person a younger teammate would actually approach. Session 15 gave him an explicit tutoring role: the module's resonance with Lock-Step's powers lets him *"give advice and assistive edges in learning to use any powers he may unlock in the future,"* and he gave the first lesson. **The teammate a younger member would approach is now the designated tutor for the most volatile power set on the team.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.5.** His one teaching moment was a briefing for the newcomer — an alien influence, and *"we are trying to change the course of how that alien evolves to not become a nemesis"* — and the newcomer's verdict was *"Ok, that told me nothing."* ⟨Flag⟩ Edge's roster tags list *Mentor (PC: Oracle)*. His backstory reads as Barbara Gordon (*"she called the Birds"*), and nothing in Session 16 shows the two had met. If the tag is meant, it is a second tutoring role; it is not scored until play shows it.
2. **Quasar-Force** `7.1`
   * **All-time.** He explains constantly and enthusiastically to anyone who will listen, shares intelligence unprompted, and models genuine curiosity. In Session 13 he briefed Lock-Step on the one threat that endangered him alone, and in Session 14 he corrected a Justice League scientist's professional manner and she changed how she spoke to the room. In Session 15 he corrected Lock-Step's plan to take over a hive. **He is undercut by being the youngest person present and reading younger still** — *"Is she going to listen to you? Like no offense? We're like kids."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.1.** **He gave the newcomer the one-line version Oracle could not:** *"If he has his way, the whole planet will become part of his brood."*
3. **Lock-Step** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** He established the surrender ritual as de facto team doctrine, which is teaching whether or not he meant it as such. His Session 14 instruction to a teammate was that he was a coward, repeated until it worked. Session 15 was better: a principle (*"You don't punish the \[bunch\] for one bad apple"*), which Quasar-Force took, and an unprompted *"consent is everything."* Against: *"That's why I don't get a job, kids, live your life."*
   * **Session 16: 4.5 → 4.0 (−0.5).** **The first lesson he gave the newcomer was that the adults are trash.** In his first minute with Edge he taught that *"all the adults are trash,"* that Iron-Side *"lived in the security room,"* that the adults had told a twelve-year-old to fix things (Quasar-Force: *"You're mainly the one that told me to fix it"*), and that Nightwing had not briefed him (he had). Against it, the most useful thing anyone taught Edge that afternoon was also his: the rule of engagement, *"we can bring them back, so knock them out."*
4. **Edge** `2.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Black Canary's student, with Quick Learner on his sheet. He has nothing to teach yet, and he was the one doing the learning.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** Nothing to teach, and no inclination to try.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **5.13 Empathic / Emotional Support**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.4`
   * **All-time.** **Compassion is his defining trait, and it works on people who have no reason to respond to it.** Shadow-Boxer opened up to him, Cassidy Yanks gave her real name because he was kind, and World Ender's facade collapsed under sincerity. In Session 13 he told a seventeen-year-old who had just been used as a weapon that his mother loves him. In Session 14 he aimed it at an alien machine intelligence with PTSD, and it landed. In Session 15 its main recipient was the entity inside him: he asked her permission before naming her and made her comfort a condition of the research.
   * **Session 16: 9.2 → 9.4 (+0.2).** **He reached the teammate who never lets anyone in.** Against Lock-Step's run of *"Adults just suck"* he said, *"I like being hopeful. I want to help people get their happy thoughts back. You are not keeping your happy thoughts anymore."* What came back was the first explanation of his cynicism Lock-Step has ever given — *"I just hope that one day, you get my happy thoughts back. Adults being adults and actually being helpful"* — and Quasar-Force answered it with a yacht party. He does it without technique, which is why it lands.
2. **Oracle** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The team's caretaker in the full sense. He cooks around individual appetites, heals people to full before deployment, notices who is not eating, and holds the space where injured people end up talking. His protectiveness toward Quasar-Force is the clearest emotional attachment anyone on this team has expressed. In Session 13 he thanked Psyche for not letting the universe be destroyed, a sentence that did more work than any heal he has cast.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** He put the newcomer at ease with one line, kept Quasar-Force on the board through the deepest knockdown of his file, and healed him afterwards.
3. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A tactful first afternoon. Met with a stranger's grievance about adults, he answered with care — *"I don't want to break your hope,"* *"I'm sorry to hear that"* — rather than with agreement or argument. In the fight his support was practical, not emotional. Third on arrival on two lines; his backstory says he went looking for help when he needed it, which is a good sign for someone asked to give it.
4. **Lock-Step** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Socially warm in a real way — the licorice, the popcorn, the video games — and attached to a willingness to override other people's autonomy that undoes most of it. Session 14 held the warmest and coldest things he had done, an hour apart. Session 15 held two of the warmest acts on his file: asking Quasar-Force unprompted whether he consented to being studied (*"consent is everything"*), and going back to check on the refugee before leaving. Against, the same pattern as always: teammates as content, and five teenagers' faces on the internet for laughs.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** For: a warm welcome to a stranger, and the first consent question asked in a fight. Against: that welcome was also a lecture on how the adults had failed, and mid-fight he told the team that this is what happens when he stays with it.
5. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** Cheerful, agreeable, and emotionally unavailable in both directions. He is going through something and has told nobody. The closest he has come was *"I feel like a coward"* in the middle of a Session 14 argument, which nobody in the room treated as a disclosure, including him.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** Nothing in either direction.

### **5.14 Team Coordination / Interoperability**

*Working with powers, styles, and skillsets that are not your own.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** He has found a use for every teammate's kit: nudging Intrepid's strikes, placing Oracle on request, funnelling enemies toward the brawlers, handing negotiations to Lock-Step, and feeding the whole team drone reconnaissance before entry. In Session 13 a drone he built became the visualisation source for a teleport he cannot perform, and in Session 15 he built two drones for a teammate's use. **He adapts to other people's abilities more readily than to his own limitations.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** The drone sweep fed the plan, and he aided the newcomer's first shot. The grab on Argent was the platform for Lock-Step's finish.
2. **Oracle** `8.8`
   * **All-time.** He interoperates with everyone by construction: he heals whoever is hurt, buffs whoever is failing, and broadcasts warnings to the whole team. In Session 14, at 3 Focus and inside a grab, he redirected a teammate's swing off another teammate and onto the boss. Session 15's resonance gave him a sanctioned interface with Lock-Step's powers — coordination grounded in two characters' powers being compatible.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.8.** His module turned Intrepid's stomp into a hit, his Focus damage finished what Edge's had started on Dragonborne, and he aided the newcomer's first shot.
3. **Lock-Step** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** He physically integrates the team better than anyone, and socially degrades the advantage by moving people without consent. Session 14 was his best-integrated session: input from every teammate in one combat, all of it converted. In Session 15 he fitted the Kwa approach around Oracle's condition and built a plan across three parties. **Against:** the interoperability he arranged with the progenitor's network was imposed on two teammates rather than offered to them.
   * **Session 16: 7.3 → 7.8 (+0.5).** **The stated deduction came off.** He asked before moving a teammate — *"Who is comfortable where they are at and who wants to move a little bit"* — took Intrepid's no, and came back for him later. He put Oracle inside Argent's sphere and finished the target Quasar-Force was holding, aided Edge's first shot, and ran an extraction that depended on Oracle's heals and Edge's rear guard.
4. **Edge** `7.3`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Fitted in from the first round.** His target call was built around the team's kit, his first shot was aided by all four teammates, he turned two of Intrepid's attacks into better ones, his Focus damage set up Oracle's demoralise, and his rear guard bought Lock-Step the turns he needed. Level on arrival with Lock-Step's Session 15 score.
5. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** He accepts positioning guidance from Oracle and Quasar-Force without ego, which is rarer and more valuable than it sounds in a character with this much raw power. In Session 14 the traffic ran both ways for the first time. In Session 15 he backed whoever was on point.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.0.** He aided Edge's first shot, took Edge's aid twice, and let Oracle's module steer his stomp.

### **5.15 Cross-Team / Institutional Alliance Capital**

*Ability to obtain resources from, and coordinate with, outside organisations.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.7`
   * **All-time.** **Built entirely from nothing.** A working relationship with Blüdhaven PD, STAR Labs access through his father and in his own right, two loans of suppression cuffs, direct contact with Wonder Woman, an open channel to a serving Green Lantern, and a habit of posting intelligence to public superhero forums. Session 14 added a personal sanctuary offer from a founding member of the Justice League, a week of Watchtower access, and Doctor Tal Taleria volunteering to build him a signal mask. In Session 15 Wonder Woman answered his communicator at once and came to the tower in person, Guy Gardner picked up mid-fight, and the specialist offered to come back to Earth with him. ⟨OOC⟩ Also, unwillingly, a line to ARGUS. **He is the team's institutional switchboard.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.7.** Nothing used, nothing lost. ⟨Flag⟩ The roster still lists *"Exemplar's Dad's Lab"* among his lab-access tags; with Exemplar II gone, the tag should be treated as stale.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.2`
   * **All-time.** Extensive alliance capital, much of it in the wrong directions. The Marrow Syndicate will sit down with him, the Destined negotiate through him, the Burnout Kings stood aside for him, and three of the most significant magical figures on Earth hold positions on his future. In Session 14 his capital went measurably negative for the first time. Session 15 added alliances nobody else on this team could have made: the Grey Lanterns, the people of Kwa, the Destined (who now hold his words), and an audience he can move. Against: the Justice League, whose station he livestreamed without asking.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.2.** The Marrow Syndicate warehouse is now the place he left a cuffed teenager. The Destined errand has not reported back.
3. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs built his body and Nightwing takes his calls. Nightwing arranged him a private appointment with a STAR Labs board member, and in Session 14 he held the direct line to Wonder Woman in a crisis and used it. The capital is real, narrow and largely inherited, and its principal holder, Nightwing, has been absent from the screen since Session 14\.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** A third session without Nightwing in the room. The team briefed Wonder Woman.
4. **Edge** `6.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The newcomer arrived with a network.** Black Canary volunteered to train him, which brings the Birds of Prey; in his backstory it was *Oracle* who found him online and *"called the Birds"*; and Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited him and briefed him personally. That is a direct line into the Bat-family and a League-adjacent hero, held by someone who has been on the team for an afternoon. ⟨Flag⟩ Which Oracle found him is the open question in §2.6 and 5.12.
5. **Intrepid** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing, and that is the complete list. The Watchtower stay put him inside the Justice League's headquarters for about a week and he came out with nothing. Since Session 15 he has a social channel with a thousand followers he did not ask for.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.5.** Nothing changed.

### **5.16 Overall Non-Combat Value / Contribution**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** Most of what this team accomplishes outside a fight runs through him: engineering, laboratory forensics, the drone network, the evidence chain, the police liaison, data custody across three sites, and a telekinetic domestic multiplier. **Remove him and the Titans stop producing intelligence.** Session 15 was the clearest demonstration yet — three builds, the investigation that tied the juveniles to a STAR Labs executive, the drone net, and the communications with every outside party that could help. Remove him now and the Titans also stop being hidden from the thing hunting one of them.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.3.** The intelligence before the door opened was his.
2. **Oracle** `8.2`
   * **All-time.** Value counts what actually gets delivered, and Oracle delivers every day: medicine, the household, awareness, perfect recall, logistics, the Nightwing channel, the STAR Labs relationship and the museum reconnaissance. In Session 15 his deliveries were the most specific forecast of his campaign and the first working explanation of what a teammate is.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.2.** The plan, the forecast that put the team at the right warehouse, and the heals that sent everyone to the bunker on their own feet.
3. **Lock-Step** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** The social layer is genuinely load-bearing and invisible on a capability chart: the Marrow Syndicate détente, the Burnout Kings standing down, the party access, the negotiated surrenders, and the weekly stipend he extracted for the whole team. **Without him this team has no diplomacy at all.** Session 15 was the proof at cosmic scale: a species exists because of a conversation he insisted on having.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** The rule of engagement and the offers of surrender were his, and so was the most honest conversation of the afternoon.
4. **Edge** `4.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Before the fight he made the team plan and gave it its target. Outside it, his value is Heightened Senses 2 on paper and a network into the Bat-family and the Birds. Logic −1 caps the rest.
5. **Intrepid** `1.5`
   * **All-time.** He patrols and finds things by walking around, which is not nothing. Session 14 added accurate witness testimony and a late, correct question about research, and Session 15 a tail and the library.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.5.** Nothing changed.

### **5.17 Overall Non-Combat Power / Potency**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** First place has been his on the strength of breadth at the top end. He leads engineering, lab forensics, cyber, coordination and alliance capital, with high placings almost everywhere else, and is weak only in social, where he is effective in one sincere mode, and in law, where he is a diligent amateur. Session 15 made him first on nine of the fifteen component axes plus both aggregates.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.3.** Still eleven firsts across seventeen: Leadership, Engineering, Cyber, Field Investigation, Lab Forensics, Law, Empathic Support, Coordination, Alliance Capital, and both aggregates.
2. **Oracle** `7.4`
   * **All-time.** Medical 9.5, Sensory Awareness 9.0, Domestic Utility 9.0 and Empathic Support 9.0, with Leadership above 8: several non-combat axes at 9.0 or above, which nobody but Quasar-Force matches. He loses the technical axes to the specialists around him and wins breadth on what he owns outright.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.4.** First on Medical, Sensory, Domestic and Teaching. **The breadth that once won him this section still wins him second.**
3. **Lock-Step** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Elite in one dimension and near-absent in every technical one. He has risen because the non-technical dimensions keep turning out to matter: human intelligence, diplomacy, the only mission-vetting proposal anyone has made, and a medical capability nobody knew he had. In Session 15 the one dimension got larger: social engineering at 9.5, alliance capital at 7.2, and the first demonstrated information-operations capability on the team.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.5.** First on Stealth and Social Engineering.
4. **Edge** `3.8`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No firsts. Second on Sensory, third on Stealth, Social Engineering and Empathic Support, fourth on six more, and last on the five Logic-driven axes where his Logic of −1 breaks a tie with Intrepid's 0\. **A combat specialist's non-combat profile, with one first-rank instrument in it.**
5. **Intrepid** `1.6`
   * **All-time.** He patrols and finds things by walking around. At Session 14 he placed last on fifteen of the seventeen non-combat axes, and at Session 15 on all seventeen.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.6.** **Last on twelve of the seventeen.** The other five last places are Edge's, and they are tie-breaks on Logic, not gains.

*⟨Note⟩ The vacancy, partly filled.* Session 15 recorded that this section had lost its third-best member and that nothing had replaced him. Edge enters fourth on both aggregates, behind Lock-Step, with first-rank capability on Sensory and nowhere else. **The section's third place is still Lock-Step's, and the gap Exemplar II left in the technical middle is still open.**

## **6\. Capability Robustness & Conditionality**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*How well capability survives contact with adverse conditions: preparation denied, powers suppressed, countermeasures applied, support removed. Session 16 tested three of these axes directly. A power was stolen from a Titan for the first time, three different counters landed on three different members, and the one member who was alone when the enemy found him was removed in a single roll.*

### **6.1 Ability to Leverage Prep-Time**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.8`
   * **All-time.** Inventor, Scientific Expertise, Logic 7 and Brilliance 4, plus access to three separate laboratories including an air-gapped underground physics lab. Give him a day and he comes back with drone modifications, a rebuilt security architecture, an evidence package or a decoded artifact. **Prep-time is the condition under which he is most dangerous.** Session 15 was the purest conversion on his file: he named the order of work three times, then built a New God signal mask, an adaptive anti-teleport device and a nanosuit upgrade, in that order, in six days. **Give him a target and a week and he comes back with three builds.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.8.** The prep window was minutes, and he used it: a drone sweep that found the five searching crates, and a deduction about their powers.
2. **Oracle** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** **Precognition 1 is prep-time as a superpower.** Twenty-four hours of lookahead is a planning window nobody else has, and Eidetic Memory means nothing he prepares is ever forgotten. Session 13 turned a prep window into a product, a complete entrance map and staffing assessment on three days' notice. Session 15 converted the lookahead into a plan: an actor, a probability, a collision window and a destination, which Lock-Step summarised as *"working backward from screwed."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** The forecast put the team at the right warehouse. At the door he had *"No plan. Not on this one,"* and then a teleport ambush.
3. **Lock-Step** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Powerful Hex can be configured in advance, which is real preparation. Temperamentally he improvises and skips the briefing. In Session 14 he argued *for* preparation for the first time, proposing the pre-mission questionnaire, and in Session 15 he did it on Kwa: a scout, an exit, a healer at his elbow and an opening question.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** He named his own role in the preparation — *"I'm just the if it all goes to hell"* — and left the planning to Oracle.
4. **Edge** `2.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Logic −1 and nothing on the sheet that converts time into advantage, except Heightened Senses 2 given time to listen. **He is the member who asked for the plan**, and he came to the door already briefed by Nightwing. Above Intrepid on the instinct.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** Nothing on his sheet converts time into advantage; he is exactly as capable after a week of planning as after ten seconds. In Session 14 his only contribution to a preparation discussion was to ask, afterwards, whether anyone had done any research.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** *"Nothing, I guess."*

### **6.2 Ability to Function Without Powers**

1. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Ego 7 and the best social capability on the team are not powers. Depowered, he still talks his way into the nightclub, still gets the location out of the lieutenant, and still runs the room. Session 14 was a full session of him operating almost entirely on personality inside an institution where none of his powers were any use. **Session 15 was the proof at the largest scale available**: the encounter that decided the fate of a species was a conversation.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.5.** Two offers of surrender that nearly worked on a controlled telepath, and the most honest conversation of the afternoon — none of it a power.
2. **Quasar-Force** `5.5`
   * **All-time.** Logic 7 and Brilliance 4 survive suppression, and an intact Quasar-Force mind is still the best analyst in Blüdhaven. What does not survive is the body: an 84-pound fourteen-year-old with Melee −2. ⟨OOC⟩ **For him, "without powers" means without the mother box, not without telekinesis**, and Taleria confirmed in Session 15 that she cannot point to where he ends and the box begins. **For him, "without powers" now means without Stella.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `4.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Agility 7 is an ability, not a power, and Black Canary trained him *"all-out."* Depowered, he is a fast fifteen-year-old with real martial training, Ego 4 and Logic −1. **Third on arrival**, behind the two members whose minds are the asset.
4. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** The hardest case on the axis. Vigilance 6 and Eidetic Memory persist as faculties, but **his powers are not equipment he carries; they are the body he lives in**, and the cybernetics keep him upright. "Without powers" for Oracle may not describe a survivable state.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Intrepid** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** His mutant physiology *is* the character. Remove it and there is a sixteen-year-old with Logic 0, Agility 0, Vigilance 3 and no skills on the sheet.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** Nothing changed.

### **6.3 Power Independence / Lack of Single Point of Failure**

1. **Intrepid** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Innate mutant physiology with no device, no artifact, no patron and no external dependency of any kind. For fifteen sessions this entry said **there is nothing to confiscate, disable or negotiate for**, and that his powers were the one thing about him that could not be taken.
   * **Session 16: 9.0 → 8.5 (−0.5).** **Session 16 found something to confiscate.** Blood Blade's Swipe took Sturdy 3 off him on a hit and used it herself for two rounds; he got it back only when a stun broke her concentration. Innate powers have no single point of failure, but they can be taken one at a time by an opponent built to do it. He stays first, because nothing else on the roster is as independent.
2. **Edge** `8.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The second-most independent kit on the roster.** A Monster origin, so his powers are what he is, and a weapon that is Constructible, so taking it from him buys an enemy very little. No patron, no device, no box. What keeps him behind Intrepid is that his attack powers are weapon powers, and that his origin is a legend nobody, including him, fully understands.
3. **Oracle** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Hardware rather than biology, which is a dependency, but distributed across an integrated system rather than concentrated in one component. STAR Labs is a maintenance dependency, not an on-switch. In Session 15 the module had its first malfunction not caused by an enemy, under temporal transport, and it now resonates with a teammate's time-altering powers. **The hardware has a sensitivity to time phenomena, and one of his teammates has turned out to be a time phenomenon.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** The module worked all fight. ⟨Volatile⟩ The device the brood came for was keyed to Lock-Step's time manipulation; what it would have done near Oracle's module is unknown.
4. **Lock-Step** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** Stacked dependencies. **The Vibranium chain whip supplies half his damage multiplier**, and Protection Hex is a single power carrying his entire defensive profile. Summonable protects the first; nothing protects the second. Healing came out of Powerful Hex too, so **everything that makes him versatile and everything that makes him fragile is the same power.** Session 15 added a third dependency, the power source itself: lantern-derived, linked to Oa and the Grey Lanterns, with a ring that will not charge until he remembers an oath. **An outside order now has a stake in what powers him.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** A Marrow Syndicate warehouse held a device keyed to his time manipulation. If the device was built to act on his power source, that is a fourth dependency; nobody knows, and it no longer exists.
5. **Quasar-Force** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** One object. It cannot be forcibly removed without killing him, and it cannot be replaced. Every power he has flows through a single Apokoliptian-modified mother box with its own damaged personality and survival drive, and Session 14 showed it can reduce output on its own initiative. **Both of his Iconic Items — the circlet and the upgraded nanosuit — carry the same two flaws, *Worn* and *Requires Origin X-Element Mother Box***, so every capability he added in Session 15 runs through the same object as everything he already had.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** **The single point held against the one attack built to exploit points like it.** Swipe, which stole a power from Intrepid, failed against his fortified mind. That is the hardware working as designed, and it is scored in 4.2 and 6.4. The dependency is unchanged.

### **6.4 Resilience Against Countermeasures & Neutralization**

1. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Protection Hex routes every incoming attack and effect through an Ego defence of 20, so there is no soft face. Argent's mind control failed twice, and Dargrave's mind-read failed outright. Powerful Hex means a counter that works one round may not apply the next. In Session 14 not a single enemy action was directed at him successfully across four turns against an Olympian goddess. Session 15 recorded the first way past the Hex (the progenitor, *"with ease"*) and closed a hole in the other direction: Elemental Infusion (Force) means phasing no longer makes a target immune to him.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** The Hex did its job — he evaded Argent's telekinetic sphere through it — and a Marvel-20 glitch bomb went through it anyway. One perfect roll is not a countermeasure; it is the dice.
2. **Edge** `6.2`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **He arrives carrying the answer to the counter that beat the team in Session 14\.** Heightened Senses 2 compensates for a lost sense, puts double trouble on anyone sneaking past him, and gives him a double edge to perceive — the capability that saw through invisibility and obscurement when Exemplar II had it. Slow-Motion Dodge strips edges from attacks on his Agility defence, he has damage reduction on both tracks, and his powers are innate. Against it, mental defences of 15 to 16, and nothing in Session 16 tested the senses. ⟨Volatile⟩ Second on paper.
3. **Quasar-Force** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Viral catalogued his mind as unusually resilient after failing to enter it, while Psyche's method worked twice. Session 14 added a cruder and better countermeasure: 60 Health, no healing, and no way to perceive an invisible attacker, so one strike from concealment ends his participation. Session 15 narrowed the telepathic route with the circlet's Uncanny 3 and answered Azimuth's search with the mask. **What remains is physical, and it is the countermeasure that actually worked.** ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ The circlet is *Worn*: knock it off and the mental defences drop by three, the mask ends, and a New God can find him again.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.0.** **Both halves, in one fight.** The mental countermeasure failed against him: Swipe could not get through his fortified mind. The physical one worked twice: a Marvel and a reaction strike through his damage reduction, and a glitch bomb that ignores it.
4. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Resilient by sheer margin: the largest Health pool on the team, the highest physical defences, H-DR 3, Iron Will, Fearless, Enduring Constitution and a healing factor. **There is no clever answer to Intrepid, only a sufficient one** — and the sufficient answers have never been damage. In Session 13 his grab did nothing while Umbra escaped; in Session 14 a force field made his grapple payload irrelevant and an area stun removed him; in Session 15 an alien hive mind took him on a 14-Marvel against his Vigilance of 14\.
   * **Session 16: 6.5 → 6.0 (−0.5).** **Beaten by something other than damage for the fourth consecutive session.** This time it was a power thief. Swipe took his Sturdy 3 — the damage reduction and the defensive boost that make up much of his margin — and the opponent who took it used it against him. A sufficient answer to Intrepid never needs to be clever; it only needs not to be a punch.
5. **Oracle** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** The most completely countered member of the team. His Ego defence of 10 was flagged for five revisions as the softest door on the roster, alongside an unrepaired write channel into his module. Then in Session 14 Ecliptica beat him with two moves that used neither weakness: an elemental grab he owns no power to escape, and **an area obscurement that removed his ranged specialism, his overwatch and his ability to locate anything.** He was held, blinded and drained from 180 Focus to 3 by an opponent who never attacked his body or his Ego. Session 15 added a telepath reading his purpose and a module degraded by temporal displacement.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** **The brood found a third counter.** Dragonborne spent a reaction to turn Oracle's Return Fire onto Lock-Step, so the reaction that punishes attackers hit a teammate instead. The grab-and-drain was not used. ⟨Flag⟩ **Two advances banked since Session 14 and undeclared for a third session.** Any mental power that projects strength breaks the grab, and Uncanny raises the soft door.

*Tie:* Quasar-Force over Intrepid at `6.0`, broken on Session 16 evidence: the same theft that took Intrepid's power failed against Quasar-Force's mind.

### **6.5 Power Failure Mode Lack-of-Severity**

*What happens when it goes wrong. High \= benign failure.*

1. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** A missed shot hits a wall, a failed heal costs Focus, and **his powers cannot kill anyone even when they work**, which makes his offensive failure modes structurally benign. Session 14 was the most complete failure he has had, and he absorbed all of the consequences himself. The structural concern dates from Session 12: the module accepts information from outside itself and renders it as its own output. In Session 15 he failed the Logic check on a forecast and still produced a figure the team planned around. **A module whose failures look like results is benign only as long as someone remembers which roll produced them.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** His one failure of the fight was induced by an enemy: Return Fire, redirected onto Lock-Step for 13 Focus. It was Focus damage, as everything he does is, and Lock-Step stayed up.
2. **Intrepid** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Ground-Shaking Stomp is indiscriminate by design, and he has come within one roll of killing ordinary humans three separate times. The failure mode is manslaughter, mitigated only by the fact that everyone, including him, can see it coming. Melee 7 and a ×7 \+7 swing (Session 15\) make the failure a point larger.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** The one stomp of the fight hit only an enemy (4.16). The failure mode is unchanged and was not triggered.
3. **Edge** `3.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** On the sheet, a marksman's failures are a miss and, on Double Tap's Fantastic, bleeding. **The real question is the power behind the weapon**: it slices through objects by concentration, it arrived with constant headaches and eyes that glow, and the backstory says the monster it belongs to *"cut down armies."* Black Canary volunteered to train him because the power is dangerous. ⟨Volatile⟩ Nobody has seen it fail. Third on arrival, on what the roster says it could do.
4. **Quasar-Force** `2.0`
   * **All-time.** His powers fail loudly. Crush produced more than twice a target's maximum Health and killed a man. Precision failures put a teammate in a toxic cloud. Commandeered by a telepath, his telekinesis brought down the main containment field on a device STAR Labs says could compromise the stability of the universe. **His ceiling — cruise-ship tonnage with no check — means a bad failure is not a casualty but an event.** In Session 15 he raised the box's trauma himself, under a telepath's read, and the box held.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.0.** The crush on Stote was calibrated to a knockout (4.17). No failure.
5. **Lock-Step** `1.8`
   * **All-time.** Berserk is a failure mode with no upper bound and no off switch, and it has fired twice. Blind teleportation once dropped him alone into a room with ten monsters. Doctor Fate's entire objection is that his failures scale past the city. Session 15 changed the diagnosis: his powers work by *"unconsciously altering time waves to make things into reality."* ⟨OOC⟩ Time powers are on the GM's banned list for a reason. **A chaos mage whose effects are unconscious edits to time is a failure mode nobody at this table has a ceiling for.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.8.** No Berserk, no blind teleport, and a fifth clean engagement. ⟨Volatile⟩ A device keyed to his time manipulation was in the warehouse, and nobody knows what it would have done with him.

### **6.6 Solo Operating Capability**

1. **Lock-Step** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Self-inserting, self-extracting, nearly unhittable behind Protection Hex, and socially capable of talking his way through anything he cannot teleport past. He already operates alone by preference and skips team activities to do it. Session 14 added a heal he can configure for himself. Session 15 cut against the profile twice without moving the score: his mental armour has been walked through once, and his face now trends.
   * **Session 16: 8.0 → 7.5 (−0.5).** **The solo operator was alone when the enemy found him.** He had struck and teleported clear, and Blitzkrieg teleported out after him and took him from 60 to −24 in one roll. Nobody was there to keep him up; Oracle's Let's Go reached him only at the end. His Round 2 complaint — *"This is what happens when I stay with the team. I should have vanished"* — got its answer two rounds later: the hit that dropped him came when he had vanished.
2. **Edge** `6.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The best solo profile on arrival after Lock-Step's.** He heals himself, his powers are innate and his weapon is constructible, he dodges, and Heightened Senses 2 means he will find the fight and know who is lying to him in it. In Session 16 he did the one solo job the fight produced: he held off Blitzkrieg alone for a round. Against it, Logic −1, 90 Health and no movement power.
3. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** A huge Health pool and fast regeneration make him extremely hard to kill alone. Logic 0, Vigilance 3 and no investigative capability mean he would never find anyone to fight. In Session 15 he did his first solo task, tailing a limousine across the city. He still needs somebody else to find the fight.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.0.** He stayed alone in the fight by choice and went down twice in it.
4. **Oracle** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Self-healing, precognitive and perceptive, with no mobility power at all and 90 Health. Session 13 established that, sent out alone, he comes back with a complete reconnaissance product. What he cannot do alone is get anywhere or escape anything; in Session 14, pinned in place, there was nothing in his own kit to call.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** His healing factor got him up from −2 on his own. Getting him out of the building took Lock-Step.
5. **Quasar-Force** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Brilliant, mobile and made of paper: 60 Health, no healing of any kind, and recent sessions ending at 1, 4, −11 and −12 Health. Session 14 executed this axis's thesis in one round: he acted alone once, and the fight ended for him. **He is the most capable person on this team and the least able to operate without it.** In Session 15 the circlet removed most of the second floor, since Azimuth now has to find him without a signal.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** **The thesis again.** At −5 in the opening exchange he stayed in the fight only because Oracle's Let's Go held him up, and from −30 he stayed on the board only within Oracle's range.

## **7\. Overall Power / Ability / Value**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

### **7.1 Versatility & Adaptability**

1. **Lock-Step** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Powerful Hex means his power list is *any power he has the rank for, prerequisites ignored.* In play it has produced Quick Phase, Teleport Blind, Teleport Together, Disrupt Person, Real Jinx, a sleep effect and, in Session 14, healing hands. **Nobody else on this team can change what they are mid-combat**, and in Session 14 he changed what he was *after* combat, into the one role the team had no backup for. Session 15 added four roles that use no power at all — first-contact diplomat, host, recruiter of outside help and broadcaster — and a fifth nobody at the table understands yet: a Grey Lantern in training.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** At the ceiling this scale allows him. In one fight he was the negotiator, the striker who finished the first target, the rescuer, and the team's transport out.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** One power set applied to everything: attack, defence, restraint, barriers, flight and heavy lifting; disarming, handcuffing, moving vehicles, pulling bodies out of gas, escaping webbing with telekinesis as raw strength, and playing an unfamiliar NBA game. **His versatility comes from imagination, not options, which means it scales with the player rather than the sheet.** Session 15 extended the list away from telekinesis entirely: fabricator of New God–grade hardware, research collaborator on his own biology, surveillance operator, vehicle-registration investigator, and the team's switchboard to the League and the Corps.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.3.** Reconnaissance, analysis, a grab through a force field, a pin and a crush, all inside four turns.
3. **Oracle** `7.0`
   * **All-time.** Five distinct battlefield jobs — ranged attacker, healer, reviver, morale restorer, and scout and overwatch — plus field reconnaissance without any relevant trait. Session 14 was the counter-case: pinned and blinded, most of those jobs became unavailable at once, because they share a single dependency on line of sight and the ability to stand somewhere. Session 15 added two roles off the field: a live network interface, and the designated tutor for a teammate's unlockable powers.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.0.** **All five jobs in one fight**, including the one he does least: he took the planner's chair when it was pointed at him.
4. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A specialist with more range than the word suggests: two damage tracks (Health through Stopping Power and Double Tap, Focus through Suppressive Fire), a called shot that disarmed a phaser, a rear guard, and a sensory role nobody else on the team can fill. He has not yet been shown doing a second job off the battlefield.
5. **Intrepid** `2.3`
   * **All-time.** One job, done superbly. Session 14 was the first session in which the job itself came apart into pieces that failed separately. Session 15 gave him the first second job of his file: on Day 56 he tailed a vehicle across the city by air, competently.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.3.** The one job, again.

### **7.2 Power Mastery / Control**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Explicitly noted as very gifted at gaining precision and control, and the play backs it. He flies constantly for the pleasure of it, disarms individually, cuffs a sleeping man without waking him, guides a teammate's fist into a target, and dodges with telekinetically assisted agility. **The fly-by-wire framing means his ceiling is bounded by what he thinks to try.** In Session 15 Taleria told him *"Your powers are her communication to you"* and prescribed the training regime, and he designed the first experiment: a suspended marker, *"like ouija board stuff."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** **A grab through a force field, and a crush sized to a knockout.** He took Argent through the field she raised against him and held her against her own telekinetic strength, and he crushed Stote — a person he had crushed before — unconscious rather than dead.
2. **Oracle** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** He uses his module correctly every time. He holds actions, reassesses targets on precognitive input, and in Session 13 used the module as a counter-stealth instrument nobody had thought of. Session 14 showed high reliability meeting a hard limit, and Session 15 two more limits — the module faltering under temporal transport, and a forecast from a failed Logic check that he reported honestly (*"I don't think I necessarily got to the death part"*). **Knowing exactly what your instrument did and did not tell you is mastery.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.8.** Charmed Life turned his opening Marvel into a super-Marvel on the right target, and the module steered a teammate's stomp onto the right enemy.
3. **Edge** `6.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Mastery of the weapon is on record; mastery of the power is not.** He made a called shot at a hand-held device, with trouble for the disarm, on a phaser at the wall, and hit. He chose Suppressive Fire three times when Stopping Power would have done more Health damage, which is a throttle used on purpose. Against it, the power behind the weapon arrived with constant headaches and is dangerous enough that Black Canary volunteered to train him. ⟨Volatile⟩ Third on arrival.
4. **Intrepid** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** Improving visibly — he takes positioning guidance before stomping rather than swinging blind — and his own stated fear is that he cannot control it. The evidence supports him. In Session 14 he correctly declined to punch through a floor, and then committed a full action to a restraint that had already failed once against the same class of defence.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.0.** The improvement showed once: the stomp went where Oracle's module pointed it, and hit only an enemy (4.16). One use is not mastery.
5. **Lock-Step** `3.5`

   * **All-time.** Chaos magic he has not mastered: two Berserk episodes he could not control, a botched blind teleport, and a hex system whose power comes from its unpredictability. It has been improving. In Session 14 he produced a configuration he had never used, on demand, **dosed deliberately to the minimum that would work** — the first time he chose the size of an effect. Zatanna's position is that he can be *trained*, which concedes that he currently isn't. Session 15 was the session in which he found out he does not know what his powers are: lantern-derived, time-altering and *unconscious*. *"So I'm like a grey lantern, but like in training."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** His most precise teleport yet — Oracle placed inside Argent's sphere — and no Berserk. The question Session 15 opened, what his powers actually are, did not come up.

### **7.3 Growth Trajectory / Remaining Headroom**

*⟨Note on the Grey Lantern's perceptions.⟩ On arrival in Session 15 the time-travelling Grey Lantern identified the four of them as Justice League members: Intrepid as Superman, Lock-Step as Green Lantern, Quasar-Force as Captain Atom, Oracle as Batman. Within the hour one had been literally confirmed — Lock-Step is a lantern — and a mind-reading alien told Intrepid he is "remembered as Intrepid, but… called Superman." This document treats all four as ⟨Volatile⟩ GM foreshadowing: not evidence of capability, but not nothing either. Session 16 added nothing on either side. It did add a fifth item of the same kind: Edge's legend, an ancient monster reborn, with a title on the roster to match — "The Reborn Calamity."*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.7`
   * **All-time.** The steepest curve on the team and the most consequential. The mechanical half is a published Rank 3→6 plan that ends in spacetime manipulation, the longest runway on the roster. The unmechanical half is the mother box, and Session 15 moved it further than any session before: *"When you learn, she learns,"* which is the roster's planned *Awakening* beginning on screen; Azimuth's view that he is *"beginning to access the source in a way he only imagined"*; and the Grey Lantern's *"no longer merely human."* ⟨Ruling⟩ Rank 3.5 went to two Iconic Items, the house rule now closes Telekinetic Protection, and Energy Absorption is the remaining Rank-4 survivability purchase.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.7.** No movement on either half. The brood's account of why it ganged up on him is an argument for Energy Absorption (§4.1 roadmap note).
2. **Lock-Step** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **Powerful Hex scales with rank as a blank cheque**: any power he has the rank for, prerequisites ignored, so every rank multiplies his options. Three of the most significant magical figures on Earth are competing over who trains him. Session 15 added the most headroom on the board, all of it plot rather than sheet: a Grey Lantern succession (*"When my time has passed, my ring will go to you"*), a secondary ring already on his finger that will charge once he remembers the oath, and a diagnosis — lantern-derived, linked to Oa, *"unconsciously altering time waves."* ⟨Ruling⟩ **Time Powers are on the GM's banned list**, so whatever the ring does will arrive as GM-granted plot rather than purchasable powers.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** The ring was not mentioned. ⟨Volatile⟩ A crime syndicate was trafficking something keyed to his time manipulation, which says somebody outside the team believes his powers can be acted on.
3. **Edge** `8.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **The newest member has the third-longest runway.** His Rank 3.5 advances are all taken (Accuracy 4, Agility 7, Suppressive Fire, Heightened Senses 1 and 2), he has Quick Learner, and a League-adjacent hero is training him. The roster calls him a *"Spatial Infiltrator"* and his weapon *"spatial-severing,"* and nothing on the sheet yet delivers either. Behind all of it is the legend: a reborn ancient weapon whose true name is lost. ⟨Volatile⟩ If the legend is literal, this is the axis it moves most. ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ If the Cutting Edge's \+1 has not been applied, there is a point of damage multiplier already on the sheet waiting to be counted.
4. **Oracle** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs hardware can be revised, and Precognition has obvious room to extend in range and reliability: growth by upgrade rather than by unfolding. Session 14 added a second path, when his module became a required component in a League-supervised build. Session 15 added a third: a module that resonates with lantern-derived time phenomena and has already produced the assist channel to Lock-Step. ⟨Volatile⟩ A time traveller looked at him and saw Batman. **§6.4 names the single highest-value purchase available to him**: any ego- or logic-based power that projects strength, which under the house rule would let him escape a grab.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.5.** ⟨Flag⟩ **Two Rank 3.5 advances are undeclared for a third session.** If either bought the escape power, this document is understating him in four places.
5. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** On the sheet he is linear: bigger numbers on the same four things, and two sessions of Crushing Grip failing against defences that scale show that the linear path has a ceiling. **Off the sheet, Session 15 was the first session to suggest a discontinuity**: a traveller from Kwa's future called him Superman, a telepath said he is *"called Superman,"* and the scenario opened with kryptonite *"headed for you, Superman."* He answered with the first ambition on his file: *"I don't even think I'm as strong as Superman. Not yet anyway."* **If the GM is foreshadowing, this is the most important line in his file. If not, it is a joke, and this entry falls back to 4.5.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.0.** Nobody called him Superman. He did what the comparison implies — stood in front of everything and refused to leave — and lost a power to a thief, which is not in the Superman brief.

### **7.4 Justice League Readiness — Capability**

*Capability only. Character is 1.14.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was, Wonder Woman pointed at him. Nightwing, operating as Batman, told him that he and Oracle are equal to or better than anyone the League could send. In Session 14 a founding member moved him into the League's headquarters, and in Session 15 he did League-grade work inside it: three pieces of hardware built alongside the League's specialist in six days, one of which defeated a known villain team's transport in play. **He is the only member whose capability would be League-relevant today rather than in five years.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** The hardware held against Swipe. A League evaluator would also note the brood's stated reason for opening on him.
2. **Oracle** `7.5`
   * **All-time.** Precognition and healing are precisely the two capabilities a large distributed organisation values most and fields least, and Nightwing named him alongside Quasar-Force as League-grade. Session 14 is what a vetting officer would find in the combat log: grabbed by a mental effect he cannot resist, blinded by an area obscurement, drained from 180 Focus to 3, and unable to treat the casualty afterwards. **A support specialist with no mobility, no escape and no Focus damage reduction can be switched off by a competent opponent**, and an evaluator would write exactly that sentence. Session 15's forecast was League-quality staff work.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.5.** Session 16 is the other page of the same file: knocked down, back up, and the fight carried on his healing and his debuffs. The evaluator's sentence still stands, because nothing on his sheet has answered it.
3. **Lock-Step** `7.4`
   * **All-time.** Strategic teleportation is a League-scale logistical asset and Powerful Hex is a League-scale wildcard. Session 13 demonstrated both, chaining a boom tube to a drone-guided teleport to close a universe-scale incident, and Session 14 produced emergency field healing from a non-medic. Session 15 was the Kwa encounter: a Lantern called for the Justice League, the Titans answered in its place, and what the scenario required — first contact with a non-human intelligence ending without a fight — is the capability the League most prizes and fields least.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.4.** A clean, disciplined fight and an extraction under fire. *Closest call:* Oracle over Lock-Step by 0.1. On capability alone, the member with Doctor Fate's hostility on file is within a tenth of the member everybody would forward.
4. **Edge** `7.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **A League-shaped specialist.** The League has long carried a marksman, and his mentor knows what one looks like. Best ranged attack on the team, the only Heightened Senses, a healing factor and a dodge, recruited personally by the acting Batman. What an evaluator would want is a second fight and an answer about the power behind the weapon.
5. **Intrepid** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Genuine top-tier durability and area damage at his rank, and Session 12 remains the best case he has made for himself. **The League has several of him, and none of them is the reason it works.** Session 14 suggested his ceiling is lower than his numbers, since the two effects that removed him from that fight are things League-tier opponents have as a matter of course. Session 15 added 210 Health and the team's only area damage.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.0.** Session 16 is the Session 14 finding again: the effect that hurt him most was not damage.

### **7.5 Justice League Readiness — Overall**

*Character (1.14) and capability (7.4) combined, plus the situational blockers that belong to neither.*

1. **Oracle** `7.8`
   * **All-time.** First on character by a wide margin and second on capability, with no blocker attached. **He is the only member who could be put forward today without a conversation about caveats first**, and Session 14 added the best single line in his file: he reported his own defeat to the sponsoring Justice Leaguer, unprompted, on the night. What holds him below 8 is the capability finding: an evaluator has a specific, repeatable method for neutralising him.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.8.** ⟨Note⟩ The silence recorded in §1.12 is still the one line a sponsor would want explained. Session 16 added two more references to the Destined errand in his hearing, and no comment from him.
2. **Quasar-Force** `6.6`
   * **All-time.** He leads capability outright, sits second on character, has been personally endorsed by the acting Batman, and is nonetheless years away. **The blockers are situational rather than personal:** he is fourteen, an open fatality investigation is unresolved, and ⟨OOC⟩ an Apokoliptian-modified New Genesis artifact is bonded to his brain and has been commandeered by a telepath before. Session 15 eased the worst blocker — the mask went on and he went home — and added a character-side sentence about Fade-Out's *"full lethal fury"* that a panel would ask about.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.6.** Nothing moved.
3. **Intrepid** `5.3`
   * **All-time.** A clean record and top-tier durability and area damage for his rank. **Unready rather than unsuitable, and the gap is experience.** In Session 14 he argued, in front of the sponsoring Justice Leaguer, that more information would not have helped. In Session 15 he gave the answer an evaluator wants from a candidate this strong: *"Not yet anyway."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.3.** Refusing to leave a fight is the kind of thing a panel admires and a field commander has to manage.
4. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A clean first session, a League-adjacent mentor willing to vouch for him, and capability in a role the League understands. The blockers are his age, an afternoon of record, and the question any panel would ask first: what, exactly, the legend says he is. He enters above Lock-Step and below Intrepid.
5. **Lock-Step** `1.5`

   * **All-time.** Real League-scale assets underneath a character file that would not reach a vote. **Doctor Fate has publicly declared him a danger to the world**, and the League would hear that before it heard anything else. Session 15 put his best capability evidence and his worst conduct evidence in the same file: capability rose nine tenths, and character fell a full point, because a panel will not get past a solicited killing through a villain team. **What is new is that a cosmic order has vouched for him** — an order the League has never heard of (*"What is a gray lantern?"*).
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.5.** His best combat conduct to date, and a minor left cuffed in a crime syndicate's warehouse. The panel's first question is still the Session 15 one.

### **7.6 Overall Value / Contribution**

*The whole question, combat and non-combat together: what does the team lose if this person walks away tomorrow?*

1. **Oracle** `9.1`
   * **All-time.** First in combat contribution, second in non-combat contribution, first in standing and first in the pecking order, and the moral spine of a group that badly needs one. He is the only healer, the only dedicated buffer, the household and the medic, and the one member who has shown he will do the right thing when a teammate with more social pull asks him not to. Session 14 showed the team a partial version of his absence while he was in the room: nobody got healed, and a chaos mage had to improvise the replacement hours later.
   * **Session 16: 9.0 → 9.1 (+0.1).** **Session 16 showed the team the whole version of his presence.** Every recovery of the fight went through him; the debuff that sent Dragonborne running was his; the plan was his. Without him, Lock-Step does not get back up to carry the others out of that warehouse.
2. **Quasar-Force** `8.9`
   * **All-time.** Almost exactly as valuable, by a completely different route: the analytical engine, the intelligence apparatus, the best control and mobility in the party, joint-highest base damage, and the widest institutional reach on the roster. **The gap to Oracle has always been the healing monopoly**, plus the fact that one strike can remove him from a fight. If he walks, the tower's shield has no maintainer, the drones lose their builder, the League and the police lose their contact, and the device that hides one member of the team from a New God walks out on that member's head. ⟨OOC⟩ The value is also entangled: the team's best asset is the reason a New God knows their address.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.9.** The intelligence was his, and so was the grab that set up the first knockout. Then he spent half the fight below zero.
3. **Lock-Step** `8.3`
   * **All-time.** Monopolies of his own: he is the only person on this team who can move it strategically, and the only person who is good with people. Since Session 14 he has also been the only member other than Oracle to restore another person's Health. Session 15 added the chair and the largest single outcome of the campaign. **Against:** he takes his liabilities with him — the Destined's favour, the streams and the posts — and some of what he generated may cost more than it returns. §3.5 prices that.
   * **Session 16: 8.1 → 8.3 (+0.2).** **The way out.** He finished the first target, carried the healer out of the fight, and brought all four teammates home on the last of his Focus. If he had walked before Session 16, the other four would still be in the warehouse.
4. **Edge** `7.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** What the team would lose if he walked: the best ranged shot on the roster, the only Heightened Senses, the shot that denied the brood its objective, and the rear guard that let the extraction start. **Fourth on arrival, ahead of the team's tank.**
5. **Intrepid** `6.7`
   * **All-time.** The team's tank: the member who absorbs the damage that would kill anyone else and ends fights outright. His contribution is concentrated in one band, and the band is the front line. With Exemplar II gone he is the only real tank, the only area damage and the cheapest member to field. **What the team loses if he walks is the thing that stands between more fragile people and whatever hits them first.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.7.** He was exactly that, for 290 damage.

*Volatile:* Oracle over Quasar-Force by 0.2, up from 0.1. Last revision said: *"One more session in which the healing monopoly does not matter and the engineering does, and this axis flips."* **Session 16 was the reverse — the healing decided the fight and the engineering was a drone sweep — and the margin doubled.**

### **7.7 Overall Power / Potency**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** The highest ceiling on the team.
