He is first in engineering, lab forensics, cyber, coordination and alliance capital, and first in crowd control, grappling, personal mobility, objective creation and combat synergy, with the steepest growth curve on the roster. **What holds the power ranking below the ceiling has always been the floor**: 60 Health, no healing, and a one-strike removal on record. Session 15 was the first session in which the floor rose (§4.1, §4.2, §6.4).
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** Still first on nine non-combat component axes and five combat ones. The floor that rose in Session 15 was tested in Session 16 and held only on the mental side.
2. **Lock-Step** `7.7`
   * **All-time.** The highest versatility on the team by a distance, and first on social, team mobility, de-escalation, rescue, stealth and solo operation, with second place on Healing since Session 14\. He is held below first by a Focus pool tied for the shallowest, the weakest power mastery on the team, and a non-combat profile that is empty outside the social column. Session 15 added a whip that reaches phasing targets, the second-best growth trajectory in the document, and a ring.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.7.** Nothing on the sheet moved. One of the best fights of his file sits beside the worst single hit he has taken.
3. **Oracle** `7.3`
   * **All-time.** First on seven combat axes and on medicine, sensory awareness, domestic utility and teaching. **This axis has never measured his value** (7.6 does); it measures his power, and his power is narrow in the places a head-to-head comparison counts.
   * **Session 16: holds at 7.3.** The head-to-head with Lock-Step now reads: he wins Healing by 7.0, Medical by 6.0 and Ally Buff by 5.2, and loses Social by 6.0, De-escalation by 1.7, Team Mobility by 5.8 and Countermeasure Resilience by 5.5.
4. **Edge** `6.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Second on combat potency (7.9), fourth on non-combat potency (3.8), and third on growth. **A sharp specialist with a long runway**, entering ahead of Intrepid on the strength of his senses, his social score and his headroom.
5. **Intrepid** `6.3`
   * **All-time.** Dominant in a narrow band, and genuinely dominant there: first on physical durability, stamina, single-target damage, area damage, power independence and overall combat power. **He stays last here without losing anything**, because a combined power ranking also counts the seventeen non-combat axes, on which he places at or near the bottom every time.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.3.** He now places last on twelve of the seventeen non-combat axes rather than all seventeen, and he is last here by two tenths, behind a member with less than half his Health.

**The inversion between 7.6 and 7.7 holds.** Oracle is first on value and third on power; Quasar-Force is second on value by two tenths and first on power by 1.3. Edge is fourth on both, and Intrepid last on both.

## **8\. Fame (Exposure)**

**Magnitude `0.0 to 10.0`**

*Exposure only — how widely known, independent of whether the opinion is good. Opinion is Section 9\.*

*⟨Note⟩ Session 15 moved every member on every public-facing axis, and one teammate, Lock-Step, was responsible for nearly all of it: a viral stream of the Watchtower, a week of follow-up broadcasts, a channel seeded for every member, and a broadcast surveillance operation. **Session 16 published nothing.** No stream is recorded, and the fight happened inside a warehouse. What Session 16 added is the price. In the fight, the brood said it had ganged up on Quasar-Force first because of his fame, his known fragility and his preference for grappling. For fifteen sessions this section measured exposure as a background risk; Session 16 is the first time an enemy said out loud that it was using it. Only the supervillain axis moved.*

### **8.1 Fame — Public**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** The most photographed member of the team by a wide margin. He hovered upside down outside the bank entrance for an entire robbery in a cosmic-mural nanosuit and was the primary media-footage subject. He flew a 120-foot yacht five hundred feet into the air over a party of teenagers. He killed a man in an incident that generated protests, a threatened lawsuit and a televised expert theorising about his mental state. He is publicly identified, looks eleven, and flies everywhere. In Session 15 he was the centrepiece of the stream that went viral (*"It's all because of this guy"*) and of a week of workbench footage after it.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.3.** Nothing was published. Lock-Step's description of him to the newcomer — *"that kid that looks like he is like 12"* — is the public image in one line.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Word-of-mouth famous, and deliberately so. He posts with hashtags, declared an Instagram Day, teleported hostages out of a bank in front of dozens of witnesses and news cameras, and is embedded in Blüdhaven's teenage social scene, the fastest-propagating network in the city. Since Session 15 he is a trending broadcaster with a co-producer, a house drone, a channel of his own and a signature sign-off (*"Lock-Step out"*).
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.5.** No broadcast is recorded.
3. **Oracle** `4.0`
   * **All-time.** Minimal public visibility during the bank robbery, and nothing on file raised it before Session 15\. Extreme Appearance means anyone who *does* see him remembers exactly what they saw. In Session 15 he appeared on the stream (*"Hey, say hi, Oracle\!"*), was talked over, and got a channel.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, minimal logged visibility, and fights mostly indoors and in alleys. Until Session 15 he was nearly invisible to the public; the viral stream then introduced him with its most shareable line — *"this guy is going to be Superman. Maybe the next Superman"* — and he has a channel.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A public identity that his own entry calls *"not very well known,"* white hair and eyes that glow, and no logged public appearance. ⟨Flag⟩ The roster's condensed entry lists his identity as *"???"*; the full entry says Public. Scored on the full entry.

### **8.2 Fame — Media**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.6`
   * **All-time.** The primary media-footage subject of the bank robbery; the yacht flight photographed against the moon; and a fatality that generated protests, a threatened lawsuit and a named science advocate theorising about his mental state on television. **He is the most covered member of this team by a wide margin, and the coverage has been accelerating.** A week of Watchtower lab footage in Session 15 added a new genre: the boy genius in space.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.6.** Nothing was published.
2. **Lock-Step** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Media-native by deliberate choice: hashtags, an Instagram Day, a bank rescue in front of news cameras, and quotable lines on purpose. In Session 15 he stopped being only a source for the media; for a week he *was* the media — a trending stream, a follow-up series, an audience-manipulation campaign with a co-producer, and a segment from a surveillance post.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** Nothing was published. ⟨Volatile⟩ The five teenagers he captioned in Session 15 were the five he fought, and the one he cuffed was left in a mob warehouse. If the second story ever meets the first, it will be his.
3. **Oracle** `4.2`
   * **All-time.** Minimal footage, though Extreme Appearance guarantees that anything captured is memorable and identifiable. Session 15 put him on a public broadcast for the first time, in a context he did not choose.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.2.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `3.8`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, and he fights mostly where cameras aren't. Since Session 15, *"the next Superman"* is a headline somebody else has already written.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.8.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No media record on file.

### **8.3 Fame — Law Enforcement (Police, Criminal Justice System)**

*The full criminal-justice apparatus at every level — police, detectives, prosecutors, criminal courts, corrections, and federal enforcement bodies. Corporations, research institutions, civil courts, schools, and city government are 8.4.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** **On more criminal-justice records than the rest of the team combined.** Blüdhaven PD knows him by name from repeated visits, a voluntary statement on the record, evidence handoffs, and the noise-complaint arrangement he set up himself. Detectives hold him as a cooperating witness across the Lex-Corp case. **A criminal court holds an open death investigation with his name on it.** And ARGUS, which operates Belle Reve, knows him because Amanda Waller tasked him personally.
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** Nothing added or removed.
2. **Lock-Step** `4.6`
   * **All-time.** Until Session 15 he had no public identity and no legal name on file, against habitual breaking and entering and an accumulating truancy record: **known to this apparatus as a pattern rather than as a person.** Session 15 gave the pattern a name — *"Eric Williams,"* held by an outside party — and a publication record: the faces of five juvenile offenders on release, broadcast with commentary implying reoffence. Juvenile justice and probation do not need anyone to report that. It is public.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.6.** Nothing reached the apparatus in the log. The cuffed teenager in the warehouse will, the moment anyone finds her.
3. **Oracle** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Present at the same incidents and rarely the one talking to officers. Restrained suspects and no bodies is a good outcome and a quiet one.
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `2.5`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity and no logged contact of any kind. Institutionally close to invisible, which is precisely the point.
   * **Session 16: holds at 2.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `1.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No record. His backstory begins with an assault near him that ended with the attacker's weapon sliced to pieces; whether that incident reached a police report is not on file.

### **8.4 Fame — Other Non-Superheroic Institutions**

*Everything institutional **outside** the criminal-justice system: corporations, research bodies, civil courts, licensing and revenue systems, schools, and city government.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Lex-Corp's legal department has him identified by name and has issued a cease-and-desist. STAR Labs knows him through his father and directly: it has lent him suppression cuffs twice, its Blüdhaven CFO has taken a meeting with him, and Doctor Varga of the Celestial Research Division wants him in a laboratory. In Session 15 he traced one of their vehicles to their CFO and parked a stealth drone in their warded garage; organisations that ward their garages check them. **The footprint is corporate and scientific rather than judicial, and it is broader than anyone else's.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** Nothing changed.
2. **Oracle** `6.2`
   * **All-time.** STAR Labs built his body, which makes him a permanent case file at a major research institution. School adds a district record, and a museum's staff knows the cyborg who mapped their entrances.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.2.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** No employer, no registrations, no assets and no filings — and a truancy record, which is exactly the institutional exposure he would least want. Iron-Side's stipend implies a payer and a payment record. Session 15 added platform presence (a trending account and administrative control of four others) and a STAR Labs employee's face and vehicle on his public stream, and Iron-Side's school now has a record of him sleeping through four days of make-up classes.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Intrepid** `3.0`
   * **All-time.** A school district record and a Secret Identity, plus, since Session 15, a social-media account he did not set up. Institutionally close to invisible.
   * **Session 16: holds at 3.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** A school record with grades that were *"already bad"* before the powers arrived, and School as an Obligation on his sheet. Nothing else institutional on file.

### **8.5 Fame — Superhero Community**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.0`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing has observed, scanned and monitored him since before he joined the team, and rates him at or above the available Justice League bench. ⟨OOC⟩ Bruce Wayne holds the dormant mother-box shell. Wonder Woman has met him several times, and Guy Gardner gave him a private audience and an open information channel. Session 14 added a founding member who decided he needed protecting and a League researcher who knew who he was before she walked into the room. Session 15 added a week of daily collaboration aboard the Watchtower, and a Grey Lantern who summarised him as *"no longer merely human."*
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.0.** Nothing changed.
2. **Lock-Step** `8.0`
   * **All-time.** Three of the most significant magical figures on Earth have opinions about him, and in Session 14 he became memorable to the Justice League in the way nobody wants to be, wrapped in the Lasso of Truth. **Everyone in this community who has met him remembers which one he was.** Session 15 added the strangest fame in the document: a Grey Lantern succession, a Kwa progenitor seeking out *"the Gray Lantern of Earth"* by name, the Watchtower's staff in the background of his stream, and Guy Gardner yelling *"Put some more money into your account\!"* across a live fight.
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Oracle** `6.5`
   * **All-time.** Mentor (Nightwing), a body built by STAR Labs, and the member who answers when somebody senior asks how the team is doing. In Session 14 he called Wonder Woman to report the loss, which is the interaction a senior hero remembers, and a League researcher called his hardware *"fascinating"* — a different kind of fame, and it generates paperwork. ⟨Volatile⟩ In Session 15 a time traveller saw Batman in him.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Known to the right people before he was known to the team.** Black Canary volunteered to train him, and the Birds of Prey were the ones called when he went looking for help. Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited him and briefed him personally. Two senior heroes and a network know his name; nobody else in the community does yet.
5. **Intrepid** `4.5`
   * **All-time.** Mentor (Nightwing) was very nearly the complete list; a week inside the Justice League's headquarters left nobody writing his name down. In Session 15 a viral stream shot inside the Watchtower called him the next Superman, after a Grey Lantern and a hive mind had already called him Superman. **He has never done anything this community would talk about, and it now has something to talk about.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 4.5.** Nothing changed.

### **8.6 Fame — Supervillain Community**

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.3`
   * **All-time.** Both recognised on sight and specifically discussed. Insidious gangers shout "Titans\!" on spotting him, and every captured villain has met him because he conducts the interrogations. The Destined once stole his drone codes, Viral catalogued his mind, Hexcaliber targeted him specifically, and Aegis-Girl walked past Exemplar to attack him personally. Session 14 added a tactical fact four thieves and a goddess can now sell: the flying kid goes from full to unconscious in one strike. Session 15 added a notice every teleporting villain will learn the hard way — the tower's shield is his.
   * **Session 16: 9.0 → 9.3 (+0.3).** **The underworld has a written answer to him, and it said so.** The brood explained its opening in its own banter: his fame, his known fragility, and his preference for grappling, which it called the most damaging thing to its tactics. Blitzkrieg followed him behind his container — *"What's ya doing, Hiding?"* — and dropped him. **What the community knows about him is now a doctrine, not a rumour.**
2. **Lock-Step** `8.5`
   * **All-time.** Shadow-Boxer tried to recruit him, and so did the Red Aegis boss. The Destined negotiate with him as the team's voice, the Marrow Syndicate has him on file, and someone spent a bespoke temporal artifact building a profile of him. **The underworld has always known his name.** In Session 15 the Destined came to work *for* him, Fade-Out congratulated him on his leadership, and he publicly mocked five teenagers who are now carrying a hostile progenitor's brood. **As of Session 15 the underworld also knows his rate.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 8.5.** The five he mocked fought him, and the Marrow Syndicate warehouse held a second device keyed to his powers. Already priced.
3. **Intrepid** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** Known by effect rather than by name. He is the one who ends fights — the hammer villain, Stote, Dragonborne, super-juicers going off the map, and in Session 14 a professional thief with an adamantine hammer, removed in one punch. Since Session 15 he is also known by a public comparison to the one hero every villain in the world has a plan for.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** The brood's verdict on why it could not take him was his weight. That is a fact about him, not a new story.
4. **Edge** `4.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Introduced to the underworld in one afternoon, by the people he beat.** He knocked out Blood Blade, put 101 into Stote, destroyed the device the brood came for, and got up from 0 in front of Blitzkrieg, whose summary was *"They keep coming back up like Daisys\!"* Two of the five walked away to tell it.
5. **Oracle** `3.5`
   * **All-time.** Recognised and the least discussed. The villains who fight him mostly learn they can't hit him, which is a quiet kind of reputation. Session 14 supplied a louder and less welcome story: an Olympian goddess held him in place for most of the fight and drained him from full to 3 Focus without ever attacking his body.
   * **Session 16: 3.2 → 3.5 (+0.3).** **Somebody on the other side has studied him.** Dragonborne came prepared to answer Return Fire, his signature reaction, by turning it onto a teammate. A counter only exists where someone has done the reading. He stays last, because the story that circulates about him is still mostly about what works against him.

### **8.7 Fame — Civilian Life / Non-Superheroic Identity Exposure**

*How exposed the person behind the mask is.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `9.5`
   * **All-time.** Total. Endo Wenas is publicly identified, his parents are known and reachable in Slate Hill, his father's employer is known, and his family's involvement with a clinician is documented. **There is no mask to be behind.**
   * **Session 16: holds at 9.5.** Nothing changed.
2. **Oracle** `6.0`
   * **All-time.** Wade Dobson is not concealed, and Extreme Appearance means concealment is not available to him anyway. What limits the score is that few people are looking. He took second on the vacancy in Session 15\.
   * **Session 16: holds at 6.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Lock-Step** `5.0`
   * **All-time.** For fourteen sessions he did not appear to have a civilian identity at all. **Session 15 supplied both halves at once**: a civilian identity, **Eric Williams**, and its link to the hero — an alien's text (*"Is this Eric Williams, known as the Gray Lantern of Earth?"*, answered *"yes it is"*), a progenitor and a Grey Lantern calling him Eric, and his own face trending for a week. The exposure is held by parties outside Earth's institutions, which is the only reason this is 5.0 rather than higher.
   * **Session 16: holds at 5.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `4.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Murphy King has a public identity, *"not very well known,"* and a face that is hard to mistake: white hair and eyes that glow black-red. A school, a family the roster does not describe, and a mentor whose name is public. ⟨Flag⟩ Identity listed as *"???"* in the condensed entry.
5. **Intrepid** `1.0`
   * **All-time.** The only fully Secret Identity on the roster, maintained without a single logged lapse.
   * **Session 16: holds at 1.0.** Nothing changed.

*⟨Session 16 note⟩ No civilian identity was spoken in play, and no Titan published anything. **The exposure that moved this section was an enemy using what was already public.***

##

## **9\. Reputation / Opinion**

**Valence `−10.0 to +10.0`**

*Session 16 happened inside a warehouse and reached no public, no outlet and no institution in the log, so most of this section holds. The one movement among the returning four is Lock-Step on Nightwing, for the first time citing Nightwing's rule as the team's rule. Edge enters with an unusual profile: almost unknown to the public, and already well regarded by the heroes who brought him in.*

### **9.1 Reputation — Public**

1. **Oracle** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Nothing negative attaches to him and nothing ever has. He heals people, asks for surrender first, is polite, and is visibly a teenager doing a hard job well. The viral stream of Session 15 introduced him to a large new audience as *"the guy that always tells us what we do wrong and tries to fix it,"* which is a teammate's joke and an accurate résumé.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** Nothing reached the public.
2. **Intrepid** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** Low salience because of the Secret Identity, and uniformly positive within that. Until Session 15 the public knew a strong man in a cape who helps. **Since Session 15 it has a story to attach to him, and it is the best one available:** *"maybe the next Superman."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+5.5.** Nothing reached the public.
3. **Lock-Step** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** Genuinely popular with the demographic that matters most for word-of-mouth, and carrying real risk. The hostage rescues, the yacht, the social media and the handcuff routine are all charming; the prisoner humiliation happened in front of witnesses. **Net positive, with a fuse in it.** Session 15 lengthened the fuse: the stream trended and the audience he built likes him, but on the same channel he mocked five released teenagers by face and captioned them *\#SomeKidsJustNeedAWhooping*. Popular, net positive, and one reposted clip away from a very different number.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+4.5.** Nothing reached the public.
4. **Edge** `+2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Public but *"not very well known,"* with nothing for or against him on record. His first public story, when it comes, could be a rescue or a legend about a monster, and the score sits slightly positive on the rescue.
5. **Quasar-Force** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** **The highest variance on the team; the ranking reflects net position, not dislike.** He is simultaneously the most beloved and the most controversial figure the Titans have: a spectacular, obviously good-hearted flying child who saved hostages and flew a yacht for a party, and the boy who killed a man, which prompted protests, a threatened suit and a public argument about untrained children wielding lethal power. **The death has been the dominant public fact about him ever since.** Session 15 put a different fact in front of a large audience — a small boy at a console in the Justice League's station with the Earth behind him, building something. **The death is still the headline. It now has competition.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.5.** Nothing reached the public.

*⟨Volatile⟩ Two stories have not surfaced.* The Session 14 museum theft is still unreported; Wonder Woman has recovered all three artifacts, so if it runs now it runs as a League recovery. **The new one is worse.** If the warehouse fight reaches the public, its headline is three teenagers left unconscious in a mob warehouse, one of them in handcuffs, by a team whose public voice had mocked all five of them a week earlier.

### **9.2 Reputation — Media**

1. **Lock-Step** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Media-native. He poses, posts and supplies quotable lines, and the handcuff ritual is a recurring bit that writes its own headline. He generates coverage deliberately, and it comes back positive. Session 15 was a larger audience with more ammunition in it: a week of his own coverage, still positive, and for the first time material a hostile outlet would want — a public shaming of minors with a hashtag. It has not been picked up yet.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** Nothing published, nothing picked up.
2. **Oracle** `+6.0`
   * **All-time.** The safe, sympathetic angle: a cyborg teenager who heals people and asks criminals to surrender. There is no negative story about him available.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Intrepid** `+4.5`
   * **All-time.** A classic-looking hero in a red cape, with nothing to write about and nothing bad to write. Since Session 15 the Superman line writes his headline for him.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+4.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `+1.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No coverage on file. The angle an outlet would find is the most striking one on the team — white hair, glowing eyes, a teenager trained by Black Canary — and the most dangerous if the word *monster* ever gets attached to it.
5. **Quasar-Force** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** **The best footage and the best controversy at once, which is the worst possible combination.** The yacht flight, the upside-down bank hover and the cosmic nanosuit are irresistible imagery; the fatality handed the same outlets a story about unsupervised children with lethal powers, and a science advocate's chemical-exposure theory is running. Session 15's week of Watchtower laboratory footage brought him to exactly neutral by giving every outlet a second, much better story about the same boy.
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.0.** Nothing changed.

### **9.3 Reputation — Law Enforcement (Police, Criminal Justice System)**

*Same scope as 8.3 — police, detectives, prosecutors, criminal courts, corrections, and federal enforcement, at every level.*

1. **Oracle** `+5.5`
   * **All-time.** He demands surrender before engaging, heals the people he fights, and has never generated an incident report. Officers arriving after an Oracle engagement find restrained suspects and no bodies, which is the single thing this apparatus most wants from a costumed teenager. He took first on the vacancy in Session 15, when Exemplar II's police connections left with him.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+5.5.** Nothing reached the apparatus.
2. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Unknown to them and carrying nothing negative. He sits above the members below him only because they carry complications he does not.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+2.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+1.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Unknown to them, no record, and one first fight in which every member of the opposition he dropped was knocked out rather than killed. Below Intrepid because his public identity gives the apparatus a name to attach anything to.
4. **Quasar-Force** `+0.5`
   * **All-time.** Working officers regard him well: he gives statements in person, maintains chain of custody, hands over evidence unprompted, and arranged the noise-complaint diversion with them rather than around them. **But the apparatus is not only its officers.** Prosecutors and a criminal court hold an open death investigation, Lex-Corp's counsel is pushing a wrongful-death theory into the same system, and ARGUS holds him as a cultivated asset. ⟨Note⟩ In Session 15 he also said *"I say get them involved"* about a villain team's burglary; the apparatus does not know that.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−6.0`
   * **All-time.** Habitual breaking and entering, a restrained prisoner humiliated in front of witnesses, and an assault on a minor teammate that a defence attorney would find in an afternoon. **Every one of those is a chargeable offence rather than a bad look.** Session 13 put the first items on the other side: a cuffed prisoner properly handed over, and three exonerated victims asked whether they would testify. Session 15 added something the apparatus can watch rather than has to discover — released juvenile offenders broadcast by face with a prescribed *"whooping"* — and, out of its sight, a solicited burglary and killing witnessed by a villain.
   * **Session 16: holds at −6.0.** The new item is one the apparatus does not have yet: one of those same juveniles, knocked out and left in handcuffs in a crime syndicate's warehouse. The rule he applied while doing it — *"we can bring them back, so knock them out"* — is the one the apparatus would want. This axis moves when someone finds her.

### **9.4 Reputation — Other Non-Superheroic Institutions**

*Same boundary as 8.4 — corporations, research bodies, civil courts, licensing and revenue systems, schools, and city government.*

1. **Oracle** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** Institutions are built to approve of people like him. STAR Labs would regard him as a success story worth publicising, and no corporation, civil court or school has a reason to think about him twice. Nothing in any file anywhere counts against him.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.5.** Nothing changed.
2. **Intrepid** `+2.0`
   * **All-time.** Unknown, compliant, and carrying no negatives. A Secret Identity means institutions have almost nothing to form an opinion from.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+2.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `0.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** The one institution with a file on him is a school, and the file says his grades were *"already bad"* before the powers arrived. Nothing else for or against.
4. **Quasar-Force** `−0.5`
   * **All-time.** Against him: a corporate legal department that has issued a cease-and-desist and is circulating a wrongful-death threat. For him, and growing: STAR Labs is positive about him in its own right, a board-level CFO gave him a morning, and the Celestial Research Division's lead scientist wants to study him. **The institutions that like him are the ones with no power over him.** In Session 15 he traced a STAR Labs executive's vehicle and watched its garage — no violation at all, and exactly the kind of thing a corporation holding an alien parasite would prefer nobody did.
   * **Session 16: holds at −0.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** A truancy record he is actively extending, and conduct that does not stay inside the criminal file it starts in: a school or a licensing body that learns about the sedation of a minor teammate has its own reason to act. Session 15 extended the truancy to make-up classes, put a STAR Labs employee's face and vehicle on a public stream, and posted minors on platforms that have policies about exactly that. **A school, a corporation and a platform each acquired their own reason to act.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −5.0.** Nothing changed.

### **9.5 Reputation — Broad Superhero Community**

1. **Oracle** `+8.0`
   * **All-time.** He reports honestly, defers to Nightwing, refuses unethical requests from teammates, and prioritises healing over glory — exactly the profile senior heroes want to see in a teen team and recommend to each other. Session 14 was the behaviour that makes the reputation: he called the Justice Leaguer who gave them the job and told her they had failed.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.0.** Nothing reached the community. A team that briefed Wonder Woman the same afternoon is the kind of report it hears.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** Wonder Woman, asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was, pointed at him. He shares intelligence proactively with police, public superhero forums and Nightwing, and offered Wonder Woman all of his data unasked. A serving Green Lantern took a personal interest. In Session 14 a founding member judged him worth protecting personally, and in Session 15 he spent a week working beside the League's specialist in their station and kept a Green Lantern informed exactly as promised. ⟨OOC⟩ Against it: the fatality, and Apokoliptian-modified hardware in his skull that unsettles anyone who learns of it.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+5.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Vouched for before he arrived.** Black Canary volunteered to train him, the Birds of Prey took his call for help, and Nightwing, operating as Batman, recruited him personally. That is two senior heroes and a network with a stake in him, which is more than two of his teammates have after sixteen sessions. The rest of the community has not met him.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Nobody has an opinion, and nothing negative exists to form one from. His one extended appearance before a senior hero, the Session 14 debrief, is scored in 9.10. A teenager promoted on a teammate's phone as the next Superman is not yet an opinion, and among people who know Superman it may be a joke.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−3.5`
   * **All-time.** The magical community is split three ways on him, with the hostile vote held by Doctor Fate, and the conventional community would object to nearly everything he does if it knew. Guy Gardner found him funny; Wonder Woman found him transparent, and she is the one the others will ask. In Session 15 a Grey Lantern tested him and named him her successor, and Wonder Woman's reply was *"What is a gray lantern?"* **The first cape-adjacent order to rate him highly is one nobody else has heard of.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −3.5.** Nothing reached the community. ⟨Note⟩ The first thing the community's newest connection to this team heard from him was that *"all the adults are trash."* Black Canary's student will have a version of that to report.

### **9.6 Reputation — Broad Supervillain Community**

1. **Quasar-Force** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** **Villains like him, and it is measurable.** Shadow-Boxer said he respects this team because they keep their word; Cassidy Yanks volunteered her real name because he was nice to her; World Ender's facade collapsed under sincerity. The counterweight: uncooperative professionals read him as a child to be played, and he is the one who accidentally killed a man. In Session 15 he locked the Destined out of the tower and then said, of the same team, *"Maybe they'll stop being assholes too."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.5.** The brood went for him first on tactics, not animus, and said so.
2. **Lock-Step** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** The surrender ritual is a genuine kindness in a business without much of it, and villains have cuffed themselves rather than fight him. Against it, he humiliates the ones who refuse, and Shadow-Boxer's recruitment attempt suggests some read him as one of their own. **Session 15 added the thing the underworld respects more than kindness: he is a client.** Fade-Out's verdict: *"About time you stepped up the leadership."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+5.0.** **Even the brood liked the offer.** Argent began to accept the cuffs before her controller reasserted itself, and Dragonborne called it *"a good idea, but something inside us is telling us not to."* He knocked out one of the five he had mocked and left her cuffed, which the underworld will remember too.
3. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** He heals people who were trying to kill him minutes earlier and attempted to revive a dead enemy. That gets around a jail block fast.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.5.** Every point of damage he dealt was Focus, and the one he drove off left on his own feet.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** Feared and respected as a physical fact, with no personal animus in either direction. He hits you and it's over and it wasn't personal.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Edge** `+0.5`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Barely an opinion yet, and an exasperated one: *"They keep coming back up like Daisys\!"* He knocked out one of the five and destroyed what they came for, and his attempt to intimidate the last two with his monstrousness did not move them.

### **9.7 Reputation — Iron-Side**

*Inferred from a professional educator's frame: methodical, disciplined, non-authoritarian, deliberately not appointing a leader, reporting to Nightwing daily, and — by her own statement — taking notes. Session 14 revised the frame: she has significant medical training. Session 15 revised it again: she will physically correct a student, a pinch on the back of the neck, and it worked. **Iron-Side does not appear in Session 16\.***

1. **Quasar-Force** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** The ideal student from her position, and he has gone out of his way to signal it. He thanked her for teaching them, fell straight into her structured wake and bedtimes, and defended her hazard pay to Lock-Step's face, in front of her. In Session 14 she took a knife out of him herself and sat with him through the night. In Session 15 eight-hour make-up days produced no complaint from him that the log records.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.5.** He defended the adults to the newcomer in her absence: *"Well everyone is busy, and we're busy too."*
2. **Oracle** `+6.5`
   * **All-time.** Compliant, structured and cooperative, and content to follow whatever education Nightwing prefers. In Session 12 he handled an absence the way she would want, obtaining a note rather than simply not appearing. Undemanding, reliable and easy to teach.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+6.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Intrepid** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** First up every morning, no friction, no complaints, and no engagement either. A perfectly fine student who leaves no impression. He knew where the library was.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.5.** Nothing changed.
4. **Edge** `+1.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Not yet met in the log. The file she will open has School as an Obligation and grades that were *"already bad"*; the student she will meet asked for a plan and a briefing before he walked into his first fight, which is the habit she pinched Lock-Step for not having.
5. **Lock-Step** `−6.5`
   * **All-time.** He argued with her at their first meeting, called her a finishing-school teacher, called her school fake to her face, accused her of doing it only for the money, insinuated that her absence during the Destined incursion was suspicious, and shows up last or not at all. Her calm reply — *"I am taking notes"* — remains the most quietly ominous line in the campaign. In Session 15 he slept through as much of her make-up teaching as he could and was pinched for not listening in briefings — and then, for the first time, asked her a question and followed the answer.
   * **Session 16: holds at −6.5.** **The first thing he told the newcomer about her was the old grievance:** during the breaches she *"lived in the security room,"* and *"I got stonewalled."* One civil question in Session 15 has not yet changed what he says about her when she is not there.

### **9.8 Reputation — Nightwing**

*⟨Session 16 note⟩ **Nightwing is off screen for a third consecutive session, and in this one he acted.** Operating as Batman, he recruited Edge and briefed him on the five villains, and the team did not know. Lock-Step cited his rule to set the team's rules of engagement. The team made no report to him; it briefed Wonder Woman. The lethal errand he has not heard about is still outstanding. **Scores are carried on earlier evidence plus what Session 16 implies.***

1. **Oracle** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Nightwing's channel into this team, formalised in Session 12\. He called about the artifact knowing exactly how Lock-Step would react, and did it anyway. When Nightwing needed somebody in a room with a STAR Labs board member, he arranged it for Oracle, and he named him alongside Quasar-Force as equal to or better than the League bench. With his mentor unreachable in Session 14, he escalated to the next appropriate authority immediately and told her the truth. ⟨Note⟩ Session 15 was the first session in which the channel carried nothing Nightwing would have wanted to hear, and *"It's no different than Nightwing"* the first critical thing anyone in Oracle's file has said about his mentor.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.5.** ⟨Note⟩ **Nightwing's one act this session went around the channel**: he briefed the newcomer, not Oracle. It reads as a recruiter briefing his recruit, and it is not scored.
2. **Quasar-Force** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** Historically the primary correspondent: a proactive intelligence-sharer, evidence-disciplined, and credited by name for the security upgrades. Session 13 aired the mother-box friction and largely settled it. Session 15 took half a point on implication: the boy Nightwing has watched since before he had a codename endorsed bringing in the Destined — the team that breached the tower Nightwing designed — and endorsed Fade-Out's *"full lethal fury."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** Nightwing would also read *"We sent the murder hobos after the bad progenitor."* The file already holds the sentence that matters.
3. **Edge** `+6.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** **Chosen by Nightwing personally.** Operating as Batman, he recruited Edge to fill the vacancy and briefed him on the five villains before the fight — the only Titan he is recorded as briefing this revision. A recruiter's confidence is not a track record, which is why Edge starts below the two members Nightwing has worked with longest.
4. **Intrepid** `+1.5`
   * **All-time.** No problems and no contribution. Invited to raise concerns at a round-table convened specifically to hear them, he had none.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.5.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.0`
   * **All-time.** He tried to conceal a dangerous temporal artifact from Nightwing, told the room Nightwing can't be trusted with their security, was angry that Oracle reported it, and sedated a minor teammate. The redemption path has been visible since Session 13: Nightwing structured the artifact's custody so Lock-Step keeps access on his own terms, volunteered to arrange his pay personally, and talks about him with amused affection. Session 15 was a list of the things Nightwing's professional life is organised to prevent — a League facility broadcast, a surveillance post broadcast, carelessness with identities, and Nightwing's own detractors hired and asked to kill a detainee.
   * **Session 16: −5.5 → −5.0 (+0.5).** **For the first time in his file he used Nightwing as an authority rather than against him.** *"Nope, nope, Nightwing did say we can bring them back, so knock them out"* — and then he applied it all fight. Against it, in the same breath: *"The other situation, is that other team's problem,"* which is the errand Nightwing has not heard about, and *"Like Batman or Nightwing, they didn't even fill him in,"* which was wrong. **The redemption path got its first step.**

### **9.9 Reputation — Batman**

*Batman is injured, unable to fight, and has time to read files. ⟨OOC⟩ He holds the dormant mother-box shell and has probably concluded that Quasar-Force will not age. Absent in Session 16; scored on what his file would now contain. Nightwing used his mantle to recruit the newcomer.*

1. **Oracle** `+7.0`
   * **All-time.** A clean record, honest reporting, medical support, and a STAR Labs predictive module that is both tactically valuable and technically auditable; Batman likes people whose capabilities he can verify. Session 14 added the behaviour he values above all others: a total failure reported immediately and in full to the responsible authority, by the person it was least comfortable for. ⟨Note⟩ Since Session 15 his module resonates with a teenager's time-altering powers, which is the kind of sentence Batman reads twice.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.0.** The page Batman would mark: an enemy who answered his Return Fire with a reaction of its own.
2. **Intrepid** `+5.0`
   * **All-time.** A Secret Identity, and Batman approves. No secrets, no factions, no technology he didn't earn, no complications, and no file to maintain. Boring is a compliment in this room.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+5.0.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+2.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Recruited under Batman's name by somebody else. Bruce Wayne, reading the file, finds a Monster-origin fifteen-year-old with a legend about cutting down armies, a power that slices through objects by concentration, Black Canary vouching for him, and one clean fight. **Batman likes capabilities he can verify, and nobody can yet verify what the legend is.**
4. **Quasar-Force** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** **The most divided assessment in the campaign.** ⟨OOC⟩ Batman holds the mother-box shell, ordered the periodic scans, and knows about the frozen aging; he will regard Endo as one of the most dangerous unresolved variables in Blüdhaven, and since Session 14 the variable has had an external claimant. At the same time, Endo's conduct is exactly the behaviour Batman rewards and rarely gets from teenagers. **High opinion of the boy; alarm about the situation.** Session 15 moved both halves in opposite directions: the box is masked from the New God hunting it, and the file on a fourteen-year-old now contains *"full lethal fury."*
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.0.** The situation half improved again — the circlet held against a power thief — and the boy half added *"murder hobos."* They cancel.
5. **Lock-Step** `−7.5`
   * **All-time.** The file is thick and none of it is reassuring: chaos magic, Doctor Fate's declared hostility, concealment from Nightwing, the sedations, prisoner abuse, an unidentified party that profiled him with a temporal artifact, and a founding member of the League who felt the need to use a compulsion artifact on him. Session 15 added proxy murder, public exposure, an open mind and an outside claim. **In fairness, and Batman is fair:** he also talked a planet out of a war, refused a burglary on principle, and asked Iron-Side for help. **The pattern under the misconduct is a teenager who does the right thing when he has time and outsources the wrong thing when he doesn't.**
   * **Session 16: holds at −7.5.** Both columns got a page. **For:** the non-lethal rule stated and kept, surrender offered twice, consent asked, and four teammates extracted. **Against:** a minor he had publicly mocked, knocked out and left in handcuffs in a crime syndicate's building. The pattern holds.

### **9.10 Reputation — Justice League**

*After the fight, the team went back to the tower and briefed Wonder Woman; the log does not record who spoke or what she said. The League took no part in Session 16\. Edge's mentor, Black Canary, gives the League a new connection to the team that does not run through Quasar-Force.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `+8.5`
   * **All-time.** Wonder Woman, asked who the smartest person in the Justice League was, **pointed at him.** In Session 14 professional regard became institutional responsibility: she extended personal protection to him, moved him into the Watchtower, assigned the League's mother-box specialist to his case, and undertook to raise the matter with New God contacts. **The League has adopted his problem.** In Session 15 he was a good guest, delivered three devices, and kept the League informed from the ground. ⟨OOC⟩ Against it: New Genesis technology modified on Apokolips, in a child, is a League-level concern.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+8.5.** Nothing changed.
2. **Oracle** `+3.5`
   * **All-time.** A STAR Labs product with clean conduct, regarded positively without anyone having to think about it. Session 14 gave the League reasons to think about it: he made the failure call himself, produced the exact inventory of what was taken, and was the one who said the apology counted. Doctor Taleria also found his hardware *"fascinating,"* a separate and less comfortable kind of attention.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+3.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Edge** `+3.0`

   * **Debut, Session 16\.** Black Canary volunteered to train him, and her opinion carries weight with the League in most versions of it. Nobody else there has heard of him. Third on arrival, on a single sponsor.
4. **Intrepid** `0.0`
   * **All-time.** Very nearly unknown to them, with a share of the collective credit for the Session 13 capture. In Session 14 he spent an extended debrief in front of a founding member saying that nothing could have been done better, that *"we are not going to win every battle,"* and that complaining gets you replaced. Nothing malicious and nothing disqualifying, and not what you want in the file. In Session 15 a stream shot in their station called him the next Superman.
   * **Session 16: holds at 0.0.** Nothing changed.
5. **Lock-Step** `−5.0`

   * **All-time.** If Doctor Fate has an opinion, the League will hear it, and his conduct record will not survive first contact with Superman. Session 14 happened in the League's own house: an argument with Wonder Woman about mission tiers, a rant, solicited souvenirs, the Lasso of Truth, and access restricted to the guest quarters. Session 15 added the stream of their station, *"So you're not going to help? Again?"*, and *"Diana."* **For, and it is genuinely League-shaped:** a Lantern asked for *"Justice League"* and got the Titans, and he handled it better than the League's own reputation suggests it would have.
   * **Session 16: holds at −5.0.** His opening to the newcomer included the same complaint — *"But you have the Justice League. Why are you not helping us?"* — said, this time, out of the League's hearing.

### **9.11 Reputation — Green Lantern Corps**

*⟨Thin evidence⟩ Still the least-supported axis in the document. Session 15 added two direct Corps contacts and one enormous indirect item; **Session 16 adds nothing.** Volatile throughout.*

1. **Quasar-Force** `+7.5`
   * **All-time.** **A near-monopoly.** Guy Gardner sought him out, made him a sandwich with the ring because he asked how it worked, got genuinely angry on his behalf that nobody had told him what was fused to his nervous system, and offered Corps files on the mother box; Nightwing endorsed the relationship. In Session 15 Quasar-Force called him the moment the progenitor threat looked real, with the critical caveat that one progenitor is civilised, and followed up by text — **the Session 13 instruction used exactly as given.** ⟨Note⟩ Guy's opinion of the boy is very high. The Corps has not formed one.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+7.5.** No contact.
2. **Oracle** `+2.5`
   * **All-time.** Collective credit and nothing against him, which on this axis is worth second place. **He is the member whose conduct this organisation would like best if it ever looked.** None of it has reached Guy.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+2.5.** Nothing changed.
3. **Intrepid** `+1.0`
   * **All-time.** Zero contact, zero record, and a share of the collective credit for a capture Guy rated highly.
   * **Session 16: holds at \+1.0.** Nothing changed.
4. **Lock-Step** `+0.5`
   * **All-time.** More contact with a Green Lantern than anyone but Quasar-Force, and it ended well — *"no hard feelings"* — after he argued that heroes should be paid and then phoned a bank to drain the man's account. Guy's *"messing with time, that's probably not a great thing"* was the first Corps-relevant flag on his name. Session 15 repeated the first contact (*"Put some more money into your account\!"*) and added an indirect item nobody knows the sign of: powers read as lantern-derived and linked to Oa, and a ring from a different order that works in time. **A Lantern was amused. A Guardian would not be.**
   * **Session 16: holds at \+0.5.** No contact. ⟨Volatile⟩ A crime syndicate held a device keyed to his time manipulation, which is the sort of fact that reaches Oa eventually.
5. **Edge** `0.0`
   * **Debut, Session 16\.** No contact and no record. The Corps does not know he exists.

## **10\. Aggregates And Cross-Cutting Synthesis**

### **10.0 Why Three Scales**

**A single 0–10 scale cannot express liability.** Intrepid's engineering is `0.5`, which is an absence. Lock-Step's standing with Iron-Side is `−6.5`, which is an active debt that is accumulating and already has consequences in motion. Flattening both to "low" loses the distinction that matters most for play.

**A single −10…+10 scale cannot express absence.** Scoring Intrepid's engineering at −9 would claim that his engineering *harms* the team, which is false. He simply isn't an engineer, and nobody expected him to be.

So the scale follows the axis. Capability and exposure are magnitudes (`0.0–10.0`). Psychology, standing and opinion have genuine negative poles (`−10.0…+10.0`). Threat exposure is only ever a cost (`0.0…−10.0`). **The aggregates normalise everything onto one consistent signed scale**, because a composite is useless if its components point in different directions.

**Recommendation:** use `−10…+10` for composites. The sign separates "contributes less than a competent peer would" from "is a net drag", and three of five PCs now sit close enough to zero that an unsigned scale would hide which side they are on. A 0–10 rescale is in 10.1.6.

**Conversion.** Magnitude axes map as **(score − 5\) × 2**, anchored so that 5.0 (the competent Rank 3 baseline) becomes 0\. A negative there means *"this far below what a baseline peer would bring,"* not *"actively harmful."*

***⟨Note on comparability this revision⟩*** **Score-based figures (10.1) are per-character means, so Edge's arrival changes none of the returning four's figures**, and every Session 15 figure in parentheses is the published one. **Position-based figures (10.2) depend on who else is on the board.** A five-person board runs 1–5, and adding a member moves the others down wherever he ranks above them: Edge ranks above Oracle on 23 axes, Quasar-Force on 26, Lock-Step on 39 and Intrepid on 57\. Section 10.2 therefore reports the five-person board and, for the returning four, a **like-for-like comparison** — Session 16 scores with Edge removed and positions re-counted — against the published Session 15 four-person figures.

#### **10.1.6 Sensitivity**

**Including the seven fame axes keeps the flip for a second revision.** Across 101 axes, with exposure treated as favourable, the composites read **Quasar-Force \+3.83, Oracle \+3.62**, Lock-Step \+0.33, Edge \+0.25, Intrepid −0.73. **Quasar-Force leads by 0.21**, down from 0.25. Fame is worth \+0.34 to Quasar-Force, \+0.27 to Lock-Step, −0.20 to Intrepid, −0.27 to Oracle and −0.32 to Edge, which is enough to drop the newcomer below Lock-Step on this treatment.

**Rescaling to `0–10`** via `(x+10)/2` on the fame-inclusive composite preserves the order: Quasar-Force **6.91**, Oracle **6.81**, Lock-Step **5.17**, Edge **5.12**, Intrepid **4.64**.

**Excluding the risk section as well — the 86 favourable axes only — puts Oracle back in front: \+4.692 to \+4.662.** Last revision Quasar-Force led this treatment by nine thousandths; Oracle now leads it by thirty. **Edge is third here (+1.084)**, ahead of Lock-Step (+0.842), because this is the treatment in which Lock-Step's risk load does not count and Edge's absence of deep negatives does.

**Section-weighted treatment** gives each section equal weight instead of each axis. Over the eight non-fame sections Oracle leads, \+3.50 to \+2.73, with Edge third at \+0.50. With fame included Quasar-Force leads, \+3.35 to \+3.11. **The weighting scheme still decides the answer.**

**Fight sensitivity.** Session 16 was the first fight since Session 14, so the question is how much of the movement the fight itself produced. Revert all twenty-two combat axes to their Session 15 values and the composites read Oracle \+3.867, Quasar-Force \+3.491, Lock-Step \+0.047 and Intrepid −0.531. **The fight's combat scores were worth \+0.021 each to Oracle and Lock-Step, \+0.003 to Quasar-Force, and exactly nothing to Intrepid**, whose four combat movements cancelled. Most of Session 16's movement came from what the fight showed about character and standing, not from the combat axes.

**One-session sensitivity.** Session 16 moved **59 axes across the returning four, against 183 last revision**:

- **Oracle** 13 (10 better, 2 worse, 1 fame), net **\+0.048**.
- **Quasar-Force** 9 (5 better, 3 worse, 1 fame), net **\+0.012**.
- **Lock-Step** 24 (16 better, 8 worse), net **\+0.045**.
- **Intrepid** 13 (6 better, 7 worse), net **−0.011**.

Strip every Session-16-derived change and the composites revert exactly to last pass. **No single character's session reshaped his profile or the standings this time**; the revision's largest change is an arrival.

**Roster sensitivity.** Edge changes no returning member's score-based figure. On position-based figures he takes **one first place** (Ranged, from Oracle) and **sixteen last places**: twelve favourable lasts (eight from Intrepid, two each from Oracle and Lock-Step) and four fame lasts from Intrepid. Seven of the sixteen are tie-breaks. Section 10.2 reports the like-for-like comparison alongside the five-person board, so none of that movement is reported as performance.
