| **Edge** | 1 | 16 | 3.56 | 1 | 15 | 27 | 42 | 16 | **0.96** |
| **Intrepid** | 8 | **42** | 3.87 | 8 | 6 | 19 | 26 | 42 | 1.24 |

*Like-for-like four-person distributions, Session 16 (Session 15):* Quasar-Force 40 / 38 / 16 / 7 *(40 / 40 / 14 / 7\)*, average 1.90 *(1.88)*; Oracle 38 / 30 / 23 / 10 *(38 / 28 / 25 / 10\)*, average 2.05 *(2.07)*; Lock-Step 15 / 26 / 30 / 30 *(15 / 25 / 31 / 30\)*, average 2.74 *(2.75)*; Intrepid 8 / 7 / 32 / 54 *(8 / 8 / 31 / 54\)*, average 3.31 *(3.30)*.

**Quasar-Force places top-two on 68 of 101 axes on the five-person board**; like-for-like it is 78, down from 80, because he lost three second places (two to Oracle, one to Lock-Step) and gained one. **Edge's distribution is the tightest on the board (SD 0.96)**: one first, sixteen lasts, and 84 placements in the middle three, half of them fourth. **Intrepid is last on 42 of 101**, down from 54, entirely because a fifth member arrived below him on twelve axes. Six of the forty-two are risk axes and are good news.

#### **10.2.5 Placement Tally (94 non-neutral axes)**

| PC | Good 1sts | Good 5ths | Bad 1sts | Bad 5ths | Net | Like-for-like *(S15)* |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Oracle** | 37 | 5 | 0 | 2 | **\+34** | \+33 *(+33)* |
| **Quasar-Force** | 27 | 7 | 6 | 0 | **\+14** | \+14 *(+14)* |
| **Edge** | 1 | 12 | 0 | 0 | **−11** | — |
| **Lock-Step** | 13 | 28 | 2 | 0 | **−17** | −19 *(−19)* |
| **Intrepid** | 8 | 34 | 0 | 6 | **−20** | −28 *(−28)* |

**Like-for-like, not one tally moved.** The five-person figures differ only because the newcomer took last places that used to be Lock-Step's, Oracle's and, above all, Intrepid's. On this measure Oracle is twenty clear.

#### **10.2.6 Polarity-Adjusted Position Composite**

*The same 94 non-neutral axes as 10.1.2, risk inverted. Five-person board: 5 \= best placement. Like-for-like: 4 \= best.*

| PC | Mean | Median | SD | Like-for-like mean *(S15)* |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Oracle** | **3.86** | 4.0 | 1.23 | **3.10** *(3.07)* |
| **Quasar-Force** | 3.48 | 4.0 | 1.37 | 2.82 *(2.84)* |
| **Lock-Step** | 2.64 | 3.0 | 1.43 | 2.14 *(2.13)* |
| **Edge** | 2.60 | 2.5 | **0.98** | — |
| **Intrepid** | 2.43 | 2.0 | 1.42 | 1.95 *(1.96)* |

**Oracle leads by 0.38 on the five-person board, and by 0.28 like-for-like, against 0.23 last revision.** On positions as on scores, the lead widened. **Third place on this measure is Lock-Step's, by four hundredths over Edge** — the opposite of the score-based composite, where Edge leads Lock-Step by half a point (10.2.7).

#### **10.2.7 Method Comparison**

Both composites run on the same axes. Matched:

|  | Score-based | Position-based | Agree? |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Excluding fame (94)** | **Oracle** \+3.888, Quasar-Force \+3.494 *(0.39)* | **Oracle** 3.86, Quasar-Force 3.48 *(0.38)* | **Yes** |
| **Including fame (101)** | **Quasar-Force** \+3.830, Oracle \+3.617 *(0.21)* | **Oracle** 3.80, Quasar-Force 3.58 *(0.22)* | **No — second consecutive revision** |
| **Third place (94)** | **Edge** \+0.566, Lock-Step \+0.068 *(0.50)* | **Lock-Step** 2.64, Edge 2.60 *(0.04)* | **No — first time** |
| **Raw first-place count (101)** | — | **Quasar-Force** 40, Oracle 37 | **No — third consecutive revision** |

**The flip held.** Last revision's closing note asked whether Quasar-Force's fame-inclusive lead would survive; it did, at 0.21, so the score-based and position-based fame-inclusive treatments disagree for a second revision, and the raw first-place count has now disagreed with the headline composite for three.

**The re-examination, updated.** Last revision split the composite into its native parts and found the two leaders tied to nine thousandths on everything they bring. Session 16:

| Component | Axes | Oracle | Quasar-Force | Leader |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Capability (magnitude, §3.6–§7) | 56 | \+3.54 | **\+4.35** | Quasar-Force by 0.81 *(0.87)* |
| Character, standing & reputation (valence) | 30 | **\+6.85** | \+5.25 | Oracle by 1.60 *(1.60)* |
| **All favourable axes (the two above)** | **86** | **\+4.692** | \+4.662 | **Oracle by 0.030** *(Quasar-Force by 0.009)* |
| Risk (§2) | 8 | **−4.75** | −9.06 | Oracle by 4.31 *(4.31)* |

**On what they bring, Oracle is back in front by three hundredths**, on a session in which he carried the fight and Quasar-Force spent half of it below zero. Quasar-Force's capability margin shrank by six hundredths; the character margin did not move. **On what threatens them, nothing changed at all:** 0.367 of Oracle's 0.394 composite lead is still the risk section, and 0.028 is everything else. Closing the lead through risk alone would now need Quasar-Force's risk mean to improve from −9.06 to about −4.4.

**The finding stands as relabelled last revision:** "Oracle first" on the headline composite means "Oracle safer" far more than "Oracle better." Session 16 adds a qualification in his favour — he is now slightly better as well — and it is thirty thousandths wide.

**Why third place disagrees.** Lock-Step has fifteen first places and twenty-eight last places; Edge has one and sixteen. **Lock-Step's negatives are deep** — Character −3.21, Reputation −1.95, Risk −8.25 — **and Edge's are shallow.** A score composite punishes depth; a position composite counts only order. Neither is wrong. **Edge is the better-rounded member, and Lock-Step the more decisive one.**

**Why fame stays excluded, now that it decides the answer.** Exposure has no fixed sign: it opens doors and it paints targets. **Session 16 supplies the clearest case yet.** The brood said, in the fight, that it ganged up on Quasar-Force first because of his fame, his known fragility and his grappling (§8.6, §2.2). The only treatment in this document that puts Quasar-Force first is still the one that scores a spotlight as an asset, and **Session 16 is the session in which the spotlight drew fire.**

### **10.3 The Sweeps**

*Where each character's first and last places actually cluster. Position convention follows each section: on §2 first place means **most** at risk.*

| PC | Firsts | Where they are | 5ths | Where they are |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Quasar-Force** | **40** | §5:11, §8:7, §2:6, §4:5, §7:4, §9:4, §1:2, §6:1 | 7 | §4:2, §6:2, §9:2, §3:1 |
| **Oracle** | 37 | §1:11, §4:7, §3:6, §9:6, §5:4, §7:2, §6:1 | 8 | §4:4, §2:2, §6:1, §8:1 |
| **Lock-Step** | 15 | §4:4, §6:3, §2:2, §3:2, §5:2, §7:1, §9:1 | 28 | §1:13, §9:7, §3:3, §4:2, §7:2, §6:1 |
| **Edge** | 1 | §4:1 | 16 | §5:5, §4:4, §8:4, §9:2, §3:1 |
| **Intrepid** | 8 | §4:5, §1:1, §3:1, §6:1 | **42** | §5:12, §4:10, §2:6, §7:5, §3:4, §6:2, §8:2, §1:1 |

**Oracle sweeps character.** He holds eleven of the fourteen psychology axes, six of nine team internals and six of eleven reputation axes: **23 of the 34 axes that measure who a person is rather than what they can do**, unchanged. His combat last places fell from six to four — Grappling, Personal Mobility, Single-Target and Combat Power — because the newcomer sits below him on Crowd Control and Area Damage. **He is still the best person on this team and the worst at hurting anybody.** He lost one first, Ranged, to the newcomer.

**Quasar-Force sweeps capability, exposure and most of the danger, and his lists did not change.** Forty firsts — eleven of the seventeen non-combat axes, all seven fame axes, five combat axes, four of seven overall axes and six of eight risk axes — and the same seven last places as last revision: Physical Durability, Healing, Power Independence, Solo Operation, Support Burden, and the two public reputations. **Five of those seven are still the same fact — a sixty-Health fourteen-year-old carrying an irreplaceable object with no way to heal — and the other two are the death.** Session 16 tested the first fact twice in one fight.

**Lock-Step is the mirror image, and Session 16 shortened only the image's edges.** His fifteen firsts are the same fifteen as last revision. His last places fell from thirty to twenty-eight, both because the newcomer arrived below him (Trust and Green Lantern Corps reputation), **and all thirteen psychology last places are unchanged**: Edge's §1 scores sit above his on every one of them. The only psychology axis he is not last on is still Moral Agency.

**Edge's distribution is the narrowest a debut can produce.** One first, Ranged. Sixteen lasts, of which **seven are tie-breaks** (Team Mobility and Area Damage under the newcomer convention; Engineering, Cyber, Lab, Law and Medical on Logic) and **four are fame axes**, where last means least exposed. **Only five are last places on substance**: Trust, Crowd Control, Rescue, and two reputations he has not had time to earn.

**Intrepid's distribution is still the most extreme on the board.** Eight firsts — five in combat, plus Civilian Stability, Support Burden and Power Independence — against forty-two last places, **down from fifty-four only because a fifth member arrived below him on twelve axes.** He is last on twelve of the seventeen non-combat axes. ⟨Note⟩ Six of the forty-two are risk axes and are good: he is the least subvertible, least targeted and least leveraged person on the roster.

### **10.4 Structural Findings**

*Team-level conclusions that fall out of the axis data rather than from any single character. Findings are carried forward with their revision history; thirteen moved this pass, four are carried, two are new, and two stay archived or superseded.*

**1\. The leadership vacuum became a division of labour.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Iron-Side still declines to appoint a leader. Nightwing was off screen for a third session but acted: operating as Batman, he recruited and briefed the newcomer. Wonder Woman remains the external authority in practice; the team briefed her after the fight. ⟨Note⟩ The user's reading that she is becoming the team's mentor is still not GM-declared. **Inside the team, the chair Lock-Step filled in Session 15 was set down in Session 16, in front of the newcomer**: *"Oracle is our plan guy. I'm just the if it all goes to hell… that's my element."* Oracle supplied the plan after saying there wasn't one, Edge supplied the target, the module ratified it, and in the crisis Lock-Step ran the exit with a consent question. **Planning to Oracle, the crisis to Lock-Step, intelligence to Quasar-Force and the first target to the newest member** — a structure nobody appointed and everybody followed. *(Standing since Session 3; externally re-anchored Session 14; internally filled Session 15; divided Session 16.)*

**2\. The tower's perimeter is half-closed, and the threat lives in its basement.** ⟨CARRIED⟩ The teleport half is done and was not tested. The anti-phasing half is not recorded as built; the fight that featured a phaser happened at a warehouse, and the hand-waved ending credits Lock-Step's Force whip, not the building, with deterring her. **One gap may be smaller than it looked:** the drones' infrared, blind to the Session 14 thieves, found the brood in Session 16\. The hostile progenitor is still in STAR Labs custody on the lower floors of the tower's own skyscraper, and Doctor Taleria's cut-off sentence about *"the other signal that's happening at your—"* is still unfollowed. *(Standing since Session 10; half-closed and relocated Session 15; carried Session 16.)*

**3\. Quasar-Force is still a single point of failure removable in one action; the edges held and the centre did not.** ⟨MOVED⟩ **The edge held:** the circlet's fortification turned away Swipe, the one attack in the fight built to take a power. **The centre did not:** a Marvel and a reaction strike put him at −5 through his damage reduction in the opening exchange, and a glitch bomb that ignores damage reduction put him at −30. **And the enemy now has a doctrine for him** — fame, known fragility, grappling — which it explained out loud. ⟨Ruling⟩ Telekinetic Protection is closed by the house rule while the suit carries Sturdy 3\. **Energy Absorption converts Health damage *after* damage reduction into Focus, which makes it the one purchase on the board that answers both of Session 16's knockdowns.** ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ The circlet is *Worn*, and it stayed on. *(Standing since Session 4; first mitigation Session 15; edges tested and held Session 16.)*

**4\. Threats inside the trust perimeter: one masked, one partly answered, one new unknown.** ⟨MOVED⟩

- **Left.** ⟨OOC⟩ Exemplar I is outside the perimeter with detailed knowledge of the team.
- **Masked.** Azimuth, for as long as the circlet stays on. It stayed on through a fight.
- **Resurfaced, and partly answered.** The warehouse where Lock-Step's time device was found and used as a lure was holding a second device keyed to his time manipulation, and the brood came for it. **Edge destroyed it.** Who built it, and for whom, is still unknown.
- **STAR Labs' CFO** and **the Destined**: no Session 16 development; the Destined's errand is unresolved (finding 18).
- **New, and not a threat: an unexamined legend.** The newcomer lives in bedroom 3 and carries a backstory nobody on the team has asked about — a monster reborn that *"cut down armies"* — and a mentor tag that may not mean what it says. ⟨Volatile⟩

*(Standing since Session 8; rewritten Session 15; partly answered Session 16.)*

**5\. Oracle has been counter-built, and the brood found a third way.** ⟨MOVED⟩ The Session 14 grab-and-drain was not used, so it is still untested whether his sheet answers it. **Session 16 added a third counter**: Dragonborne spent a reaction to turn Oracle's Return Fire onto Lock-Step. He was knocked down by 72 physical damage and got up on his own healing factor. **Two Rank 3.5 advances have now been undeclared for three sessions.** Any mental power that projects strength breaks the grab under the house rule; Uncanny raises the soft door. *(New Session 14; untested Session 15; third counter Session 16.)*

**6\. Cost runs opposite to capability, and the newcomer is cheap, not free.** ⟨MOVED⟩ **The cheapest member cost an extraction**: Intrepid fell from \+7.0 to \+6.5 when Lock-Step went back for him on the last of his Focus. Quasar-Force's bill rose (−2.0 → −2.5) on two knockdowns that the hardware did not prevent and the medic spent half the fight on. **Edge enters second at \+6.0** — self-healing, self-armed, trained at someone else's expense, but housed by the team and fifteen. *(Standing since Session 9; anchor removed Session 15; half-restored Session 16.)*

**7\. Robustness runs opposite to power, and the gap widened again.** ⟨MOVED⟩ The section spread on the composite scale went 1.17 → **1.27**, after compressing for the first time last revision. **The member at the bottom fell**: Intrepid lost a power to a thief and was beaten by something other than damage for a fourth straight session. Lock-Step, still at the top, gave back a little on one roll outside the warehouse. **The newcomer enters second, ahead of Oracle**, so the member with the second most robust profile is also the one with the least record. *(Standing since Session 11; first narrowing Session 15; widened Session 16.)*

**8\. One healer, tested at full load; the backup still untested.** ⟨MOVED⟩ **Session 16 is the session this finding was written for.** Every member went to zero or below, and every recovery in the fight went through Oracle: Let's Go on Quasar-Force, then on Lock-Step, and the heals that sent everyone home. **The worst case arrived for one round**: with Oracle down at −2, nobody healed anybody until his own healing factor got him up. Lock-Step's hex-heal was not used, because every point of his Focus went into teleports. **The finding is confirmed in its strongest form short of the failure itself**: the team has no recovery mechanism during a fight if Oracle is neutralised, and in Session 16 the brood neutralised him for a round with physical damage. Edge brings a second healing factor, not a second healer. *(Standing since Session 7; tested at full load Session 16.)*

**9\. Legal exposure is concentrated in two members, and the second is still accumulating.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Quasar-Force remains *the* case (−9.5). **Lock-Step moved to −8.5**: one of the five teenagers he publicly accused of reoffending in Session 15 is now unconscious, in his handcuffs, in a crime syndicate's warehouse. The conduct was defensible — surrender offered first, non-lethal force — and the exposure is the paperwork. If the Destined act on his Session 15 request, the solicitation sits beside it. *(Standing since Session 8; broadened Session 14; concentrated Session 15; accumulating Session 16.)*

**10\. The best pairings on this team: one declared by the GM, one formed in an afternoon.** ⟨MOVED⟩ The Oracle–Lock-Step resonance from Session 15 was not used. **Two new working pairings appeared in the newcomer's first fight.** Edge and Intrepid: two aids, one turning a Marvel miss into the hit that stunned Blood Blade and gave Intrepid his Sturdy back. Edge and Oracle: Edge's 31 Focus left Dragonborne at 11, and Oracle's 15 demoralised him. **And the newcomer's first shot was aided by all four teammates at once.** The telekinetic nudge remains undeclared doctrine, and the pre-mission questionnaire is still unwritten — but *"Do we have a plan?"* was asked before the door opened, by the member who had been on the team for minutes. *(Standing since Session 6; half-closed Session 15; extended Session 16.)*

**11\. "The powers were never the problem" — resolved in the affirmative.** ⟨CLOSED, ARCHIVED⟩ The finding was about Exemplar II's sheet; §11 carries the record. *(Opened Session 9; closed Session 14; archived Session 15.)*

**12\. Research institutions hold this team's biology, and one of them is a suspect.** ⟨CARRIED⟩ STAR Labs built Oracle's body, wants Quasar-Force in a laboratory, holds the Breeder in custody, and its CFO signed out the car that took five teenagers into its garage. Doctor Taleria's consensual research relationship with Quasar-Force and Iron-Side's unexplained medical training are unchanged. **The five teenagers carrying the Breeder's brood are now three unconscious in a warehouse and two at large**, and the log records no screening of any of them. *(Standing since Session 12; STAR Labs converted from custodian to suspect Session 15; carried Session 16.)*

**13\. The Justice League has gone from client to host to backstop to debrief.** ⟨MOVED⟩ The League took no part in Session 16\. The team went home and briefed Wonder Woman, and the log records neither who spoke nor what she said. ⟨Flag⟩ Her Session 14 disclosure commitment was made to Exemplar II, and whether it binds the current roster is still untested. **The newcomer gives the League-adjacent world a second line into this team**: Black Canary, who trains him. *(Standing since Session 12; converted Sessions 14 and 15; debrief Session 16.)*

**14\. Two Justice League civilian identities spoken aloud in a compromised building.** ⟨SUPERSEDED by 16⟩ No civilian identity was spoken in Session 16\. *(New Session 14; superseded Session 15.)*

**15\. The team has no answer to area denial or obscurement — and now, on paper, it has one.** ⟨ANSWERED ON PAPER⟩ **Edge carries Heightened Senses 2**, the power that let Exemplar II see through the Vapors of Galator and track an invisible assassin in Session 14\. Nothing tested it in Session 16\. **Area denial is still unanswered**: Intrepid's stomp is still the team's only area attack, and nothing on any sheet addresses an obscurement field except the newcomer's senses. The opponents who used those tools are still at large, bar Ecliptica. *(New Session 14; worse by departure Session 15; answered on paper Session 16.)*

**16\. The team is publishing itself — and an enemy has read it.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Nothing was published in Session 16\. **What changed is that the exposure was used.** The brood told the team in the fight that it ganged up on Quasar-Force first because of his fame, his known fragility and his preference for grappling, and Dragonborne arrived ready to answer Oracle's Return Fire. **For one revision this finding was about the team producing exposure; Session 16 is the first time an enemy is recorded spending it.** *(New Session 15; first priced Session 16.)*

**17\. A second cosmic institution has a claim on a Titan, and it works in time — and somebody on Earth has a use for the same powers.** ⟨CARRIED, with a new datum⟩ The ring and the oath did not come up. **But a Marrow Syndicate warehouse was trafficking a device keyed to Lock-Step's time manipulation**, the brood was sent to retrieve it, and Edge destroyed it. ⟨Ruling⟩ Time Powers are on the GM's banned list, and whatever the device did will arrive as plot. *(New Session 15; carried Session 16.)*

**18\. The team has contracted a villain team for lethal work, and it is still unresolved.** ⟨CARRIED⟩ The Destined have not reported back. The errand came up twice in passing — Quasar-Force's *"We sent the murder hobos after the bad progenitor"* and Lock-Step's *"The other situation, is that other team's problem"* — and **nobody objected, Oracle included**. His one line on the subject came out garbled (*"hopefully, die, harming them?, dissuade them?"*), and it was Lock-Step who insisted on knock-outs for the five. The Session 15 watch item on Oracle's silence is carried, unresolved. *(New Session 15; unresolved Session 16.)*

**19\. The roster is five again, and the newcomer filled the vacancy's edges, not its centre.** ⟨MOVED⟩ Of the six things §11.3 said the replacement would most usefully bring, Edge brought one on paper (an answer to obscurement), two in part (field investigation by senses rather than method; robustness, second on the index) and three not at all (a second healer, adult or professional standing, a second area attack or real crowd control). **He also brought things the vacancy did not describe**: the best ranged attack on the team, a second Suppressive Fire line at ×7, the team's only dodge reaction, the objective-denial shot, and the second-highest social ability score. §11.3 has the detail. *(New Session 15; partly filled Session 16.)*

**20\. The hive wants the team.** ⟨NEW⟩ Mid-fight, the controller's orders changed. Blitzkrieg: *"my master's orders has changed to collecting you since you proved yourselves worthy,"* and a round later, *"we need to collect these specimens."* The hand-waved ending says she could not carry out Intrepid or Edge, and that she withdrew under orders because Intrepid weighs over 300 pounds and Lock-Step's Force whip can hit her while she is phased. **A hostile force has now said, in the middle of a fight, that it wants the Titans themselves** — not their data, their devices or their deaths. The interest is not new: Doctor Varga wants Quasar-Force in a laboratory, and the Prodigy breeder once called this team *excellent progeny*. **What is new is that it arrived as orders, to a team already in the room with them.** Two members of the brood escaped, and both have now seen what each Titan does in a fight. *(New Session 16.)*

**21\. The first fight in which every member went down, against the people the team was there to bring back.** ⟨NEW⟩ All five Titans reached zero Health or below, the healer was down for a round, and the team left the building one teleport at a time on the last of Lock-Step's Focus. **The team won the objective and lost the field.** The device was destroyed, but the five were left as they were found: three unconscious in a mob warehouse, one in handcuffs, and two at large, none secured, screened or cured, with the 24-hour clock from Session 15 unresolved in the log. **The rule the team fought by — *"we can bring them back, so knock them out"* — was the right one**, and Session 16 is the first time it was tested on people the team could not afford to leave behind, and did. *(New Session 16.)*

##

## **11\. Departed — Exemplar II (Archive and Vacancy Analysis)**

*Exemplar II's player left the campaign between Sessions 14 and 15\. In the fiction he was left out of the Grey Lantern's transport; on Day 46 he "returns to earth, with only a quick goodbye to the team," and from Day 53 he "is not present" at the tower. The roster lists him under Departed PCs. **He is not ranked in this revision.** His replacement, Edge, arrived in Session 16 and is scored throughout. This section keeps Exemplar II's final standing as the document last measured it and, for the first time, measures the replacement against the vacancy.*

### **11.1 Final Standing (frozen at Session 14\)**

| Measure | Figure | Place (of five) |
| :---- | :---- | :---- |
| Composite (94 axes) | **\+1.49** | 3rd |
| Median / SD / range | \+2.5 / 4.04 / −8.0 → \+8.5 | — |
| Fame-inclusive composite / 0–10 rescale | \+1.64 / 5.82 | 3rd |
| Combat / Non-Combat / Robustness / All-Capability | 4.71 / 6.49 / **6.25** / 5.70 | 5th / 3rd / **1st** / 4th |
| Character / Team Standing / Reputation / Combined | \+3.89 / \+5.18 / \+3.23 / \+3.86 | 3rd / 2nd / 3rd / 3rd |
| Risk Load | −6.06 | 3rd most exposed |
| Raw position (101 axes) | 6 firsts, 15 lasts, average 3.21 | — |
| Polarity-adjusted position composite | 2.71 (median 3.0) | 3rd |

**His six first places:** Civilian Life Stability (`+7.0`), Low Operational Support Burden (`+8.5`), Field Investigation (`8.8`), Law (`7.0`), Function Without Powers (`8.0`), and Law-Enforcement Reputation (`+6.0`). Four of the six were versions of one fact: **he was the only adult with a job.**

**His final sheet.** He had 150 Health, 90 Focus, H-DR 2 and F-DR 2\. His abilities were Melee 7, Agility 0, Resilience 5, Vigilance 3, Ego −2 and Logic 6, and his defences 19/19/17/15/18/18. He carried two Iconic Items, both flawed *Custom Built Parts for the Exemplar II Project* and *Human (David's DNA) use only*:

- **AI Cybernetic Implants & Sensory Bionics:** AllSpeak, Heightened Senses 2, Microscopic Awareness, Uncanny 2\.
- **Torso Cybernetic Implantation:** Mighty 3, Speed Run 1, Venom Bash, Venom Burst.

The roster's Rank 3→4 plan ends at its fourth line: *"{Player left the game}."*

**His arc in this document.** He joined in Session 3 and went through four sheets in eight sessions. For three revisions this document described a generalist who bought capability and never used it. **His last session on the team was the best any character had had in the campaign**, and he played it on the sheet he already had:

- **Azimuth.** He saw the astral figure nobody else could, sketched it, and put a name on a New God missing for thousands of years.
- **Ecliptica.** He found the drone footage that identified her.
- **The casualty.** He moved the unconscious Quasar-Force out from under the figure.
- **Wonder Woman.** He told a founding member of the Justice League that she had failed a team of minors, and got *"Consider me admonished"* and a disclosure commitment in return.

Session 14 moved him on sixty-one axes, the record until Lock-Step's seventy-two in Session 15\. ⟨OOC⟩ The clone subplot — a father who intends to overwrite him — is unresolved. So is the one instrument that would have answered it for him: Microscopic Awareness, never once demonstrated on screen.

### **11.2 The Vacancy, Axis by Axis**

*What the team had in him at Session 14, who covered it after he left, and who covers it now that Edge has arrived.*

| Capability | Exemplar II (S14) | Best remaining (S15) | Covered now (S16) | What the arrival changed |
| :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- | :---- |
| **Field Investigation** §5.4 | **8.8** (1st) | Quasar-Force 7.5 | Quasar-Force 7.5 | Little. Edge (5.0) brings Heightened Senses 2's lie-detection and range, with Logic −1 and no record. |
| **Sensory Awareness** §5.9 / counter-stealth | 8.0 (2nd) | Oracle 9.0, Quasar-Force 7.0 | Oracle 9.0, **Edge 8.0** | **The senses are back.** Edge carries the same Heightened Senses 2, at the same score. Untested. |
| **Law** §5.6 | **7.0** (1st) | Quasar-Force 5.0 | Quasar-Force 5.0 | Nothing. Edge is fifteen (1.0). |
| **Alliance Capital** §5.15 | 9.0 (2nd) | Lock-Step 7.2 | Lock-Step 7.2 | A new line, not a replacement: Edge (6.0) brings Black Canary, the Birds of Prey and Nightwing's personal recruitment. |
| **Engineering / Lab / Cyber** §5.2–5.5 | 7.0 / 7.5 / 5.0 (2nd on all three) | Oracle 4.5 / 7.0 / 4.5 | Oracle 4.5 / 7.0 / 4.5 | Nothing. Edge has Logic −1 (0.5 on all three). |
| **Low Support Burden** §3.5 | **\+8.5** (1st) | Intrepid \+7.0 | Intrepid \+6.5, **Edge \+6.0** | A cheap member, not a net contributor: self-healing and self-armed, housed by the team. |
| **Civilian Stability** §1.7 | **\+7.0** (1st) | Intrepid \+6.0 | Intrepid \+6.0 | Nothing. Edge's civilian life (+1.5) carries the headaches, the grades and the legend. |
| **Function Without Powers** §6.2 | **8.0** (1st) | Lock-Step 7.5 | Lock-Step 7.5 | Some. Edge (4.5) has Agility 7 and Black Canary's training underneath the powers. |
| **Robustness index** §6 | **6.25** (1st) | Lock-Step 5.38 | Lock-Step 5.30, **Edge 5.12** | Partly. Edge enters second on the index. |
| **Area Damage** §4.11 | 5.0 (2nd) | Lock-Step 3.0 | Lock-Step 3.0 | Nothing. Edge is single-target only (0.5). Intrepid's stomp is still the only area attack. |
| **Physical Durability** §4.1 | 7.8 (2nd) | Oracle 6.5 | **Edge 6.2** | Partly. A second healing factor and a dodge, on 90 Health rather than 150\. |
| **Law-Enforcement Reputation** §9.3 | **\+6.0** (1st) | Oracle \+5.5 | Oracle \+5.5 | Nothing. Edge (+1.0) is unknown to the police. |

**What left that the team is better off without** is unchanged: his OPSEC record (§1.6 `−1.0` at Session 14), whose exposure did not leave with him, and his risk load, including a patron who was also his adversary.

###

### **11.3 What the Replacement Actually Brought**

*Last revision ranked six vacancies by how much each one cost the four who remained, and said the replacement would be scored on arrival against that list. Session 16 is the arrival.*

1. **An answer to obscurement and invisibility** (finding 15). **Yes, on paper.** Heightened Senses 2 is the power that saw through the Vapors of Galator and tracked an invisible assassin in Session 14\. Nothing in Session 16 tested it, and the opponents who exploit obscurement are still at large.
2. **A second healer** (finding 8). **No.** He brings a second healing factor, which heals only him. The single-healer finding was tested at full load in Session 16 and confirmed.
3. **Field investigation in person.** **Partly.** The instrument is excellent — quadruple range, a double edge to perceive, and a heartbeat lie-detector — and the operator has Logic −1 and no record. He did arrive already briefed by Nightwing.
4. **Adult or professional standing** — law, contracts and police relations. **No.** He is fifteen, with a School obligation. His mentor is an adult professional, which is a connection rather than standing.
5. **Robustness**: a member who functions when powers, preparation or support are removed. **Partly.** Second on the index (5.12), with innate powers, a constructible weapon, a dodge and a healing factor; without powers he is a fast, trained fifteen-year-old.
6. **A second area attack or real crowd control.** **No.** Every attack he has takes one target, and he is last on Crowd Control.

**What nobody asked for, and he brought anyway:**

- **The best ranged attack on the team** (§4.12, first on arrival), at ×7 \+7, with a ⟨Mechanical flag⟩ that it may be ×8.
- **A second Suppressive Fire line, at ×7** — Oracle's debuff at nearly twice the multiplier, which set up the demoralise that ended the fight.
- **The team's only dodge reaction**, which stripped a Marvel and made a glitch bomb miss.
- **Objective denial at range**: the shot that destroyed the device the brood came for.
- **Ego 4**, the second-highest social ability score on the team, and a Monster trait that intimidates.

**The verdict: one of six on paper, two in part, three not at all, and a strong specialist profile the vacancy did not describe.** The newcomer replaced Exemplar II's senses and none of his adulthood. He enters third on the composite (+0.57), second on combat value, and fourth on non-combat potency.

### **11.4 Loose Ends He Leaves Behind**

- **The Azimuth file.** He produced the only worked identification from Justice League material Wonder Woman handed him. The log does not record whether the team kept the file.
- **Wonder Woman's disclosure commitment** — *"every piece of available information I have, even if it is just suspicion"* — was made to him, in front of four witnesses. Finding 13 flags whether it still binds; the team briefed her after Session 16, and nothing in the log says what she shared back.
- **Exemplar I.** ⟨OOC⟩ He intends to overwrite his son, he knows this team, and in Session 10 he hacked a phone in seconds. He is outside the perimeter, and the roster's own backstory note anticipates him becoming *"an enemy of David and the group."*
- **Quasar-Force's lab access.** The roster still lists *"Exemplar's Dad's Lab"*, and it should be treated as stale.
- **Bedroom 3** is occupied again, by Edge.
- **⟨OOC⟩ Precedent.** This campaign has lost three player characters before him: Robin was kidnapped, tortured and placed at the centre of a sacrifice ritual; Magius was abducted, and the abduction turned out to be staged; Up-Tempo quit silently. The GM has turned departures into plot before, and the father is the obvious hook if the GM wants one. ⟨Volatile⟩

##

## **12\. Closing note on this revision.**

**Session 16 had one fight, one arrival, no sheet changes among the returning four, and the hardest afternoon in the team's record.** Every member went to zero Health or below. The brood explained, mid-fight, that it had gone for Quasar-Force first because of his fame, and then announced that its orders had changed to collecting the team. The team destroyed the device it came for, left three infected teenagers unconscious in a mob warehouse — one in handcuffs — and went home one teleport at a time.

**The standings barely moved, and they moved for a reason.** Fifty-nine axes changed among the returning four, a third of Session 15's 183\. Oracle's composite lead widened from 0.36 to 0.39, the first widening since 0.77 → 0.81, because **the session tested what Oracle does and not what Quasar-Force does**: it ran on healing, debuffs and a plan, and nothing in it needed an engineer. On the 86 favourable axes Oracle is back in front, by three hundredths; the rest of his lead is still the risk section. **Edge enters third, at \+0.57**, ahead of Lock-Step and Intrepid, on the flattest profile on the board, and the score-based and position-based methods disagree about whether he or Lock-Step is third.

**Last revision asked Session 16 to test eight things:**

1. **The Mob Warehouse.**
   * **The healing backup:** untested. Lock-Step's hex-heal was not needed, because the healer was never drained; it was the healer who carried the fight.
   * **The counter-build:** not used. The brood beat Oracle another way, with 72 physical damage and a reaction that turned his Return Fire onto a teammate. His healing factor got him up.
   * **The Force-infused whip against anything that phases:** never swung at a phaser in the log. The hand-waved ending credits it as one reason Blitzkrieg withdrew. ⟨Volatile⟩
   * **The circlet against a telepath:** Argent made no recorded attempt. The circlet held against a power thief instead — the same Swipe that took Intrepid's Sturdy.
   * **Five infected teenagers the team wants to cure rather than kill:** no fatalities. Three were knocked out and left behind, one cuffed; two left on their own feet. None was secured, screened or cured.
2. **The Destined at STAR Labs.** Unresolved; not in the log. The errand came up twice in passing and nobody objected.
3. **Oracle's two advances.** Still undeclared, for a third session.
4. **Oracle's voice.** Not resolved. There was no lethal-force discussion; two passing references drew no objection from him, his own line about the five came out garbled, and it was Lock-Step who insisted on knock-outs. Carried.
5. **The replacement.** Scored throughout, and against the vacancy in §11.3: one of six on paper, two in part, three not at all, plus a specialist profile nobody asked for.
6. **Nightwing.** Off screen for a third session, and active for the first time in three: he recruited and briefed the newcomer, operating as Batman. The team briefed Wonder Woman, not him, and the report containing Day 56 is still unsent.
7. **The ring.** Not mentioned. A crime syndicate's device keyed to Lock-Step's time manipulation was, and it no longer exists.
8. **The flip.** Held. The fame-inclusive score composite favours Quasar-Force (by 0.21) while the position composite favours Oracle, for a second revision; the raw first-place count has disagreed with the headline composite for three.

**What Session 17 should test:**

1. **The three in the warehouse, and the 24-hour clock.** What happened to Argent, Stote and Blood Blade after the team left — and to the cuffed one in particular — decides §2.5 and §9.3 for Lock-Step, and finding 21 for everyone.
2. **The hive's collection order.** Whether *"collecting you"* becomes an operation against the team, and whom it goes for first. On Session 16's evidence, the answer is the member with the most fame and the least Health.
3. **The Destined errand.** Whether Lock-Step's private request was carried out is still the largest single swing available to him in §1, §2 and §9, and a real one for Quasar-Force. When it reports back, it is also the moment that settles **Oracle's voice**.
4. **Oracle's advances.** Undeclared for three sessions. If either bought a mental power that projects strength, or Uncanny, then §4.2, §4.5, §6.4, §7.3 and finding 5 all move.
5. **Edge's senses against obscurement.** The next time the team meets invisibility or a fog, finding 15 moves from paper to record, one way or the other.
6. **Edge's legend, and the mentor tag.** What the monster is, whether it can be woken, and which Oracle found him — the scores in §2.1, §2.8, §6.5 and §7.3 that carry ⟨Volatile⟩ markers all wait on the first answer.
7. **Quasar-Force's floor.** Energy Absorption at Rank 4 is the one purchase that answers both of Session 16's knockdowns. Until it lands, the next glitch bomb is the same test.
8. **The healing backup.** Lock-Step's hex-heal has been used once, in Session 14\. If it recurs, finding 8 narrows; if Oracle is neutralised for longer than a round with nothing to replace him, it becomes the defining fact of the fight.
9. **Nightwing's first report.** It now has to cover Day 56, the warehouse, and the recruit he briefed without telling the team.
10. **The flip and the third place.** If the fame-inclusive lead holds again, the two methods disagree for a third revision. If Edge's score-based third survives a session with deep negatives in it, the methods will agree on him; if it does not, the position composite was right.
